40.75461pt\raise-32.27974pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.
Proof.
Using the presentation
Xi⊞r=(⨆αi∈𝔸i∂αXi×[−r,0]𝔽αi)/∼ for i=1,2,X_{i}^{\boxplus r}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\alpha_{i}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{i}}\partial^{\alpha}X_{i}\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha_{i}}}\right)/\sim\text{ for }i=1,2,
we define the restriction of f⊞rf^{{\boxplus r}} over ∂α1X1×[−r,0]𝔽α1→∂ι⁡(α1)X2×[−r,0]𝔽ι⁡(α1)\partial^{\alpha_{1}}X_{1}\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha_{1}}}\to\partial^{\iota(\alpha_{1})}X_{2}\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\iota(\alpha_{1})}} to be f×idf\times\text{id}. It is easy to see that equivalent points are mapped to equivalent points, therefore we can define f⊞rf^{{\boxplus r}} to be the induced map.
∎
The outer collar construction is compatible with group actions. Let GG be a topological group acting on XX via stratum-preserving homeomorphisms. For each r>0r>0, define the GG-action on X⊞rX^{\boxplus r} as follows. For each g∈Gg\in G and (x,(ti)i∈𝔽α)∈∂αX×[−r,0]𝔽α(x,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}, define
g⋅(x,(ti)i∈𝔽α)=(g​x,(ti)i∈𝔽α)∈∂αX×[−r,0]𝔽α⊂X⊞r.g\cdot(x,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})=(gx,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\subset X^{\boxplus r}.
It is easy to check that the action is well-defined, continuous, and preserves strata.
3.3.2. Outer-collaring of flow categories and bimodules
The outer-collaring construction described above can be naturally carried over to a system of stratified spaces included in a flow category or bimodule. Let 𝒫{\mathcal{P}} satisfy Setup 3.5 and let T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} be a topological flow category over 𝒫{\mathcal{P}} (the Π\Pi-action in this discussion is trivial hence we omit the reference to it). Fix r≥0r\geq 0. All outer-collaring in this discussion will be of a fixed width rr and will be labelled by ++ instead of ⊞r{\boxplus r}. Then we can apply the outer-collaring construction to each individual space Tp​qT_{pq} for all p<qp<q, obtaining 𝔸p​q𝒫{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}-spaces Tp​q+T_{pq}^{+}. Define “composition maps”
Tp​r+×Tr​q+≅∂p​r​qTp​q+↪Tp​q+T_{pr}^{+}\times T_{rq}^{+}\cong\partial^{prq}T_{pq}^{+}\hookrightarrow T_{pq}^{+}
as the composition
Tp​r+×Tr​q+ (Tp​r×Tr​q)+ (∂p​r​qTp​q)+ ∂p​r​q(Tp​q+) .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.68022pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.68022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T_{pr}^{+}\times T_{rq}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.68022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.68022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(T_{pr}\times T_{rq})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 133.45738pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 133.45738pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\partial^{prq}T_{pq})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 211.4214pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 211.4214pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{prq}(T_{pq}^{+})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.
Here the first map comes from item (2) of Lemma 3.12, the second map is the canonical outer-collaring of the original composition map Tp​r×Tr​q→∂p​r​qTp​qT_{pr}\times T_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}T_{pq}, and the third map comes from item (1) of Lemma 3.12. It is easy to check that the composition maps are still associative. Hence we obtained a new topological flow category over 𝒫{\mathcal{P}}, which we call the outer-collaring of T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}}, denoted by
(T𝒫)+(T^{\mathcal{P}})^{+}
whose morphisms spaces are Tp​q+T_{pq}^{+}.
Similar happens when we apply outer-collaring to flow bimodules. Let T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} and T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} be flow categories over 𝒫{\mathcal{P}} and 𝒫′{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime} respectively and let M𝒫​𝒫′M_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} be a flow bimodule from T𝒫T^{{\mathcal{P}}} to T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}. By doing outer-collaring to each individual space Mp​p′M_{pp^{\prime}} for p∈𝒫p\in{\mathcal{P}} and p′∈𝒫′p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}, obtaining new spaces Mp​p′+M_{pp^{\prime}}^{+}, one obtains a flow bimodule M𝒫​𝒫′+M_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}^{+} from T𝒫+T^{{\mathcal{P}}+} to T𝒫′+T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}+}. This new flow bimodule is called the outer-collaring of M𝒫​𝒫′M_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}.
3.4. Derived orbifold charts with stratifications
As mentioned before, the morphism spaces of flow categories and flow bimodules are a priori only topological spaces. We would like to define the notion of derived orbifold lift for (topological) flow categories and flow bimodules.
3.4.1. Stratified topological manifolds corners
Before discussing smooth structures, we introduce a refined structure on stratified spaces in a special setting. It is well-known that “manifolds with corners” are identical to manifolds with boundary in the topological category. One way to define topological 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifolds is given by [Abo22, Definition 2.4]. We specialize loc. cit. which suffices for our purpose.
First we introduce some notations. The standard model for a manifold with corners is the space [0,+∞)k[0,+\infty)^{k} for k≥0k\geq 0. It is stratified by subspaces where a subset of coordinates are equal to 00.
Notation 3.14.
For any finite set 𝔽{\mathbb{F}}, let 𝔸(𝔽){\mathbb{A}}^{({\mathbb{F}})} be the set of all subsets of 𝔽{\mathbb{F}} where the partial order is induced by inclusion:
α≤β⟺β⊆α.\alpha\leq\beta\Longleftrightarrow\beta\subseteq\alpha.
𝔸(𝔽){\mathbb{A}}^{({\mathbb{F}})} is homogeneous with a unique maximal element ∅\emptyset and its depth function being
depth⁡(α)=#​α.{\rm depth}(\alpha)=\#\alpha.
We abbeviate 𝔸(k):=𝔸({1,…,k}){\mathbb{A}}^{(k)}:={\mathbb{A}}^{(\{1,\ldots,k\})}. Then [0,+∞)k[0,+\infty)^{k} is an 𝔸(k){\mathbb{A}}^{(k)}-space.
Definition 3.15.
Let 𝔸{\mathbb{A}} be a (countable) homogeneous poset. An 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space is called a (topological) 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold if the following is true.
(1)
For any α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}, the space ∂αX\partial^{\alpha}X is a topological manifold (possibly with boundary) whose interior is XαX_{\alpha}.
(2)
Each x∈Xα⊂Xx\in X_{\alpha}\subset X has a corner chart, i.e., a stratified open embedding
Ux,α×[0,1)depth⁡(α)\textstyle{U_{x,\alpha}\times[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}φ\scriptstyle{\varphi}X\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}{α}×𝔸(depth⁡(α))\textstyle{\{\alpha\}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{({\rm depth}(\alpha))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝔸\textstyle{\mathbb{A}}
where Ux,αU_{x,\alpha} is an open subset of xx in XαX_{\alpha} (with the trivial stratification indexed by the singleton {α}\{\alpha\}).
(3)
For each corner chart φ:Ux×[0,1)depth⁡(α)→X\varphi:U_{x}\times[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}\to X near x∈Xαx\in X_{\alpha}, one has
α≤β⟹Im​φ∩Xβ≠∅.\alpha\leq\beta\Longrightarrow{\rm Im}\varphi\cap X_{\beta}\neq\emptyset.
In other words, corner charts touch all strata above α\alpha.
Remark 3.16.
Conditions of Definition 3.15 impose more restrictions on the homogeneous poset 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}. For example, for each α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}, the set {β∈𝔸|α≤β}\{\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}\ |\ \alpha\leq\beta\} is isomorphic to 𝔸(depth⁡(α)){\mathbb{A}}^{({\rm depth}(\alpha))} and hence contains a unique maximal element. Moreover, each α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}} has a definite set of elements α≤β\alpha\leq\beta with depth⁡(β)=1{\rm depth}(\beta)=1 whose cardinality is equal to its depth. The posets 𝔸p​q𝒫{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔸p​p′{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}} appeared in the disucssions of flow categories and flow bimodules all satisfy these additional restrictions.
Definition 3.17.
Let GG be a topological group acting on an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold XX viewing XX as an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space. We say that XX is an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-stratified GG-manifold with corners if each x∈Xαx\in X_{\alpha} has a GG-equivariant corner chart, i.e., a corner chart Ux,α×[0,1)depth⁡(α)→XU_{x,\alpha}\times[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}\to X which is a GG-equivariant map. Here Ux,α⊂UαU_{x,\alpha}\subset U_{\alpha} is a GG-invariant open subset and GG acts trivially on the factor [0,1)depth⁡(α)[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}.
3.4.2. Stratified smooth manifolds with corners
Next we recall some notions related to smooth manifolds with corners. We use the following notion of smoothness for functions defined on arbitrary subsets of Euclidean spaces. Let A⊆ℝnA\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{n} be a subset. A function f:A→ℝf:A\to{\mathbb{R}} is called smooth if for each a∈Aa\in A there is an open neighborhood Ua⊂ℝnU_{a}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n} such that f|A∩Uaf|_{A\cap U_{a}} can be extended to a smooth function fa:Ua→ℝf_{a}:U_{a}\to{\mathbb{R}}. Then one can define the notion of smooth maps f:A→Bf:A\to B for subsets A⊆ℝnA\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{n} and B⊆ℝmB\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{m}. Then compositions of smooth maps are still smooth. A smooth map f:A→Bf:A\to B is a diffeomorphism if it admits a smooth inverse.
The concept of smooth manifolds with corners is defined as follows. Let MM be a topological space. A chart (of an nn-dimensional smooth manifold with corners) consists of an open subset U⊂MU\subset M and a homeomorphism φ:U→[0,+∞)n\varphi:U\to[0,+\infty)^{n} onto an open subset. Two charts φi:Ui→[0,+∞)n\varphi_{i}:U_{i}\to[0,+\infty)^{n}, i=1,2i=1,2, are compatible if φ1∘φ2−1:φ2​(U1∩U2)→φ1​(U1∩U2)\varphi_{1}\circ\varphi_{2}^{-1}:\varphi_{2}(U_{1}\cap U_{2})\to\varphi_{1}(U_{1}\cap U_{2}) is a diffeomorphism. A structure of smooth nn-dimensional manifold with corners is a maximal compatible atlas on MM. If MM is equipped with such a structure, we say that MM is a smooth nn-dimensional manifold with corners. Over smooth manifolds with corners, one can define the notions of smooth vector bundles, smooth connections, and smooth Riemannian metrics, etc.
The concept of manifold with faces is one which can exclude certain pathological scenarios. Let MM be a smooth nn-dimensional manifold with corners. For each point x∈Mx\in M and each chart φ:U→[0,+∞)n\varphi:U\to[0,+\infty)^{n} defined over an open neighborhood of xx, the number depth⁡(x){\rm depth}(x) of coordinates of φ⁡(x)\varphi(x) which are zero is independent of the choice of charts. Given k≤nk\leq n, the subset
∂kM:={x∈M|depth⁡(x)=k}⊂M\partial^{k}M:=\{x\in M\ |\ {\rm depth}(x)=k\}\subset M
is naturally an n−kn-k-dimensional smooth manifold. A connected face of MM is a connected component of the set {x∈M|depth⁡(x)=1}\{x\in M\ |\ {\rm depth}(x)=1\}. MM is called a smooth manifold with faces if every x∈Mx\in M belongs to the closure of exactly depth⁡(x){\rm depth}(x) different connected faces.
As we are treating various manifolds with faces which have more refined structure of stratifications, we would like to introduce the following concepts.
Definition 3.18.
Let 𝔸{\mathbb{A}} be a countable homogeneous poset.
(1)
A smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold is a smooth manifold with faces MM which is also a topological 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold such that for each α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}, MαM_{\alpha} is an open and closed subset of ∂depth⁡(α)M\partial^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}M
and such that each x∈Mx\in M has a smooth corner chart.
(2)
Let GG be a compact Lie group. A smooth GG-action on a smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold MM is a GG-action on the underlying topological 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold of MM which acts on MM by smooth maps.
Note that any smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold MM has a natural structure of a topological 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold by forgetting the smooth structure.
Definition 3.19.
A smooth map ff from a smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold MM to a smooth manifold without boundary NN is called stratified submersive if for any (open) stratum MαM_{\alpha}, the restriction
f|Mα:Mα→Nf|_{M_{\alpha}}:M_{\alpha}\rightarrow N
is a smooth submersion.
Lemma 3.20.
Suppose f:M→Nf:M\to N is stratified submersive. Then for any y∈Ny\in N, the space f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) is a smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold. Moreover, if GG is a compact Lie group acting on MM and ff is GG-invariant, then f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) has a smooth GG-action.
Proof.
By Definition 3.19, the subsets f−1​(y)∩Mαf^{-1}(y)\cap M_{\alpha} are smooth manifolds. Using the collars near each stratum, we see that f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) is a smooth manifold with corners. We now verify that f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) is a manifold with faces, namely, each x∈f−1​(y)x\in f^{-1}(y) is contained in exactly depth⁡(x){\rm depth}(x) connected faces. Indeed, viewing xx as a point in MM, by definition, xx is contained in exactly depth⁡(x){\rm depth}(x) connected faces of MM. The implicit function theorem implies that xx is contained locally in exactly depth⁡(x){\rm depth}(x) faces and these faces are still disconnected globally in f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y). Hence f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) is a smooth manifold with faces.
Next, we see that the decomposition
f−1​(y)=⨆α∈𝔸f−1​(y)∩Mαf^{-1}(y)=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}f^{-1}(y)\cap M_{\alpha}
induces an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space structure on f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y). Conditions of Definition 3.15 can then be verified using the implicit function theorem. Transversality implies that f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) has smooth corner charts. Hence f−1​(y)f^{-1}(y) is a smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold. The equivariant case follows immediately from the definition.
∎
3.4.3. Stratified smooth orbifolds with corners and derived orbifold charts
Smooth orbifolds with corners can be defined as in Section 2.1, with the modification that for an nn-dimensional orbifold with corners 𝒰{\mathcal{U}}, each local chart C=(U,Γ,ψ)C=(U,{\mathit{\Gamma}},\psi) has the following property: consider ℝn−k×ℝ≥0k{\mathbb{R}}^{n-k}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{k} where Γ{\mathit{\Gamma}} acts linearly on the first factor and trivially on the second factor. Then U⊂ℝn−k×ℝ≥0kU\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n-k}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{k} is a Γ{\mathit{\Gamma}}-invariant open subset and ψ:U/Γ→𝒰\psi:U/{\mathit{\Gamma}}\to{\mathcal{U}} is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of 𝒰{\mathcal{U}}. Just as the manifold case, one can define the notion of smooth orbifold with faces and the notion of smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-orbifolds for any homogeneous poset 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}. We omit the discussions of bundles, sections, and embeddings in the stratified sense. Note that if α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}} and 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} is an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-orbifold, the ∂α𝔸\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}-space ∂α𝒰\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}} is naturally a ∂α𝔸\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}-orbifold.
Now we can generalize the definition of derived orbifold charts (see Definition 2.1) to the case when the domains have corners or faces.
Definition 3.21 (Stratified derived orbifold charts).
(1)
An 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-stratified derived orbifold chart is a triple (𝒰,ℰ,𝒮)({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}), where 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} is a smooth 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-orbifold, ℰ→𝒰{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}} is an orbifold vector bundle, and 𝒮:𝒰→ℰ{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}} is a continuous section. We say that (𝒰,ℰ,𝒮)({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}) is compact if 𝒮−1​(0){\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0) is compact.
(2)
Given 𝔸i{\mathbb{A}}_{i}-stratified derived orbifold charts Ci=(𝒰i,ℰi,𝒮i)C_{i}=({\mathcal{U}}_{i},{\mathcal{E}}_{i},{\mathcal{S}}_{i}), i=1,2i=1,2, a chart embedding 𝜾21:C1↪C2{\bm{\iota}}_{21}:C_{1}\hookrightarrow C_{2} consists of a smooth stratified embedding
𝒰1\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}ι21\scriptstyle{\iota_{21}}𝒰2\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝔸1\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝔸2\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{2}}
and a bundle embedding ι^21:ℰ1→ℰ2\widehat{\iota}_{21}:{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{2} covering ι21\iota_{21} which intertwines with the sections 𝒮1{\mathcal{S}}_{1} and 𝒮2{\mathcal{S}}_{2}.
(3)
Given an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space XX, a derived orbifold presentation (or called a D-chart presentation) of XX is a quadruple (𝒰,ℰ,𝒮,ψ)({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\psi) consisting of an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-stratified derived orbifold chart (𝒰,ℰ,𝒮)({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}) with a homeomorphism of 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-spaces ψ:𝒮−1​(0)→X\psi:{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)\to X.
The following set of notations are frequently used. If (𝒰,ℰ,𝒮,ψ)({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\psi) is a derived orbifold presentation of the 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space XX, for any stratum α\alpha, the datum (∂α𝒰,ℰ|∂α𝒰,𝒮|∂α𝒰,ψ|𝒮−1​(0)∩∂α𝒰)(\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}},\psi|_{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)\cap\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}}) is a derived orbifold presentation of ∂αX\partial^{\alpha}X. In practice, we might have a derived orbifold presentation of the boundary stratum ∂αX\partial^{\alpha}X different from the restriction of a derived orbifold presentation of XX. To carry out our construction of perturbations, we would like to relate the possibly different derived orbifold presentations of the boundary strata. This motivates the following definition, which should be thought of as a special case of the Kuranishi diagram from [Abo22, Definition 6.6]. Some notions appear in the following were defined in Definition 2.3.
Definition 3.22.
A system of D-chart presentation of an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space XX consists of the following objects.
(1)
A collection of derived orbifold presentations
{Cα=(𝒰α,ℰα,𝒮α,ψα)}α∈𝔸\big\{C_{\alpha}=({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha},\psi_{\alpha})\big\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}
of the collection of boundary strata {∂αX}α∈𝔸\{\partial^{\alpha}X\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}} as stratified spaces.
(2)
A collection of chart embeddings
{𝜾β​α:Cα→∂αCβ}α≤β.\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}.
These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.
(A)
The collection of chart embeddings satisfy the cocycle condition. More precisely, for any triple of strata α≤β≤γ\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma, there holds
ιγ​β∘ιβ​α=ιγ​α.\iota_{\gamma\beta}\circ\iota_{\beta\alpha}=\iota_{\gamma\alpha}.
(B)
Adjacent strata differ by a stabilization. More precisely, for any pair of strata α≤β\alpha\leq\beta, there exist an orbifold vector bundle ℱβ​α→𝒰α{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha} and a germ equivalence
Stabℱβ​α​(Cα)≃∂αCβ{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\simeq\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}
In certain special cases the chart embeddings between different strata are open. For example in the case of Morse flow category and the pearly bimodule. We introduce the following notion.
Definition 3.23.
A system of derived orbifold presentations of an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space XX is called single-layered if all chart embeddings 𝜾β​α:Cα→∂αCβ{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta} are open embeddings, or equivalently, one can take ℱβ​α=0{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}=0 for all α≤β\alpha\leq\beta.
3.4.4. Derived orbifold lifts of flow categories and bimodules
Now we can go back to the setup in Section 3.2. The following definition imposes certain regular structures on morphism spaces of flow categories and flow bimodules.
Definition 3.24.
Let T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} be a flow category over the poset 𝒫{\mathcal{P}}. A derived orbifold lift of T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}}, denoted by 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}, consists of the following objects.
(1)
A collection
{Cp​q=(𝒰p​q,ℰp​q,𝒮p​q,ψp​q)}p≤q\big\{C_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq})\big\}_{p\leq q}
of derived orbifold presentations of the 𝔸p​q𝒫{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}-space Tp​qT_{pq} such that for each connected component 𝒰p​q,j⊂𝒰p​q{\mathcal{U}}_{pq,j}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}, one has
dimℝ​𝒰p​q,j−rankℝ​ℰp​q|𝒰p​q,j≡ind𝒫​(p)−ind𝒫​(q)−1​mod​ 2​N.{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq,j}-{\rm rank}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{pq,j}}\equiv{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)-{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(q)-1\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.
(3.5)
(2)
A collection of chart embeddings
{𝜾p​r​q:Cp​r×Cr​q↪∂p​r​qCp​q}p≤r≤q\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:C_{pr}\times C_{rq}\hookrightarrow\partial^{prq}C_{pq}\big\}_{p\leq r\leq q}
(with the underlying poset identification 𝔸p​r𝒫×𝔸r​q𝒫≅∂p​r​q𝔸p​q𝒫{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{rq}\cong\partial^{prq}{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}). In particular, if ιp​r​q:𝒰p​r×𝒰r​q↪𝒰p​q\iota_{prq}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}_{pq} is the associated domain embedding and ι^p​r​q:ℰp​r⊞ℰr​q↪ℰp​q\widehat{\iota}_{prq}:{\mathcal{E}}_{pr}\boxplus{\mathcal{E}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{pq} is the associated bundle embedding, then the following diagram commutes.
ℰp​r⊞ℰr​q\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{pr}\boxplus{\mathcal{E}}_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}ι^p​r​q\scriptstyle{\widehat{\iota}_{prq}}ℰp​q\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝒰p​r×𝒰r​q\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}ιp​r​q\scriptstyle{\iota_{prq}}𝒮p​r×𝒮r​q\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{rq}}𝒰p​q\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝒮p​q\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}}
(3.6)
These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.
(A)
For p=qp=q, the space 𝒰p​p{\mathcal{U}}_{pp} is a singleton with trivial isotropy and ℰp​p={0}{\mathcal{E}}_{pp}=\{0\} is the trivial bundle.
(B)
The chart embeddings satisfy the associativity. More precisely, whenever p<r<s<qp<r<s<q, the following diagram commutes.
Cp​r×Cr​s×Cr​q\textstyle{C_{pr}\times C_{rs}\times C_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}id×𝜾r​s​q\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}_{rsq}}𝜾p​r​s×id\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}_{prs}\times{\rm id}}∂p​r​sCp​s×Cr​q\textstyle{\partial^{prs}C_{ps}\times C_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​s​q\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}_{psq}}Cp​r×∂r​s​qCr​q\textstyle{C_{pr}\times\partial^{rsq}C_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​r​q\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}}∂p​r​s​qCp​q\textstyle{\partial^{prsq}C_{pq}}
(C)
For each p​qpq and all α=pr1⋯rlq∈𝔸p​q𝒫\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}, define
Cα:=Cp​r1×⋯×Crl​q.C_{\alpha}:=C_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{r_{l}q}.
Then condition (B) implies that for each pair of elements α≤β\alpha\leq\beta in 𝔸p​q𝒫{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}, there is a well-defined chart embedding
𝜾β​α:Cα↪∂αCβ.{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}.
Then we require that the collection {Cα}α∈𝔸p​q𝒫\{C_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}} of derived orbifold presentations and the collection {𝜾β​α}α≤β\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta} of chart embeddings constitute a system derived orbifold presentations of Tp​qT_{pq}.
(D)
The strict Π\Pi-equivariance condition: for any a∈Πa\in\Pi and p<qp<q, there is an isomorphism between derived orbifold charts (in the obvious sense) ϕ~a:Cp​q→Ca⋅p​a⋅q\tilde{\phi}_{a}:C_{pq}\to C_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q} satisfying ϕ~a1⋅a2=ϕ~a1∘ϕ~a2\tilde{\phi}_{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}=\tilde{\phi}_{a_{1}}\circ\tilde{\phi}_{a_{2}}, and ϕ~a\tilde{\phi}_{a} restricts to ϕa\phi_{a} along the zero locus 𝒮p​q−1​(0){\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{-1}(0) to the map ϕa\phi_{a} from Definition 3.7. Furthermore, ϕ~0=Id\tilde{\phi}_{0}={\rm Id} for a=0∈Πa=0\in\Pi should be the identity map.
Now consider derived orbifold lifts of bimodules.
Definition 3.25.
Let MM be a flow bimodule from a flow category T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} to T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} as in Definition 3.10. Suppose T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} resp. T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} is endowed with a derived orbifold lift
𝔇𝒫=({Cp​q𝒫=(𝒰p​q𝒫,ℰp​q𝒫,𝒮p​q𝒫,ψp​q𝒫)}p<q,{𝜾β​α𝒫}α≤β) resp. {\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}=\Big(\big\{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq},\psi^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq})\big\}_{p<q},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\mathcal{P}}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big)\text{ resp. }
𝔇𝒫′=({Cp′​q′𝒫′=(𝒰p′​q′𝒫′,ℰp′​q′𝒫′,𝒮p′​q′𝒫′,ψp′​q′𝒫′)}p′<q′,{𝜾β′​α′𝒫′}α′≤β′).{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}=\Big(\big\{C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}=({\mathcal{U}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}},{\mathcal{E}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}},\psi^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}})\big\}_{p^{\prime}<q^{\prime}},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\big\}_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta^{\prime}}\Big).
A derived orbifold lift of MM compatible with (or extending) 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} consists of the following objects.
(1)
A collection
{Cp​p′M=(𝒰p​p′M,ℰp​p′M,𝒮p​p′M,ψp​p′M)}p∈𝒫,p′∈𝒫′\big\{C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}=({\mathcal{U}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{E}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},\psi^{M}_{pp^{\prime}})\big\}_{p\in{\mathcal{P}},p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
of derived orbifold presentations of Mp​p′M_{pp^{\prime}} for Mp​p′M_{pp^{\prime}} as an 𝔸p​p′{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}-space such that for each connected component 𝒰p​p′,jM⊂𝒰p​p′M{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime},j}^{M}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}, one has
dimℝ​𝒰p​p′,jM−rankℝ​ℰp​p′M|𝒰p​p′,jM≡ind𝒫​(p)−ind𝒫′​(p′)​mod​ 2​N.{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime},j}^{M}-{\rm rank}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{E}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime},j}^{M}}\equiv{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)-{\rm ind}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.
(3.7)
(2)
Given p∈𝒫p\in{\mathcal{P}} and p′∈𝒫′p^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} for Mp​p′≠∅M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset, for any p≤qp\leq q, a chart embedding
𝜾p​q​p′M:Cp​q𝒫×Cq​p′M↪∂p​q​p′Cp​p′M,{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pqp^{\prime}}:C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\times C^{M}_{qp^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\partial^{pqp^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},
and for any q′≤p′q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}, a chart embedding
𝜾p​q′​p′M:Cp​q′M×Cq′​p′𝒫′↪∂p​q′​p′Cp​p′M.{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}:C^{M}_{pq^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\partial^{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.
The precise meaning can be spelled out as in Equation (3.6).
These objects are required to satisfy the following conditions.
(A)
For p=qp=q, the chart embedding 𝜾p​q​p′M{\bm{\iota}}_{pqp^{\prime}}^{M} is the identity map after identifying Cp​q𝒫C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq} with the trivial chart for the singleton. Similarly, for q′=p′q^{\prime}=p^{\prime}, the chart embedding 𝜾p​q′​p′M{\bm{\iota}}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{M} is also the identity map.
(B)
The chart embeddings satisfy the associativity. Namely, given Mp​p′≠∅M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset, the following three diagrams commute if the relevant topological spaces are nonempty.
For p<q1<q2p<q_{1}<q_{2}, we have
Cp​q1𝒫×Cq1​q2𝒫×Cq2​p′M\textstyle{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times C^{\mathcal{P}}_{q_{1}q_{2}}\times C^{M}_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}id×𝜾q1​q2​p′M\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{q_{1}q_{2}p^{\prime}}}𝜾p​q1​q2𝒫×id\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}q_{2}}\times{\rm id}}∂p​q1​q2Cp​q2𝒫×Cq2​p′M\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{1}q_{2}}C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{2}}\times C^{M}_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​q2​p′M\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{2}p^{\prime}}}Cp​q1𝒫×∂q1​q2​p′Cq1​p′M\textstyle{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times\partial^{q_{1}q_{2}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​q1​p′M\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{1}p^{\prime}}}∂p​q1​q2​p′Cp​p′M.\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{1}q_{2}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.}
For q2′<q1′<p′q_{2}^{\prime}<q_{1}^{\prime}<p^{\prime}, we have
Cp​q2′M×Cq2′​q1′𝒫′×Cq1′​p′𝒫′\textstyle{C^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}id×𝜾q1′​q2′​p′𝒫′\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}q_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}𝜾p​q2′​q1′M×id\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}\times{\rm id}}∂p​q2′​q1′Cp​q1′M×Cq1′​p′𝒫′\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pq_{1}^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​q1′​p′M\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}Cp​q2′M×∂q2′​q1′​p′Cq2′​p′𝒫′\textstyle{C^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}}\times\partial^{q_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​q2′​p′M\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}∂p​q2′​q1′​p′Cp​p′M.\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.}
Finally, for p<qp<q and q′<p′q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}, we have
Cp​q𝒫×Cq​q′M×Cq′​p′𝒫′\textstyle{C^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{pq}\times C^{M}_{qq^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}id×𝜾q​q′​p′M\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{qq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}𝜾p​q​q′M×id\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pqq^{\prime}}\times{\rm id}}∂p​q​q′Cp​q′M×Cq′​p′𝒫′\textstyle{\partial^{pqq^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pq^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​q′​p′M\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}Cp​q𝒫×∂q​q′​p′Cq​p′M\textstyle{C^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{pq}\times\partial^{qq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{qp^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}𝜾p​q​p′M\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pqp^{\prime}}}∂p​q​q′​p′Cp​p′M.\textstyle{\partial^{pqq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.}
(C)
For each p,p′p,p^{\prime} and all α=pr1⋯rlrl′⋯r1′p′\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}r_{l}^{\prime}\cdots r_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}, define
CαM=C𝒫p​r1×⋯×CMrl​rl′×⋯×C𝒫′r1′​p′.C_{\alpha}^{M}=C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C^{M}_{r_{l}r_{l}^{\prime}}\times\cdots\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{r_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}.
As in Definition 3.24, for each pair α≤β\alpha\leq\beta in 𝔸p​p′{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}, there is a well-defined chart embedding
𝜾β​αM:CαM↪CβM.{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{M}:C_{\alpha}^{M}\hookrightarrow C_{\beta}^{M}.
It is required that the derived orbifold presentations {CαM}α∈𝔸p​p′\{C_{\alpha}^{M}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}} and the collection of chart embeddings {𝜾β​α}α≤β\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta} constitute a system of derived orbifold presentations of Mp​p′M_{pp^{\prime}}.
(D)
The strict Π\Pi-equivariance condition: for any a∈Πa\in\Pi and Mp​p′≠∅M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset, there is an isomorphism between derived orbifold charts ϕ~aM:Cp​p′M→Ca⋅p​a⋅p′M\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a}:C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}\to C^{M}_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot p^{\prime}} satisfying ϕ~a1⋅a2M=ϕ~a1M∘ϕ~a2M\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}=\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a_{1}}\circ\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a_{2}}, and ϕ~aM\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a} restricts to the map ϕaM\phi^{M}_{a} from Definition 3.10 along the zero locus (𝒮p​p′M)−1​(0)({\mathcal{S}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}})^{-1}(0). Moreover, ϕ~i​dM\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{id} for a=i​d∈Πa=id\in\Pi is the identity map. Moreover, the left and right actions of the charts from T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} and T𝒫′T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}} on the charts of MM should be Π\Pi-equivariant.
3.5. Additional structures
In order to construct a coherent system of FOP perturbations, we need more structures on the derived orbifold lifts. They are the collar structure, scaffolding, and straightening.
3.5.1. Collar structure

Here 0γ​β=(0,…,0)∈[0,ϵ)𝔽β∖𝔽γ0_{\gamma\beta}=(0,\ldots,0)\in[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}} and hence (tβ​α,0γ​β)(t_{\beta\alpha},0_{\gamma\beta}) is regarded as a point of [0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽γ[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}}.
(3)
A collar structure on a derived orbifold lift of a flow category (using the same notations as in Definition 3.24), is a collection of collar structures on the chart Cp​qC_{pq} such that the following is satisfied.
(a)
For each α=pr1⋯rlq∈𝔸p​q𝒫\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}, the collar structures on Cp​r1,…,Crl​qC_{pr_{1}},\ldots,C_{r_{l}q} define a product collar structure on the product chart CαC_{\alpha}. Then the collection of collar structures on CαC_{\alpha} is a collar structure on the derived orbifold presentation of Tp​qT_{pq} as defined in above item (2).
(b)
The collar structure is strictly Π\Pi-equivariant.
(4)
Suppose 𝔇𝒫​𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} is a derived orbifold lift of a flow bimodule MM from T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} to T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} which extends 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}. Suppose 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} are equipped with collar structures. Then a collar structure on 𝔇𝒫​𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} which extends the collar structure on 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} is a collection of collar structures on all the derived orbifold charts (𝒰p​p′M,ℰp​p′M,𝒮p​p′M,ψp​p′M)({\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M},{\mathcal{E}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M},{\mathcal{S}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M},\psi_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}) of the form

which satisfy similar compatibility conditions as the case of flow categories using the factorizations of boundary strata. We omit the details.
Remark 3.27.
Although the description of collar structures seems to be very complicated, they are constructed using the outer-collaring method which implies the required conditions almost automatically.
3.5.2. Scaffolding
Definition 3.28.
A scaffolding of a system of D-chart presentations ((Cα)α∈𝔸,(𝜾β​α)α≤β)((C_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}},({\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha})_{\alpha\leq\beta}) of an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space XX is a collection of data
(ℱβ​α,𝜽β​α)α≤β\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}
where for each pair α≤β\alpha\leq\beta
(1)
the difference bundle ℱβ​α→𝒰α{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha} is an orbifold vector bundle ℱβ​α→𝒰α{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}. In notation, when 𝔸=𝔸p​q𝒫{\mathbb{A}}={\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}} resp. 𝔸p​p′{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}} and β\beta is the maximal element p​qpq resp. p​p′pp^{\prime}, denote ℱβ​α{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha} by ℱp​q,α{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha} resp. ℱp​p′,α{\mathcal{F}}_{pp^{\prime},\alpha}.
(2)
the stabilization map 𝜽β​α{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha} is a germ equivalence
𝜽β​α=(θβ​α,θ^β​α):Stabℱβ​α​(Cα)≃∂αCβ{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}=(\theta_{\beta\alpha},\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}):{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\simeq\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}
(3.8)
which extends the chart embedding 𝜾β​α:Cα↪∂αCβ{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}. This germ equivalence induces a projection map
πβ​α:∂αCβ→Cα\pi_{\beta\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}\to C_{\alpha}
as well as a bundle splitting
ℰβ|𝒰α=ℰα⊕ℱβ​α{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}={\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
(3.9)
where ℰβ|𝒰α=𝜾β​α∗​ℰβ{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}={\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta} and a bundle isomorphism
ϑβ​α:πβ​α∗​(ℰβ|𝒰α)≅ℰβ|∂α𝒰β.\vartheta_{\beta\alpha}:\pi_{\beta\alpha}^{*}({\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}})\cong{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}.
(3.10)
These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.
(A)
For any triple of stratum α≤β≤γ\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma, as subbundles of ℰγ|𝒰α{\mathcal{E}}_{\gamma}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}} there holds
ℱγ​α=ℱβ​α⊕ℱγ​β|𝒰α.{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\alpha}={\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}.
(3.11)

107.06024pt\raise-48.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{\gamma\alpha}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.06024pt\raise-56.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-14.7854pt\raise-64.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}C_{\gamma}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 71.29526pt\raise-64.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 34.04199pt\raise-70.4472pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma\beta}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.7854pt\raise-64.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.
(3.13)
Here the “==” arrow is induced from the identity ℱγ​α=ℱγ​β|𝒰α⊕ℱβ​α{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\alpha}={\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}.
Now consider a derived orbifold lift of a flow category.
Definition 3.29.
A scaffolding of a derived orbifold lift of a flow category T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} consists of a collection of scaffoldings for the induced system of derived orbifold chart presentations of Tp​qT_{pq}
((ℱβ​α,𝜽β​α)α≤β)p<q\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big)_{p<q}
satisfying
(1)
Suppose pr1⋯rlq=α≤β=ps1⋯smqpr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q=\alpha\leq\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q. Denote α0=pr1⋯s1\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}, …\ldots, αm=sm⋯rlq\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q. Then as subbundles of ℰβ|𝒰α{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}, in view of the identification 𝒰α=𝒰α0×⋯×𝒰αm{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{0}}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{m}}, one has
ℱβ​α=ℱp​s1,α0⊞⋯⊞ℱsm​q,αm.{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}={\mathcal{F}}_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus{\mathcal{F}}_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.
(2)
With respect to the last identity, one has (as germs of maps)
𝜽β​α=𝜽p​s1,α0×⋯×𝜽sm​q,αm.{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}={\bm{\theta}}_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\times\cdots\times{\bm{\theta}}_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.
Definition 3.30.
Given two flow categories T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} and T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} endowed with derived orbifold lifts 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}, 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} respectively, let MM be a flow bimodule from T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} to T𝒫′T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} endowed with a compatible derived orbifold lift 𝔇M{\mathfrak{D}}^{M}. Suppose 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}} come with scaffoldings
({ℱβ​α𝒫,𝜽β​α𝒫}α≤β)p<q, ({ℱβ′​α′𝒫′,𝜽β′​α′𝒫′}α′≤β′)p′<q′.\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta\alpha}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\big)_{p<q},\text{ }\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}},{\bm{\theta}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\big\}_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta^{\prime}}\big)_{p^{\prime}<q^{\prime}}.
A scaffolding of such a derived orbifold lift compatible with the given scaffoldings is given by a collection of scaffoldings for the induced system of derived orbifold chart presentations
({ℱβ~​α~,𝜽β~​α~}α~≤β~)α~,β~∈𝔸p​p′M\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}},{\bm{\theta}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}}\big\}_{\tilde{\alpha}\leq\tilde{\beta}}\big)_{\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}}
satisfying similar conditions as in Definition 3.29 using the factorization of the boundary strata (see Definition 3.25 (C)).
3.5.3. Straightening
As we will perform FOP perturbations over stratified charts, we need to consider straightenings in the stratified situation.
Definition 3.31 (Straightenings).
(1)
Let (𝒰,ℰ,𝒮)({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}) be an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-stratified derived orbifold chart. A straightening consists of a Riemannian metric on 𝒰{\mathcal{U}} and a connection on ℰ{\mathcal{E}}
such that for each α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}, the restriction of the metric and the connection onto 𝒰α{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha} (which is itself a smooth orbifold) is a straightening of (𝒰α,ℰ|𝒰α,𝒮|𝒰α)({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}) (see Definition 2.1).
(2)
Consider a system of derived orbifold presentations on an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-stratified space XX. A straightening consists of a collection of straightenings of CαC_{\alpha} for all α∈𝔸\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}} such that for each pair α≤β\alpha\leq\beta (with associated chart embedding 𝜾β​α=(ιβ​α,ι^β​α):Cα↪∂αCβ{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}=(\iota_{\beta\alpha},\widehat{\iota}_{\beta\alpha}):C_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}), the domain embedding ιβ​α\iota_{\beta\alpha} is isometric and the bundle embedding preserves the connection, i.e., the image of the bundle embedding is preserved by the connection ∇ℰβ\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}} and the restriction of ∇ℰα\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}} to the image of the bundle embedding coincides with ∇ℰα\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}}.
(3)
Consider a derived orbifold lift 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} of a topological flow category T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} over 𝒫{\mathcal{P}}. A straightening on 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} consists of a straightening on all charts Cp​qC_{pq} satisfying the following condition. Fix p<qp<q. For each α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q, there is a product straightening on the associated chart Cα=Cp​r1×⋯×Crl​qC_{\alpha}=C_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{r_{l}q}. Then we require that the collection of these straightenings for all α∈𝔸p​q𝒫\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}} is a straightening on the system of derived orbifold presentations of Tp​q𝒫T_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}.
(4)
It is straightforward to define the notion of straightenings on a derived orbifold lift of a flow bimodule MM compatible with existing straightenings on the lifts of the two flow categories T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} and T𝒫′T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}} with derived orbifold lifts 𝔇𝒫{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}} and 𝔇𝒫′{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}. Namely, it consists of a straightening on all charts Cp​p′MC^{M}_{pp^{\prime}} such that: for α=pq1⋯qkq′k′⋯q′1p′∈𝔸p​p′\alpha=pq_{1}\cdots q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdots q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}} with Mα≠∅M_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset, there is a product straightening on the product chart C𝒫p​q1×⋯×CMqk​qk′′×⋯×C𝒫′q1′​p′C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times\cdots\times C^{M}_{q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}\times\cdots\times C^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}}; we require that the collection of these straightenings for all α∈𝔸p​p′\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}} is a straightening on the system of derived orbifold presentations of Mp​p′M_{pp^{\prime}}.
3.5.4. Compatibility
We need the three kinds of additional structures (collar structure, scaffolding, and straightening) to be compatible in a certain sense. These compatibility conditions are necessary for the inductive construction of FOP perturbations. For example, once a perturbation is chosen on all boundary strata, it can be canonically extended to a neighborhood of the boundary using the collar structure; meanwhile, there is another automatic extension using the scaffolding. Hence a compatibility requirement between these two structures is necessary. We also need the automatic extensions remain in the class of FOP perturbations, which further requires that the straightening is compatible with the collar structure and the scaffolding.
Remark 3.32.
In practice the three kinds of structures are constructed in different ways. The collar structure is constructed by the outer-collaring construction, which is very easy and straightforward. Then (see Subsection 5.8) we construct scaffolding in the topological category. The scaffolding will play a role in the smoothing process. After smoothing, the topological charts become smooth. Then finally we construct the compatible straightenings.
Remark 3.33.
In the following, we just define the notions of compatibility of a derived orbifold presentation. The relevant compatibility requirements for flow categories and flow bimodules are then reduced to the corresponding requirements for the induced derived orbifold presentations of all relevant topological spaces.
Definition 3.34.
Consider a system of derived orbifold presentations {Cα}α∈𝔸\{C_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}} of an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-stratified space XX. Suppose it is equipped with a collar structure and a scaffolding. We say that they are compatible if the following conditions are satisfied.
(1)
Given a triple α<β<γ\alpha<\beta<\gamma, we require that the following diagram commutes.
