Notation 5.53.
The Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariantization of the embedding ϑα\vartheta_{\alpha} is denoted by
ϑα∼:Gp​q×GαVα→∂αVp​q\vartheta_{\alpha}^{\sim}:G_{pq}\times_{G_{\alpha}}V_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
and its image is denoted by
Vα∼⊂∂αVp​q.V_{\alpha}^{\sim}\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}.
Points in Vα∼V_{\alpha}^{\sim} are those maps whose underlying map into ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d} are in δ⁡(α)\delta(\alpha)-normal position and whose obstruction vector are coming from factors of α\alpha.
5.4.4. More on obstruction bundles
Here we single out a particular pattern of the obstruction bundles which will be used several times in the rest of the paper. We fix p,q∈𝒫Floerp,q\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer} and α=pr1⋯rlq∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}. For each d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}, one has part of the obstruction bundles
Op​r1(d)→Vp​r1,…,Orl​q(d)→Vrl​q.O_{pr_{1}}^{(d)}\to V_{pr_{1}},\ldots,O_{r_{l}q}^{(d)}\to V_{r_{l}q}.
Via the above product construction, denote their images by
Op​r1→p​q(d),…,Orl​q→p​q(d)→Vα=Vp​r1×⋯×Vrl​q.O_{pr_{1}\to pq}^{(d)},\ldots,O_{r_{l}q\to pq}^{(d)}\to V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q}.
They are embedded into the bundle Op​q(d)|VαO_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}. Denote their (direct) sum by
Oα(d)→Vα.O_{\alpha}^{(d)}\to V_{\alpha}.
Notice that inside Op​q(d)|VαO_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}} there are also subbundles which are embedded images of Oβ(d)|VαO_{\beta}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}} for all intermediate strata β\beta.
In many inductive constructions, we wouldl like to choose structures (such as inner products, connections, maps, etc.) on Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)} inductively. When we make this choice over a particular stratum Vα⊂Vp​qV_{\alpha}\subset V_{pq}, we would like to extend the existing structures on those subbundles coming from product construction. These subbundles have overlaps and the existing structures agree on overlaps by the correct inductive hypothesis. However, this does not guarantee the existence of an extension; indeed some extra properties of these subbundles Oβ(d)O_{\beta}^{(d)} is needed.
Lemma 5.54.
Fix α=pr1⋯rlq∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}. Consider the two subbundles
Op​rl→p​q(d)→Vp​rl​q⊂Op​q(d)|Vp​rl​q,Or1​q→p​q(d)→Vp​r1​q⊂Op​q(d)|Vp​r1​q.O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pr_{l}q}\subset O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{pr_{l}q}},O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pr_{1}q}\subset O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{pr_{1}q}}.
(1)
One has
Op​rl→p​q(d)∩Or1​q→p​q(d)=Or1​rl→p​q(d).O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}\cap O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}=O_{r_{1}r_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}.
(5.43)
(2)
For each β∈𝔸p​qFloer\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer} with α≤β\alpha\leq\beta, there one has
Oβ(d)|Vα⊂Op​rl→p​q(d)|Vα+Or1​q→p​q(d)|Vα.O_{\beta}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}\subset O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}+O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}.
(5.44)
Proof.
We first consider the single-layered case. Choose a point x∈Vp​r1​rl​qx\in V_{pr_{1}r_{l}q} with underlying curve Σ\Sigma. Recall that vectors in Op​qO_{pq} are η∈H0​(Y)⊗H0​(L)0¯\eta\in H^{0}(Y)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(L)_{0}} where Y→ΣY\to\Sigma is a Hermitian vector bundle and L→ΣL\to\Sigma is a holomorphic line bundle. Let Σr1​rl⊂Σ\Sigma_{r_{1}r_{l}}\subset\Sigma be the union of components “between” r1r_{1} and rlr_{l}. Then a vector in the intersection (5.43) is η∈H0​(Y)⊗H0​(L)r1​rl¯\eta\in H^{0}(Y)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(L)_{r_{1}r_{l}}} where H0​(L)r1​rl⊂H0​(L)0H^{0}(L)_{r_{1}r_{l}}\subset H^{0}(L)_{0} is the subspace of holomorphic sections supported in Σr1​rl\Sigma_{r_{1}r_{l}} which vanish at the corresponding two nodes. On the other hand, recall Y=Hom¯​(ιF∗​T​ℂ​ℙd,u∗​TM)⊗ιF∗​𝒪​(k)Y=\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k). As the underlying curve inside ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d} is in normal position, there is a corresponding fan Wx,p​r1,Wx,r1​rl,Wx,rl​qW_{x,pr_{1}},W_{x,r_{1}r_{l}},W_{x,r_{l}q} in ℂd+1\mathbb{C}^{d+1} in the normal position. The condition η∈Op​rl→p​q\eta\in O_{pr_{l}\to pq} implies that η\eta vanishes on the normal direction of Wx,p​r1+Wx,r1​rlW_{x,pr_{1}}+W_{x,r_{1}r_{l}}; the condition η∈Or1→p​q\eta\in O_{r_{1}\to pq} implies that η\eta vanishes on the normal direction of Wx,r1​rl+Wx,rl​qW_{x,r_{1}r_{l}}+W_{x,r_{l}q}. Therefore, η\eta vanshes on the normal direction of Wx,r1​rlW_{x,r_{1}r_{l}}. By the way we define the inclusion Or1​rl→p​q↪Op​qO_{r_{1}r_{l}\to pq}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}, we see that η∈Or1​rl→p​q\eta\in O_{r_{1}r_{l}\to pq}. This proves (5.43) in the single layered case. The multi-layered case, more generally, can be proved by induction on the number of layers.
For (5.44), suppose β=p​s1,…,sm​q\beta=ps_{1},\ldots,s_{m}q. Then s1≥r1s_{1}\geq r_{1} and sm≤rls_{m}\leq r_{l}. Hence each summand of Oβ(d)O_{\beta}^{(d)} i.e., one of Op​s1→p​q(d)O_{ps_{1}\to pq}^{(d)}, …\ldots, Osm​q→p​q(d)O_{s_{m}q\to pq}^{(d)}, is either contained in Op​rl→p​q(d)O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)} or in Or1​q→p​q(d)O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}.
∎
5.5. Stabilization maps
Now we state our proposition regarding the stabilization maps.
Proposition 5.55.
Fix p<qp<q in 𝒫Floer{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}. There exist the following objects.
(1)
For each α∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}, a GαG_{\alpha}-equivariant subbundle Fp​q,α⊂Ep​q|VαF_{pq,\alpha}\subset E_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}} which is complementary to EαE_{\alpha} and carries a stratification by GαG_{\alpha}-invariant subbundles
Fβ​α​∀β∈𝔸p​qFloer,α≤βF_{\beta\alpha}\ \forall\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer},\alpha\leq\beta
such that
Eα⊕Fβ​α=Eβ|Vα.E_{\alpha}\oplus F_{\beta\alpha}=E_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}.
(2)
A germ of GαG_{\alpha}-equivariant embedding
θp​q,α:StabFp​q,α​(Vα)→∂αVp​q\theta_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
satisfying the following conditions.
(a)
Its Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariantization is a germ of open embedding.
(b)
Its restriction to VαV_{\alpha} (the zero section) coincides with the embedding ιp​q,α:Vα→∂αVp​q\iota_{pq,\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}.
(c)
For each stratum β\beta between α\alpha and p​qpq, one has
θp​q,α​(StabFβ​α​(Vα))⊂∂αVβ.\theta_{pq,\alpha}\left({\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right)\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}.
(5.45)
In fact we would like the maps constructed to have better properties. The discussion here is to showcase the analytical part of the construction. The above proposition is enough to establish the construction at this moment, i.e., Theorem 5.62.
5.5.1. The difference bundle
We first construct the bundle appeared in the stabilization map. Recall that Ep​q→Vp​qE_{pq}\to V_{pq} is naturally decomposed into Op​qO_{pq} and Qp​qQ_{pq} where the latter is a trivial bundle. The embedding ιp​q,α:Vα→∂αVp​q\iota_{pq,\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq} is covered by a bundle embedding Oα→Op​q|∂αVp​qO_{\alpha}\to O_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}. We can take a Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant inner product on Op​qO_{pq} and consider the orthogonal complement Op​q,α:=(Oα)⊥O_{pq,\alpha}:=(O_{\alpha})^{\bot}. On the other hand, we have already chosen a good complement of Qα⊂Qp​qQ_{\alpha}\subset Q_{pq}, denoted by Qp​q,αQ_{pq,\alpha} (which is trivial). Define
Fp​q,α:=Op​q,α⊕Qp​q,α.F_{pq,\alpha}:=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}.
5.5.2. Analytical setup
As expected, the stabilization map is constructed using the infinite-dimensional implicit function theorem in a global fashion. We need a global analytical setup for this operation.
Fix p​qpq and abbreviate d=dp​qd=d_{pq}. For each point x¯∈Bd\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d}, its fiber in the universal curve is a well-defined prestable cylinder Σx¯\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}} over which there is a holomorphic map μ¯:Σx¯→ℂ​ℙd\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}. Then there is a fiber bundle over Σx¯×M\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\times M denoted by
Ex¯(d)→Σx¯×M.E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}\to\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\times M.
More explicitly, in the single-layered case,
Ex¯(d)=Hom¯​(μ¯∗​T​ℂ​ℙd,TM)⊗μ¯∗​𝒪​(k)⊗H0​(μ¯∗​𝒪​(k))0¯.E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}=\overline{\rm Hom}(\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},TM)\otimes\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}}.
(5.46)
If we fix the point x¯\underline{\smash{x}}, then the fiber πp​q−1​(x¯)\pi_{pq}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}}) is a subset of the space of sections (u,η):Σ→Ex¯(d)(u,\eta):\Sigma\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)} where u:Σ→Mu:\Sigma\to M is a map and η\eta is a section of Ex¯(d)|graph⁡(u)E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}|_{{\rm graph}(u)}.
Now fix a positive integer ll. Let 𝒞l​(x):=Cl​(Σ,Ex¯(d)){\mathcal{C}}^{l}(x):=C^{l}(\Sigma,E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}) be the space of ClC^{l}-sections (u,η):Σ→Ex¯(d)(u,\eta):\Sigma\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}. Here we impose the asymptotic constraints at nodes and marked points to periodic orbits. Take the union over BdB_{d}, we obtained a well-defined topological space, denoted by 𝒞p​ql{\mathcal{C}}^{l}_{pq}. It is also stratified by 𝔸p​qFloer{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}. Now notice that one has a Gp​qG_{pq}-action on 𝒞p​ql{\mathcal{C}}^{l}_{pq} and an invariant subspace
Vp​q=Vp​q(d)⊂𝒞p​ql.V_{pq}=V_{pq}^{(d)}\subset{\mathcal{C}}^{l}_{pq}.
In fact, the complexification Gp​qℂG_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}} acts on 𝒞lp​q{\mathcal{C}}_{l}^{pq} by transforming the underlying points x¯\underline{\smash{x}} and the maps (u,η)(u,\eta), although Vp​qV_{pq} is not Gp​qℂG_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}-invariant.
Now we consider the difference between VαV_{\alpha} and ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}. We regard both as subspaces of the infinite-dimensional object, i.e.
Vα⊂∂αVp​q⊂∂α𝒞p​ql.V_{\alpha}\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\subset\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.
We define an approximate solution map
θp​q,αapp:StabFp​q,α​(Vα)→∂α𝒞p​ql.\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm app}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.
(5.47)
by
[Σ,u,μ,ηα,ηp​q,α,hp​q,α]↦(Id+ρhp​q,α)​[Σ,u,μ,ηα+ηp​q,α].[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{pq,\alpha},h_{pq,\alpha}]\mapsto({\rm Id}+\rho_{h_{pq,\alpha}})[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta_{\alpha}+\eta_{pq,\alpha}].
Here ρhp​q,α\rho_{h_{pq,\alpha}} is the upper-triangular matrix defined in the proof of Proposition 5.24. The matrix Id+ρhp​q,α{\rm Id}+\rho_{h_{pq,\alpha}} acts on the framing map μ¯:Σ→ℂ​Pd\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}P}^{d} and the obstruction sections. This is a GαG_{\alpha}-equivariant map and satisfies
θp​q,αapp​(StabFβ​α​(Vα))⊂∂β𝒞p​ql.\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm app}\left({\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right)\subset\partial^{\beta}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.
However, it is not necessarily an exact solution, because the extra ηp​q,α\eta_{pq,\alpha} can perturb the equation and the equation is not invariant under the complexification of Gp​qG_{pq}. We need to use right inverses to correct the solution.
Remark 5.56.
When we construct stabilization maps inductively, the approximate solution map may not be just the linear addition in the fiber direction. In fact we may also need to deform the map uu.
We first discuss the notion of linearizations of the defining equation of the thickened moduli space more carefully. Fix a real number a>2a>2. Fix an element x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq}.
Definition 5.57.
Given x¯∈Bd\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d}, one has the Banach manifold ℬp​q,x¯{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}} of W1,aW^{1,a}-sections (u,η):Σx¯→Ex¯(d)(u,\eta):\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)} which are asymptotic to prescribed periodic orbits and a Banach bundle ℰp​q,x¯→ℬp​q,x¯{\mathcal{E}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}} of corresponding inhomogeneous terms of class LaL^{a}. Here the Banach norms are defined with respect to the cylindrical metric on cylindrical components and arbitrary smooth metric on spherical components.
For each point x∈πp​q−1​(x¯)x\in\pi_{pq}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}}), the linearization of the defining equation at xx is a Fredholm operator
Dp​q,x:Tx​ℬp​q,x¯→ℰp​q,x¯|x.D_{pq,x}:T_{x}{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}|_{x}.
(5.48)
By the regularity statement Proposition 5.49, the linearizations are always surjective in our setting. Then we can talk about bounded right inverses. A right inverse of Dp​q,xD_{pq,x} is denoted by Tp​q,xT_{pq,x}. When p​qpq is understood, we use DxD_{x} and TxT_{x} instead.
To obtain global stabilization maps using the implicit function theorem, we need to choose a family of right inverses to the linearization. Obviously, we need a continuity condition. We define the notion of continuity by comparing with the local families obtained from the standard gluing procedure. Let us briefly recall the gluing construction in the context of pseudoholomorphic curves which should be most familiar to the reader. Given a nodal JJ-holomorphic curve (Σ,u)(\Sigma,u) with a stable domain Σ\Sigma therefore finite automorphism group, there exists a local universal unfolding of the domain Σ\Sigma. There is also a way to identify any compact subset of the nodal Σ\Sigma away from the nodes with a compact subset of nearby fibers. Choose a right inverse TT at the point represented by (Σ,u)(\Sigma,u). Then using such identifications and certain cut-and-paste procedure, one can obtain a family of approximate right inverses for nearby solutions. For details, the reader could consult [Par16, Equation (C.7.7)] for instance. These approximate right inverses determine exact right inverses which have the same images, as one could read off from [Par16, Equation (C.7.30)]. If ζ\zeta is a parameter in a neighborhood of the point represented by (Σ,u)(\Sigma,u), then we denote by TζglueT_{\zeta}^{\rm glue}. The construction further depends on some auxiliary data including Riemannian metrics which define the parallel transport to compare the deformation space between the central curve and nearby curves, and a cut-off function which is needed for the construction of the pre-gluing map [Par16, Equation (C.7.9)]. These data are referred to as a gluing profile.
Similar construction applies to our current situation for the thickened moduli spaces Vp​qV_{pq}. Indeed, as the spaces BdB_{d} are locally isomorphic to the moduli space ℳ¯0,d′+2ℝ\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,d^{\prime}+2}^{\mathbb{R}} for some d′>0d^{\prime}>0,the space of stable marked cylinders we can compare nearby fibers of the fiberwise Banach manifolds and identify compact subsets away from nodes with subsets of nearby fibers. The corresponding gluing construction can be performed in a similar way. The reader could consult Proposition 6.22 for more arguments along this line.
Definition 5.58 (Continuous family of right inverses).
A family of right inverses {Tx}x∈Vp​q\{T_{x}\}_{x\in V_{pq}} is said to be continuous at xx if the following is true. On one hand, for each x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq} and any representative x~=(Σ,u,μ,η)\tilde{x}=(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta), the restriction of TxT_{x} to each irreducible component of the domain defines a right inverse for the restriction of the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator. Then given a gluing profile for x~\tilde{x}, these right inverses induce a right inverse TζglueT_{\zeta}^{\rm glue} for any ζ\zeta near x~\tilde{x}. On the other hand, TxT_{x} induces a family of right inverses TζT_{\zeta} over this open neighborhood. Then for each compact set Z⊂Σ∖{nodes}Z\subset\Sigma\setminus\{{\rm nodes}\} there holds
limζ→0‖Tζglue−Tζ‖Z=0.\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\|T_{\zeta}^{\rm glue}-T_{\zeta}\|_{Z}=0.
Here we use the operator norm on the domain ZZ.
Remark 5.59.
We left to the reader to check that the above notion of continuity is intrinsic, i.e., independent of the choice of the gluing profile. In fact one only needs to check that the family of right inverse obtained from one gluing profile is continuous with respect to another gluing profile.
Lemma 5.60.
For each pair p<qp<q in 𝒫Floer{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}, there exists a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant continuous family of right inverses TxT_{x} for x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq}.
Proof.
It suffices to construct a continuous family of right inverses without the equivariance condition as we can average over the symmetry group Gp​qG_{pq}. Moreover, as any convex linear combination of two right inverses is still a right inverse, we only needs to construct continuous families locally and patch together using a continuous partition of unity. From the definition we know the local family of right inverses given by a gluing construction is locally continuous.
∎
Let us fix a choice of a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant continuous family of right inverses TxT_{x} for x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq}. Now using the product construction, for α=pr1⋯rlq∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}, one obtains a fiberwise Banach manifold ℬα,x¯{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}} for each x¯∈Bδ⁡(α)\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{\delta(\alpha)} and corresponding linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator Dα,xD_{\alpha,x} at each x∈πα−1​(x¯)x\in\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}}). A chosen family of right inverses Tp​r1,…,Trl​qT_{pr_{1}},\ldots,T_{r_{l}q} provides a family of right inverse Tα,xT_{\alpha,x} for all x∈Vαx\in V_{\alpha}. Now as we have another layer of thickening, and we would like to solve the corresponding equation for this “thicker” problem, we need to stabilize the right inverse.
First, notice that we have the natural inclusions
ℰα,x¯ ℰp​q,x¯ ℬα,x¯ ℬp​q,x¯ .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.02301pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.88412pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.16191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.16191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.46599pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.02301pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.02301pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.
The differences are given by deformations/obstructions in the difference between the two bundles over MM, Eα,x¯E_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}} and Ep​q,x¯E_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}. By looking at the thickened equation (5.32), we can see that the linearization at x∈Vα⊂∂αVp​qx\in V_{\alpha}\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq} is block upper-triangular
Dp​q,x=[Dα,x∗0Dp​q,α,x]D_{pq,x}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}D_{\alpha,x}&*\\
0&D_{pq,\alpha,x}\end{array}\right]
where Dp​q,α,xD_{pq,\alpha,x} is a surjective linear Cauchy–Riemann operator. Hence we can extend Tα,xT_{\alpha,x} to a block-upper triangular right inverse
Tp​q,x=[Tα,x∗0Tp​q,α,x].T_{pq,x}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}T_{\alpha,x}&*\\
0&T_{pq,\alpha,x}\end{array}\right].
In fact this only depends on the choice of the right inverse Tp​q,α,xT_{pq,\alpha,x} and we can make this choice continuously depending on xx.
The last ingredient we need for applying the implicit function theorem is a choice of local charts in the Banach manifold and local frames of the Banach vector bundle. In fact there is a natural one coming from the Riemannian metric on MM. For each x¯∈Bd\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d}, the fiberwise Banach manifold is a space of maps into the total space of a vector bundle Ep​q,x¯→ME_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\to M which is essentially a tensor bundle over MM. Hence the parallel transport using the Levi–Civita connection associated to the metric ω⁡(⋅,J)\omega(\cdot,J) induces local charts of the fiberwise Banach manifold.
We would like to say that so far the right inverse and the local charts/frames are chosen for points which are in normal position. If we go slightly away from the locus of normal position, the parallel transport along shortest geodesics automatically produce a family of local charts/frames as well as right inverses.
5.5.3. Definition of the stabilization map
Now we can describe the stabilization map. Let us temporarily denote by ℱp​q{\mathcal{F}}_{pq} the nonlinear Cauchy–Riemann operator (5.32). First, apply the approximate solution map (5.47)
θp​q,αapp:StabFp​q,α​(Vα)→∂α𝒞p​ql.\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm app}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.
(which is in fact only defined in a small disk bundle in Fp​q,αF_{pq,\alpha}). Then there exists ϵ>0\epsilon>0 such that for all x∈Vαx\in V_{\alpha} and (ηp​q,α,hp​q,α)∈Fp​q,α|x(\eta_{pq,\alpha},h_{pq,\alpha})\in F_{pq,\alpha}|_{x} with ‖ηp​q,α‖+‖hp​q,α‖<ϵ\|\eta_{pq,\alpha}\|+\|h_{pq,\alpha}\|<\epsilon, ℱp​q​(θp​q,αapp​(x,ηp​q,α,hp​q,α)){\mathcal{F}}_{pq}(\theta^{\rm app}_{pq,\alpha}(x,\eta_{pq,\alpha},h_{pq,\alpha})) is very small. Then using the local charts on the fiberwise Banach manifold induced from the Levi–Civita connection centered at xx and the corresponding right inverse of the linear operator, one obtains an exact solution to ℱp​q​(x)=0{\mathcal{F}}_{pq}(x)=0 which is sufficiently close to the approximate solution and its difference with the approximate solution is contained in the image of the right inverse.
There are several things to check. First, we need to show that the map is continuous. In fact, this is due to the continuity of the family of right inverses and approximate solutions and how Gromov topology on Vp​qV_{pq} is defined. Second, we show that the map is injective. This is a consequence of the implicit function theorem and the fact that (on each fiber over Bp​qB_{pq}), the approximate solution is transverse to the image of the right inverse we use. Third, we show that the map is onto an open subset. Indeed, from the construction we know that the stabilization map is onto an open subset on each fiber over BdB_{d}. As the image also covers a neighborhood of Bδ⁡(α)∼B_{\delta(\alpha)}^{\sim} inside ∂δ⁡(α)Bd\partial^{\delta(\alpha)}B_{d}, the stabilization map is onto an open subset. In fact, this is exactly the content of the gluing theorem presented in [Par16, Appendix C]. Again, the reader could refer to Proposition 6.22 to see how to implement the aforementioned theorem into our setting. Lastly, it satisfies the stratum-preserving property (5.45) which can be seen from the fact that the right inverses we used are all block upper-triangular.
Remark 5.61.
It is natural to require the stabilization map to be compatible with compositions of chart embeddings. In fact to achieve this goal one needs to make a number of compatible choices, including showing that such compatible choices exist. The choices include a system of compatible inner products on the obstruction bundles, a system of stabilization maps, and a system of bundle identifications. The details are given in Appendix C.
5.6. Weak K-chart lift

(which satisfy the required conditions).
(2)

5.7. Outer collaring
We need to apply the outer-collaring construction in order to improve the weak K-chart lift constructed above to a K-chart lift and set space for the inductive smoothing procedure. Recall that the outer-collaring construction has been reviewed in Subsection 3.3 in the topological category, including both the case of a single stratified space or a flow category, bimodule, etc. There is no more complexity to generalize to the case of the K-chart lift 𝔎Floer{\mathfrak{K}}^{\rm Floer}. Indeed, for each pair p<qp<q, if we apply the outer-collaring construction to the thickened moduli space Vp​qV_{pq} (with a fixed width, say 11), then one obtains a new 𝔸p​qFloer{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}-manifold, denoted by Vp​q+V_{pq}^{+}. The Gp​qG_{pq}-action naturally extends to a Gp​qG_{pq}-action on Vp​q+V_{pq}^{+}. The obstruction bundle Ep​qE_{pq} and the Kuranishi section Sp​qS_{pq} are also naturally extended to a bundle Ep​q+→Vp​q+E_{pq}^{+}\to V_{pq}^{+} and a section Sp​q+:Vp​q+→Ep​q+S_{pq}^{+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to E_{pq}^{+}. The footprint map ψp​q:Sp​q−1(0)/Gp​q→ℳ¯Floerp​q\psi_{pq}:S_{pq}^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer} is also canonically extended to a map
ψp​q+:(Sp​q+)−1(0)/Gp​q→(ℳ¯)Floerp​q+\psi_{pq}^{+}:(S_{pq}^{+})^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\to(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer})^{+}
where (recall that) (ℳ¯)Floerp​q+(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer})^{+} is the outer-collaring of ℳ¯p​qFloer\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}. Notice that the structure maps required for a K-chart lift of a flow category are also obtained among the new charts via outer-collaring and their properties are still satisfied. In summary, one obtains a K-chart lift (𝔎Floer)+({\mathfrak{K}}^{\rm Floer})^{+} of the outer-collared flow category (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}.
In a similar way, one can apply the outer-collaring construction to the system of auxiliary moduli spaces. Each space BdB_{d} becomes a new space Bd+B_{d}^{+} and the projection maps πp​q:Vp​q→Bdp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{d_{pq}} are extended to πp​q+:Vp​q+→Bdp​q+\pi_{pq}^{+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to B_{d_{pq}}^{+} with listed properties in Theorem 5.62 remain valid.
What we need to take care of is the smoothness of the outer-collared auxiliary moduli spaces. Though BdB_{d} is a smooth manifold with faces, it is not a priori clear whether Bd+B_{d}^{+} has an automatically induced smooth structure or if the outer-collaring of the maps ζ(d0,d1):Bd0×Bd1→∂(d0,d1)Bd0+d1\zeta_{(d_{0},d_{1})}:B_{d_{0}}\times B_{d_{1}}\to\partial^{(d_{0},d_{1})}B_{d_{0}+d_{1}} are still smooth. What we learned from the work of Fukaya–Oh–Ohta–Ono [FOOO] is that moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic curves have a stronger structure near corners which comes from their “exponential decay estimates” associated to the gluing construction. This kind of structure is formalized in [FOOO20, Chapter 25] under the name “admissible smooth structure.” In short, an admissible structure on a smooth manifold (or orbifold) with faces is an atlas of smooth charts such that the coordinate changes among them satisfy certain exponential decay estimates near the boundary and corner; moreover, the outer-collaring of an admissible smooth manifold or orbifolds automatically carries an admissible smooth structure. This argument applies to our situation of BdB_{d}. Indeed, BdB_{d} comes from the moduli space of stable maps into ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d} which is an algebraic object. The exponential decay estimate holds automatically, for both the original moduli and the real blowup BdB_{d}. Therefore, the outer-collaring Bd+B_{d}^{+} is still smooth and the embeddings
ζ(d0,d1)+:Bd0+×Bd1+→Bd0+d1+\zeta_{(d_{0},d_{1})}^{+}:B_{d_{0}}^{+}\times B_{d_{1}}^{+}\to B_{d_{0}+d_{1}}^{+}
are still smooth.
5.7.1. Matching the Kuranishi sections
The collection of global Kuranishi charts constructed in the previous section does not have matching Kuranishi maps over boundary or corners. Using the room constructed by the outer-collaring operation, we can connect these Kuranishi maps.
Proposition 5.63.
There exist a collection of Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant sections
Sˇp​q+:Vp​q+→Ep​q+\check{S}_{pq}^{+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to E_{pq}^{+}
satisfying the following conditions.
(1)
The restriction of Sˇp​q+\check{S}_{pq}^{+} to Vp​q⊂Vp​q+V_{pq}\subset V_{pq}^{+} coincides with Sp​q:Vp​q→Ep​qS_{pq}:V_{pq}\to E_{pq}.
(2)
With respect to the direct sum decomposition Ep​q+=Op​q+⊕Qp​q+E_{pq}^{+}=O_{pq}^{+}\oplus Q_{pq}^{+}, if we write Sˇp​q+\check{S}_{pq}^{+} as Sˇp​qO,+⊕Sˇp​qQ,+\check{S}_{pq}^{O,+}\oplus\check{S}_{pq}^{Q,+}, then Sˇp​qO,+=Sp​qO,+\check{S}_{pq}^{O,+}=S_{pq}^{O,+}.
(3)
There is a homeomorphism
ψp​q+:(Sˇp​q+)−1(0)/Gp​q≅ℳ¯.Floerp​q\psi_{pq}^{+}:(\check{S}_{pq}^{+})^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}.
(4)
Let ψˇp​q\check{\psi}_{pq} be the corresponding extension of the footprint map. If we replace Sp​q+S_{pq}^{+} by Sˇp​q+\check{S}_{pq}^{+} and replace ψp​q+\psi_{pq}^{+} by ψˇp​q+\check{\psi}_{pq}^{+} for all p​qpq, then the collection
(𝔎Floer)+:=((Kp​q+)p<q,(𝜾p​r​q+)p<r<q)({\mathfrak{K}}^{\rm Floer})^{+}:=\Big((K_{pq}^{+})_{p<q},({\bm{\iota}}_{prq}^{+})_{p<r<q}\Big)
is a K-chart lift of the flow category (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}.
Proof.
The failure of the naively obtained outer-collaring to be a K-chart lift is that the normalization condition (i.e. the section Sp​qQ,+:Vp​q+→Qp​q+S_{pq}^{Q,+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to Q_{pq}^{+}) does not match with the products on the boundary. We need to interpolate between Sp​qQ,+S_{pq}^{Q,+} and the products on the collar parts. Here we only describe the situation near a codimension one stratum p​r​qprq. Let (x,t)∈Vp​q+(x,t)\in V_{pq}^{+} be a point in the collar region ∂p​r​qVp​q×[−1,0]\partial^{prq}V_{pq}\times[-1,0]. Geometrically, xx is represented by a framed cylinder (Σ,u,μ,η)(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta) where Σ=Σp​r∪Σr​q\Sigma=\Sigma_{pr}\cup\Sigma_{rq} and the map μ:Σ→𝒞0,2​(dp​q)\mu:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}_{0,2}(d_{pq}) is represented by a frame
F=(f0,…,fdp​q)∈(H0​(Lu,Σ))dp​q+1.F=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{pq}})\in(H^{0}(L_{u},\Sigma))^{d_{pq}+1}.
Proposition 5.24 tells us that the underlying map μ¯:Σ→ℂ​ℙdp​q\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}} can be transformed canonically to a map μ¯normal:Σ→ℂ​ℙdp​q\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{\rm normal}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}} via an invertible complex matirx of the form Idp​q+1+ρQI_{d_{pq}+1}+\rho_{Q}. Choose a smooth cut-off function χ⁡(t)\chi(t) which is equal to 11 for tt near −1-1 and euqal to 00 for tt near 00. Then define
μ¯t=(Idp​q+1+χ⁡(t)​ρQ)​μ¯:Σ→ℂ​ℙdp​q.\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{t}=(I_{d_{pq}+1}+\chi(t)\rho_{Q})\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}.
Then μ¯t\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{t} is in normal position for tt near −1-1 and corresponding to a frame
Ft=(f0t,…,fdp​qt)∈(H0​(Lu,Σ))dp​q+1F^{t}=(f_{0}^{t},\ldots,f_{d_{pq}}^{t})\in(H^{0}(L_{u},\Sigma))^{d_{pq}+1}
(which is well-defined up to ℂ∗{\mathbb{C}}^{*}). For those tt near −1-1, the frame FtF^{t} can be reconstructed from a frame on the component Σp​r\Sigma_{pr} and a frame on Σr​q\Sigma_{rq} and one can interpolate between the normalization using from the L2L^{2}-inner product on Σ\Sigma and the normalization using the product construction. The situation of the interpolation near deeper strata can be iterated by a routine induction argument.
∎
5.7.2. Collar structure
The outer-collaring provides an analogous collar structure as in the case of derived orbifold liftings. To save notations we would rather view the collar structure as being given by “interior” collars than “exterior” ones. Therefore, we have obtained a K-chart lift of a flow category over 𝒫Floer{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer} such that each chart Kp​q=(Gp​q,Vp​q,Ep​q,Sp​q)K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq}) has a system of collars
Ep​q|∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α θ^p​q,αcollar Ep​q ∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α θp​q,αcollar Vp​q ,∀α∈𝔸p​qFloer\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 57.76045pt\raise 7.84723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37502pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.11127pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{pq}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}},\ \forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
(5.49)
which are compatible in a sense similar to the requirements of collar structures of a derived orbifold lift stated in Definition 3.26, where one needs to replace the notions for derived orbifold charts by the ones for K-charts, with extra care taken on the equivariance with respect to the Lie group actions. The readers are invited to fill in the details.
5.8. Constructing scaffolding
The stabilization map constructed previously describes the relation between a product chart and the corresponding stratum of a larger thickened moduli space. However, to inductively construct the FOP perturbations, we need to specify such stabilization maps in all such situations and we require that the stabilization maps are compatible in some natural way. Such a requirement was packaged in terms of the notion “scaffolding” for derived orbifold lifts of a flow category and in particular, in the smooth category (see Definition 3.28 and Definition 3.29). In our construction of the derived orbifold lift of (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}, we need a scaffolding-like structure to do smoothing. Hence, we first need such structures in the topological category, also in the equivariant setting but not in the orbifold setting. Below we state what we can construct in this setting; its proof is given in Appendix C. Just as (5.49), we simplify the notations by pretending that the K-charts are equipped with interior collars. The reader should keep in mind that the following proposition holds only after taking the outer-collaring. In the sequel, the resulting structure from the following proposition will be referred to as a collared scaffolding.
Proposition 5.64.
There exist the following objects.
(1)
For each p​qpq and each integer d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}, a Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant inner product on the bundle Op​q(d)→Vp​qO_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq}. To state the next class of objects, introduce the following notions. Let Op​q,α⊂Op​q|VαO_{pq,\alpha}\subset O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}} be the orthogonal complement of Oα→VαO_{\alpha}\to V_{\alpha} and let Qp​q,α⊂Qp​q|VαQ_{pq,\alpha}\subset Q_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}} be the orthogonal complement of Qα→VαQ_{\alpha}\to V_{\alpha} (with resepct to the trivial inner product on Qp​qQ_{pq}). Denote
Fp​q,α:=Op​q,α⊕Qp​q,α⊂Ep​q|VαF_{pq,\alpha}:=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}\subset E_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}
and its equivariantization
Fp​q,α∼:=Gp​q×GαFp​q,α⊂Ep​q|Vα∼.F_{pq,\alpha}^{\sim}:=G_{pq}\times_{G_{\alpha}}F_{pq,\alpha}\subset E_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}.
(2)
For each stratum α\alpha, a stratified topological embedding over the poset ∂α𝔸p​qFloer\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}
θp​q,α:StabFp​q,α​(Vα)↪∂αVp​q\theta_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
(5.50)
whose restriction to VαV_{\alpha} coincides with the embedding ιp​q,α:Vα↪∂αVp​q\iota_{pq,\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq} such that its equivariantization
θp​q,α∼:StabFp​q,α∼​(Vα∼)→∂αVp​q\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\sim}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}^{\sim}}(V_{\alpha}^{\sim})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
is an open embedding. This embedding induces a projection map
πp​q,α:∂αVp​q→Vα∼.\pi_{pq,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\to V_{\alpha}^{\sim}.
(5.51)
(3)
For each d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}, an equivariant bundle isomorphism
θ^p​q,α(d),∼:πp​q,α∗​(Op​q(d)|Vα∼)≅Op​q(d)|∂αVp​q.\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{(d),\sim}:\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\Big(O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\Big)\cong O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}.
(5.52)
These objects need to satisfy the following list of conditions.
(A1)
For all p​qpq and d′>d≥dp​qd^{\prime}>d\geq d_{pq}, the inclusion Op​q(d)↪Op​q(d′)O_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})} (5.34) is isometric.
(A2)
For each stratum α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q of p​qpq, the bundle embedding
ι^p​q,α(d),∼:Op​q,α(d)↪Op​q(d)|∂αVp​q\widehat{\iota}_{pq,\alpha}^{(d),\sim}:O_{pq,\alpha}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}
(5.53)
is isometric. By Lemma 5.65 below, it follows that, for any intermediate stratum β=ps1⋯smq\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q between α\alpha and p​qpq, the following is true. Abbreviate α0=pr1⋯s1\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}, …\ldots, αm=sm⋯rlq\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q as strata of p​s1ps_{1}, ⋯\cdots, sm​qs_{m}q respectively. Then
Op​q,α≅Op​q,β|Vα⊕(Op​s1,α0⊞⋯⊞Osm​q,αm).O_{pq,\alpha}\cong O_{pq,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus\left(O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus O_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}\right).
(We explain the notations here. The bundles Op​s1,α0O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}} etc. in the second summand above are subbundles of Op​s1|Vα0O_{ps_{1}}|_{V_{\alpha_{0}}} etc. Their product is a subbundle of Op​q|VαO_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}.) It implies that the bundle Op​q,αO_{pq,\alpha} is stratified (linearly) by all strata between α\alpha and the top stratum, where the stratum corresponding to the intermediate stratum β\beta is the subbundle
Oβ​α:=Op​s1,α0⊞⋯⊞Osm​q,αm.O_{\beta\alpha}:=O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus O_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.
Similarly, the bundle Qp​q,αQ_{pq,\alpha} is also stratified by subbundles Qβ​αQ_{\beta\alpha} where
Qβ​α=Qp​s1,α0⊞⋯⊞Qsm​q,αm.Q_{\beta\alpha}=Q_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus Q_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.
Denote
Fβ​α:=Oβ​α⊕Qβ​α⊂Fp​q,α.F_{\beta\alpha}:=O_{\beta\alpha}\oplus Q_{\beta\alpha}\subset F_{pq,\alpha}.
Then there is a splitting
Fp​q,α=Fp​q,β|Vα⊕Fβ​α.F_{pq,\alpha}=F_{pq,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus F_{\beta\alpha}.
(5.54)
(B1)
The stabilization maps are stratum-preserving, i.e., for any intermediate stratum β\beta between α\alpha and the top stratum, there holds
θp​q,α​(StabFβ​α​(Vα))⊂∂αVβ,\theta_{pq,\alpha}\Big({\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\Big)\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta},
where Fβ​αF_{\beta\alpha} is viewed as a subbundle of Fp​q,αF_{pq,\alpha} under the splitting (5.54), and the space VβV_{\beta} is identified with its image in ∂βVp​q\partial^{\beta}V_{pq} under ιp​q,β\iota_{pq,\beta}.
(B2)
The above restriction of the stabilization map coincides with the product of stabilization maps. More precisely this means the following. Abbreviate α0=pr1⋯s1\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}, …\ldots, αm=sm⋯rlq\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q as strata of p​s1ps_{1}, ⋯\cdots, sm​qs_{m}q respectively. The stabilization maps θp​s1,α0\theta_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}, ⋯\cdots, θsm​q,αm\theta_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}} are germs of homeomorphisms
θp​s1,α0:StabFp​s1,α0​(Vα0)→∂α0Vp​s1,⋯,θsm​q,αm:StabFsm​q,αm​(Vαm)→∂αmVsm​q.\theta_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}}(V_{\alpha_{0}})\to\partial^{\alpha_{0}}V_{ps_{1}},\ \cdots,\ \theta_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}}(V_{\alpha_{m}})\to\partial^{\alpha_{m}}V_{s_{m}q}.
Their product gives a germ of homeomorphisms
StabFp​s1,α0⊞⋯⊞Fsm​q,αm(Vα)→∂α0Vp​s1×⋯×∂αmVsm​q↪∂αVp​q.{\rm Stab}_{F_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus F_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha_{0}}V_{ps_{1}}\times\cdots\times\partial^{\alpha_{m}}V_{s_{m}q}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}.
Notice that the left hand side is just StabFβ​α​(Vα){\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}). We require that
θp​r1,α0×⋯×θsm​q,αm=θp​q,α|StabFβ​α​(Vα).\theta_{pr_{1},\alpha_{0}}\times\cdots\times\theta_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}=\theta_{pq,\alpha}|_{{\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}.
(Compare this conditions with the conditions in Definition 3.29).
(C1)
The bundle isomorphisms satisfy the following commutative diagram for all d<d′d<d^{\prime}.
πp​q,α∗​(Op​q(d)|Vα∼) θ^p​q,α(d),∼ (5.34) Op​q(d)|∂αVp​q (5.34) πp​q,α∗​(Op​q(d′)|Vα∼) θ^p​q,α(d′),∼ Op​q(d′)|∂αVp​q .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Hence they induce an isomorphism
θ^p​q,αO:πp​q,α∗​(Op​q∞|Vα∼)≅Op​q∞|∂αVp​q.\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}:\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\left(O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\right)\cong O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}.
(C2)
The bundle isomorphism θ^p​q,αO\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O} preserves the stratification. Namely, for any intermediate stratum β\beta between α\alpha and p​qpq, one has
θ^p​q,αO​(πp​q,α∗​(Oβ|Vα∼))=Oβ|∂αVp​q.\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}\left(\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}(O_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}})\right)=O_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}.
(D)
Let ζp​q,α=ζp​q,β+ζβ​α\zeta_{pq,\alpha}=\zeta_{pq,\beta}+\zeta_{\beta\alpha} be a point of Fp​q,αF_{pq,\alpha} at a point xα∈Vαx_{\alpha}\in V_{\alpha} with respect to the splitting (5.54). Then the above condition (C3) implies that the bundle isomorphism θ^p​q,αO\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O} identifies ζp​q,β\zeta_{pq,\beta} with a vector of Fp​q,βF_{pq,\beta} at xβ=θp​q,α​(xα,ζβ​α)∈∂αVβx_{\beta}=\theta_{pq,\alpha}(x_{\alpha},\zeta_{\beta\alpha})\in\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}. Then we require that
θp​q,β​(xβ,ζp​q,β)=θp​q,α​(xα,ζp​q,α).\theta_{pq,\beta}(x_{\beta},\zeta_{pq,\beta})=\theta_{pq,\alpha}(x_{\alpha},\zeta_{pq,\alpha}).
This implies that
πp​q,α∘πp​q,β=πp​q,α.\pi_{pq,\alpha}\circ\pi_{pq,\beta}=\pi_{pq,\alpha}.
(5.55)
(E)
The above implies that
πp​q,α∗​(Op​q∞|Vα∼)=πp​q,β∗​(πp​q,α∗​(Op​q∞|Vα∼)|∂αVβ∼).\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}(O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}})=\pi_{pq,\beta}^{*}\left(\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}(O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}})|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}^{\sim}}\right).
We require that the bundle isomorphism satisfies
θ^p​q,αO=θ^p​q,βO∘θ^p​q,αO.\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}=\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\beta}^{O}\circ\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}.
(5.56)
More precisely, using notations in condition (D) above, the following diagram commutes.
Op​q∞|xα\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{x_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}θ^p​q,αO\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}}θ^p​q,αO\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}}Op​q∞|xβ\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{x_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}θ^p​q,βO\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\beta}^{O}}Op​q∞|θp​q,α​(xα,ζp​q,α)\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{\theta_{pq,\alpha}(x_{\alpha},\zeta_{pq,\alpha})}}
(F)
Given any intermediate stratum β=ps1⋯smq\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q, using the notations used in (B2), one has
θ^p​q,αO|∂αVβ=θ^p​s1,α0O×⋯×θ^sm​q,αmO.\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}=\widehat{\theta}_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}^{O}\times\cdots\times\widehat{\theta}_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}^{O}.
(G)
The metric on Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)}, the stabilization map θp​q,α\theta_{pq,\alpha}, and the bundle isomorphism θ^p​q,αO\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O} respect collars. More precisely,
(a)
The bundle isomorphism associated to the collar structure θ^p​q,αcollar\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar} is isometric. It follows that following diagram commutes
Fp​q,β|∂αVβ×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β\textstyle{F_{pq,\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}θ^p​q,αcollar\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}Fp​q,β\textstyle{F_{pq,\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Ep​q|∂αVβ×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β\textstyle{E_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}θ^p​q,αcollar\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}Ep​q|Vβ\textstyle{E_{pq}|_{V_{\beta}}}
(b)
The following diagram commutes.
∂α(StabFp​q,β(Vβ))×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β StabFp​q,β​(Vβ) ∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β ∂βVp​q .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 71.35017pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-71.35017pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise (c)
The following diagram commutes.
(πp​q,β∗Op​q∞|Vβ∼)|∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β\textstyle{(\pi_{pq,\beta}^{*}O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\beta}^{\sim}})|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}πp​q,β∗​Op​q∞|Vβ∼\textstyle{\pi_{pq,\beta}^{*}O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\beta}^{\sim}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Op​q∞|∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Op​q∞|∂βVp​q\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{\partial^{\beta}V_{pq}}}

Lemma 5.65.
Suppose the bundles Op​q(d)→Vp​qO_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq} are equipped with Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant inner products which satisfy conditions (A1) and (A3) of Proposition 5.64. Then the following is true. Consider a pair of strata α,β∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer} where α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q and β=ps1⋯smq\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q. Denote α0=pr1⋯s1\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}, …\ldots, αm=sm⋯rlq\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q. Consider the bundles
Op​s1,α0→Vα0,⋯,Osm​q,αm→VαmO_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\to V_{\alpha_{0}},\ \cdots,\ O_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}\to V_{\alpha_{m}}
whose product is a subbundle
Oβ​α⊂Op​q|Vα.O_{\beta\alpha}\subset O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}.
Then as subbundles of Op​q|VαO_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}, one has
Op​q,α=Op​q,β|Vα⊕Oβ​α.O_{pq,\alpha}=O_{pq,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus O_{\beta\alpha}.
Proof.
Linear algebra.
∎
6. Smoothing
In this section, we use equivariant (relative) smoothing theory to equip the K-chart presentation of various moduli spaces constructed in Section 5 with smooth structures after stabilization. The proof is based on induction, which is quite similar to the construction of straightening and coherent FOP perturbations discussed in Section 3 and Section 4. After taking the associated quotient orbifold of the smooth K-chart presentation, we obtain a derived orbifold lift of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category TFloerT^{\rm Floer}. Moreover, we explain how to construct a normal complex structure on the resulting derived orbifold lift.
6.1. Preliminaries
We list out some basic notions relevant for the smoothing theory in this subsection. Then we state the relative smoothing statement Theorem 6.10 which will proved in Appendix B. Following that, some elementary technical results in the smoothing construction will be proved.
6.1.1. Microbundles
We start by discussing some notions and facts about microbundles and their roles in smoothing theory.
Definition 6.1.
Let XX be a topological space.
(1)
A microbundle (of rank nn) over XX is a diagram
X\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}s\scriptstyle{s}ℰ\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}p\scriptstyle{p}X\textstyle{X}
where ℰ{\mathscr{E}} is a topological space, pp and ss are continuous maps satisfying
(a)
p∘s=IdXp\circ s={\rm Id}_{X}.
(b)
for each x∈Xx\in X there exist a neighborhood Ux⊂XU_{x}\subset X of xx, a neighborhood 𝒱x⊂ℰ{\mathscr{V}}_{x}\subset{\mathscr{E}} of s⁡(x)s(x), and a homeomorphism
hx:Ux×ℝn→𝒱xh_{x}:U_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathscr{V}}_{x}
for which p∘hx=prUxp\circ h_{x}={\rm pr}_{U_{x}} and hx|Ux×{0}=sh_{x}|_{U_{x}\times\{0\}}=s. Here prUx{\rm pr}_{U_{x}} is the natural projection onto the factor UxU_{x}.
Often we use a single symbol ℰ{\mathscr{E}} to denote a microbundle.
(2)
Given two microbundles over XX (not necessarily of the same rank)
X s1 ℰ1 p1 X ,\displaystyle\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.2517pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.25824pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.8264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 72.8264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,\
X\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}s2\scriptstyle{s_{2}}ℰ2\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}p2\scriptstyle{p_{2}}X\textstyle{X}
a morphism from ℰ1{\mathscr{E}}_{1} to ℰ2{\mathscr{E}}_{2} is a germ of continuous maps ϕ:U1→E2\phi:U_{1}\to E_{2} defined over a neighborhood 𝒰1⊂ℰ1{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1} of s1​(X)s_{1}(X) which commutes with the maps s1,s2s_{1},s_{2} and p1,p2p_{1},p_{2}. Two morphisms ϕ1,ϕ2\phi_{1},\phi_{2} from ℰ1{\mathscr{E}}_{1} to ℰ2{\mathscr{E}}_{2}, defined over 𝒰1⊂ℰ1{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1} and 𝒰2⊂ℰ2{\mathscr{U}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{2} respectively, are called equivalent if there exists a neighborhood of s1​(X)s_{1}(X) in ℰ1{\mathscr{E}}_{1} contained in 𝒰1∩𝒰2{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\cap{\mathscr{U}}_{2} on which ϕ1=ϕ2\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}.
(3)
Using the notations as above, a morphism between microbundles
ℰ1{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{E}}_{1}}X{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}X{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}ℰ2{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}p1\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}ϕ\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}s1\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}s2\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}p2\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
is said to be an isomorphism if ϕ\phi maps 𝒰1{\mathscr{U}}_{1} homeomorphically onto an open subset 𝒰2⊂ℰ2{\mathscr{U}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{2} containing s2​(X)s_{2}(X).
(4)
An isotopy between two isomorphisms ϕ0,ϕ1:ℰ→ℰ′\phi_{0},\phi_{1}:{\mathscr{E}}\to{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime} of microbundles over XX is a family of microbundle isomorphisms ϕ~t:ℰ→ℰ′\tilde{\phi}_{t}:{\mathscr{E}}\to{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}, t∈[0,1]t\in[0,1] which defines a continuous map from ℰ×[0,1]→ℰ′{\mathscr{E}}\times[0,1]\to{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}, such that ϕ~0\tilde{\phi}_{0} is equivalent to ϕ0\phi_{0} and ϕ~1\tilde{\phi}_{1} is equivalent to ϕ1\phi_{1}.
Example 6.2.
(1)
If p:E→Xp:E\to X is an ordinary real vector bundle, then together with the zero section EE defines a microbundle over XX. We denote this microbundle by EμE_{\mu}.
(2)
Let XX be a topological manifold. The tangent microbundle is the diagram
X\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Δ\scriptstyle{\Delta}X×X\textstyle{X\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}p1\scriptstyle{p_{1}}X\textstyle{X}
where p1p_{1} is the projection onto the first factor. We denote the tangent microbundle of XX by Tμ​XT_{\mu}X.
If a topological manifold admits a smooth structure, then its tangent microbundle is isomorphic to the associated tangent bundle. In general, a microbundle ℰ{\mathscr{E}} over a space XX may or may not come from a vector bundle. We recall the following definition.
Definition 6.3.
Let ℰ→X{\mathscr{E}}\to X be a microbundle. A vector bundle lift (or vector bundle reduction) of ℰ{\mathscr{E}} is a vector bundle E→XE\to X together with a microbundle isomorphism Eμ→ℰE_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{E}}.
Microbundles have many properties similar to vector bundles. One can construct microbundles by taking certain operations.
Definition 6.4 (Direct sums of microbundles).
Let X\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}si\scriptstyle{s_{i}}ℰi\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}pi\scriptstyle{p_{i}}X\textstyle{X}, i=1,2i=1,2 be two microbundles and let Δ:X→X×X\Delta:X\to X\times X be the diagonal inclusion map. Let
ℰ12:=(p1×p2)−1​(Δ⁡(X))⊂ℰ1×ℰ2.{\mathscr{E}}_{12}:=(p_{1}\times p_{2})^{-1}(\Delta(X))\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\times{\mathscr{E}}_{2}.
Then the direct sum of ℰ1{\mathscr{E}}_{1} and ℰ2{\mathscr{E}}_{2} is the microbundle
X (s1×s2)∘Δ ℰ12 p1×p2 Δ⁡(X)≅X .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.27419pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(s_{1}\times s_{2})\circ\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 61.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{12}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 95.66237pt\raise 5.84723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.48613pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p_{1}\times p_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 136.81255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 106.81255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 136.81255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta(X)\cong X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.
On the other hand, the notions of pullbacks and restrictions of microbundles can be defined in the same way as for the case of vector bundles. The correspondence E↦EμE\mapsto E_{\mu} commutes with these operations.
For the purpose of discussing the uniqueness of stable (equivariant) smoothings later, we need to compare different vector bundle lifts.
Definition 6.5.
([Las79])
Let ϕi:(Ei)μ→ℰ\phi_{i}:(E_{i})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{E}}, i=0,1i=0,1 be two vector bundle lifts of a microbundle ℰ{\mathscr{E}} over XX. We say that ϕ0\phi_{0} and ϕ1\phi_{1} are
(1)
equivalent if there exists a vector bundle isomorphism ψ:E0→E1\psi:E_{0}\to E_{1} (which induces a microbundle isomorphism ψ:(E0)μ→(E1)μ\psi:(E_{0})_{\mu}\to(E_{1})_{\mu} such that ϕ0\phi_{0} is equivalent to ϕ1∘ψ\phi_{1}\circ\psi,
(2)
isotopic if there exist a vector bundle E→X×[0,1]E\to X\times[0,1] and a continuous family of vector bundle lifts ϕt:(E|X×{t})μ→ℰ\phi_{t}:(E|_{X\times\{t\}})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{E}} which restricts to ϕ0\phi_{0} and ϕ1\phi_{1} when t=0,1t=0,1.
Example 6.6.
Suppose MM is a smooth manifold. Then there is a canonical isotopy class of vector bundle reductions of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M. Choose a Riemannian metric gg on MM and let exp\exp be the associated exponential map. Then there is an open neighborhood U⁡(T​M)U(TM) of the zero section of T​MTM such that the exponential map is defined over U⁡(T​M)U(TM). Then define
φg:U⁡(T​M)→M×M,(x,ξ)↦(x,expx⁡ξ)\varphi_{g}:U(TM)\to M\times M,\ (x,\xi)\mapsto(x,\exp_{x}\xi)
which is a vector bundle reduction of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M. Moreover, the vector bundle reduction defined by any two Riemannian metrics are isotopic as microbundle isomorphisms.
Now we discuss the equivariant situation.
Definition 6.7.
Let GG be a topological group and XX be a GG-space. A GG-microbundle is a microbundle X\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}s\scriptstyle{s}ℰ\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}p\scriptstyle{p}X\textstyle{X} where ℰ{\mathscr{E}} is a GG-space such that pp and ss are both equivariant.
All previous notions for non-equivariant microbundles can be easily extended to the equivariant situation. We just emphasize that if XX is a GG-topological manifold, then its tangent microbundle Tμ​XT_{\mu}X is a GG-microbundle; if GG is a compact Lie group and XX is a smooth GG-manifold, then its tangent microbundle Tμ​XT_{\mu}X admits a GG-equivariant vector bundle lift (by its smooth tangent bundle); a concrete lift can be given via the exponential map associated to a GG-invariant Riemannian metric; moreover, the equivariant isotopy class of such a lift is unique.
6.1.2. Stable equivariant smoothings
Let MM be a topological manifold (without boundary). A smoothing of MM is by definition a pair (N,ϕ)(N,\phi) where NN is a smooth manifold and ϕ:N→M\phi:N\to M is a homeomorphism. A smoothing is also equivalent to a smooth structure α\alpha on MM, i.e., a maximal atlas of C∞C^{\infty} compatible charts. Two smooth structures α0\alpha_{0}, α1\alpha_{1} on MM are called isotopic if there is an isotopy (through homeomorphisms) between IdM{\rm Id}_{M} and a diffeomorphism ϕ:Mα0→Mα1\phi:M_{\alpha_{0}}\to M_{\alpha_{1}}, where MαiM_{\alpha_{i}} is the smooth manifold given by equipping MM the smooth structure αi\alpha_{i}.
If GG is a compact Lie group acting on MM, then a GG-smoothing is a smooth structure α\alpha on MM such that the GG-action is smooth. Two GG-smoothings α0\alpha_{0} and α1\alpha_{1} are GG-isotopic if there is an isotopy through GG-equivariant homeomorphisms between IdM{\rm Id}_{M} and a diffeomorphism from Mα0M_{\alpha_{0}} to Mα1M_{\alpha_{1}}.
For the non-equivariant case, one can see that a necessary condition for a topological manifold being smoothable is that its tangent microbundle admits a vector bundle lift. In fact, this is almost also a sufficient condition. Once the tangent microbundle of a topological manifold MM admits a vector bundle lift, then MM is stably smoothable, i.e., there is a smoothing (M×ℝk)α(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k})_{\alpha} on M×ℝkM\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k} for some k≥0k\geq 0 [Mil64, Section 5]. Two stable smoothings (M×ℝki)αi(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{i}})_{\alpha_{i}}, i=0,1i=0,1, are stably isotopic if there exist k0′,k1′≥0k_{0}^{\prime},k_{1}^{\prime}\geq 0 with k0+k0′=k=k1+k1′k_{0}+k_{0}^{\prime}=k=k_{1}+k_{1}^{\prime} and an isotopy between (M×ℝk0)α0×ℝk0′(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{0}})_{\alpha_{0}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{0}^{\prime}} and (M×ℝk1)α1×ℝk1′(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{1}})_{\alpha_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{1}^{\prime}} as smoothings on M×ℝkM\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k}, where the Euclidean spaces are endowed with the standard smooth structure. The work of Lashof [Las79] basically shows that this correspondence extends to the GG-equivariant case. Let us introduce the notion of stable GG-smoothings.
Definition 6.8.
[Las79]
Let GG be a compact Lie group and MM be a topological GG-manifold.
(1)
A stable GG-smoothing of MM is a GG-smoothing on the product M×𝑹M\times{\bm{R}} where 𝑹{\bm{R}} is a finite-dimensional orthogonal representation of GG.
(2)
Two stable GG-smoothings, (M×𝑹i)αi(M\times{\bm{R}}_{i})_{\alpha_{i}}, i=0,1i=0,1, of MM, are stably GG-isotopic, if there exist orthogonal representations 𝑹0′{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}, 𝑹1′{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime} of GG such that 𝑹0⊕𝑹0′≅𝑹≅𝑹1⊕𝑹1′{\bm{R}}_{0}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}\cong{\bm{R}}\cong{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime} as GG-representations and (M×𝑹0)α0×𝑹0′(M\times{\bm{R}}_{0})_{\alpha_{0}}\times{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime} is isotopic to (M×𝑹1)α1×𝑹1′(M\times{\bm{R}}_{1})_{\alpha_{1}}\times{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime} as GG-smoothings on M×𝑹M\times{\bm{R}}.
(3)
A stable GG-vector bundle lift of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M is a GG-vector bundle lift of Tμ​M⊕RT_{\mu}M\oplus R, where R→MR\to M is the trivial bundle M×𝑹M\times{\bm{R}} with 𝑹{\bm{R}} being an orthogonal representation of GG.
(4)
Two stable GG-vector bundle lifts, ϕi:(Ei)μ≅Tμ​M⊕Ri\phi_{i}:(E_{i})_{\mu}\cong T_{\mu}M\oplus R_{i}, i=0,1i=0,1, are stably GG-isotopic, if there are orthogonal GG-representations 𝑹0′{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}, 𝑹1′{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime}, with 𝑹0⊕𝑹0′≅𝑹≅𝑹1⊕𝑹1′{\bm{R}}_{0}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}\cong{\bm{R}}\cong{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime} such that the induced GG-vector bundle lifts
ϕi⊕IdRi′:(Ei⊕Ri′)μ≅TμM⊕Ri⊕Ri′≅TμM⊕R,i=0,1\phi_{i}\oplus{\rm Id}_{R_{i}^{\prime}}:(E_{i}\oplus R_{i}^{\prime})_{\mu}\cong T_{\mu}M\oplus R_{i}\oplus R_{i}^{\prime}\cong T_{\mu}M\oplus R,\ i=0,1
are GG-isotopic.
Any stable GG-smoothing (M×𝑹)α(M\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha} of MM induces a stable GG-vector bundle lift
T​(M×𝑹)α→Tμ​M⊕R.T(M\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha}\to T_{\mu}M\oplus R.
However it is not obvious that the stable GG-isotopy class of the lift only depends on the stable GG-isotopy class of the smoothing. By using an intermediate notion of stable sliced concordance among stable GG-smoothings, Lashof [Las79] showed that there is indeed such a correspondence sending stable GG-isotopy classes of stable GG-smoothings to stable GG-isotopy classes of stable GG-vector bundle reductions of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M. In fact, this correspondence is bijective.
Theorem 6.9.
[Las79] Let GG be a compact Lie group and MM be a topological GG-manifold which only has finitely many orbit types. Suppose E→ME\to M is a GG-equivariant vector bundle and φ:Eμ→Tμ​M\varphi:E_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}M is a GG-equivariant vector bundle reduction of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M, then there exists an orthogonal GG-representation 𝐑{\bm{R}} and a GG-smoothing α\alpha on M×𝐑M\times{\bm{R}} satisfying
(1)
There is a GG-equivariant vector bundle isomorphism ρ:pM∗​E⊕R≅T​(M×𝑹)α\rho:p_{M}^{*}E\oplus R\cong T(M\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha} where pM:M×𝑹→Mp_{M}:M\times{\bm{R}}\to M is the natural projection.
(2)
Denote by ρμ\rho_{\mu} the induced map between microbundles from ρ\rho. Then the vector bundle reduction expM×R∘ρμ:pM∗​Eμ⊕R→Tμ​(M×𝑹)\exp_{M\times R}\circ\rho_{\mu}:p_{M}^{*}E_{\mu}\oplus R\to T_{\mu}(M\times{\bm{R}}) is isotopic to the stabilization pM∗​φ⊕IdRp_{M}^{*}\varphi\oplus{\rm Id}_{R}.1717
17
This claim is not explicitly stated in [Las79] but can be observed from Lashof’s construction.
Moreover, the correspondence φ↦α\varphi\mapsto\alpha induces a bijection between stable isotopy classes of stable GG-smoothings of MM and stable isotopy classes of stable GG-vector bundle reductions of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M.
Our construction relies on the following relative version of Lashof’s theorem.
Theorem 6.10.
Let GG, MM, EE, and φ\varphi be as in Theorem 6.9. Let C⊂MC\subset M be a GG-invariant closed set and U⊂MU\subset M be a GG-invariant open neighborhood of CC. Suppose
(1)
UU is equipped with a GG-smoothing α0\alpha_{0} and E|UE|_{U} is equipped with a smooth GG-equivariant vector bundle structure.
(2)
φ|U:(E|U)μ→Tμ​U\varphi|_{U}:(E|_{U})_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}U is a smooth microbundle reduction and is isotopic to the microbundle reduction T​Uα0→Tμ​UTU_{\alpha_{0}}\to T_{\mu}U.
Then there exists an orthogonal GG-representation 𝐑{\bm{R}} and a GG-smoothing α\alpha on M×𝐑M\times{\bm{R}} such that over a GG-invariant open neighborhood U′⊂UU^{\prime}\subset U of CC, (U′×𝐑)α(U^{\prime}\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha} is diffeomorphic to the product (U′)α0×𝐑(U^{\prime})_{\alpha_{0}}\times{\bm{R}}.
Proof.
See Appendix B.
∎
6.1.3. Topological submersions
Now we start to prepare for smoothing the K-chart lift of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category following a generalization of the strategy used in [AMS21]. First, we discuss topological submersions which are relevant for constructing lifts of tangent microbundles.
Definition 6.11.
Let MM be an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space and BB be a smooth 𝔸′{\mathbb{A}}^{\prime}-manifold. Let π:M→B\pi:M\to B be a continuous map covering a map between the posets 𝔸→𝔸′{\mathbb{A}}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{\prime}.
(1)
Let p∈Mp\in M and b=π⁡(p)∈Bb=\pi(p)\in B. A product neighborhood of pp is a pair (W,ι)(W,\iota) where WW is an open neighborhood of pp (denoting W|b=π−1​(b)∩WW|_{b}=\pi^{-1}(b)\cap W) and ι:W→W|b×π⁡(W)\iota:W\to W|_{b}\times\pi(W) is an homeomorphism satisfying
•
π⁡(W)\pi(W) is an open neighborhood of bb;
•
π∘ι−1:W|b×π⁡(W)→π⁡(W)\pi\circ\iota^{-1}:W|_{b}\times\pi(W)\to\pi(W) is the projection to π⁡(W)\pi(W);
•
ι|W|b:W|b→W|b×{b}\iota|_{W|_{b}}:W|_{b}\to W|_{b}\times\{b\} is the identity map.
(2)
The map π\pi is called a topological submersion if every point p∈Mp\in M admits a product neighborhood and each fiber is a topological manifold (of a fixed dimension) without boundary. In this case, the vertical tangent microbundle Tμvt​MT_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M is the microbundle
M\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Δ\scriptstyle{\Delta}M×BM\textstyle{M\times_{B}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}p1\scriptstyle{p_{1}}M\textstyle{M}
where p1:M×BM→Mp_{1}:M\times_{B}M\to M is the projection to the first factor, viewing the fiber product M×BMM\times_{B}M as a subset of M×MM\times M.
Remark 6.12.
Our primary examples of topological submersions come from the forgetful map πp​q:Vp​q→Bp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq} in (5.38). It is important to notice that the fibers are indeed topological manifolds without boundary, because the complex structure on the domain of the element (Σ,u,F,η1,…,ηd)(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{d}) has been fixed.
Now we include group actions. Let GG be a compact Lie group. Recall that a GG-action on an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-space is a continuous GG-action which preserve each stratum. We assume that GG acts continuously on MM and smoothly on BB, and that π:M→B\pi:M\to B is GG-equivariant. Then for each g∈Gg\in G, p∈Mp\in M, and a product neighborhood (W,ι)(W,\iota) of pp, (g⁡(W),g∗​ι)(g(W),g_{*}\iota) is a product neighborhood of g⁡(p)g(p), where
g∗​ι​(w)=g⁡(ι⁡(g−1​(w))).g_{*}\iota(w)=g(\iota(g^{-1}(w))).
Here g:W|b×π⁡(W)→W|g⁡(b)×π⁡(g⁡(W))g:W|_{b}\times\pi(W)\to W|_{g(b)}\times\pi(g(W)) is the diagonal action.
Definition 6.13.
[AMS21, Definition 4.19]
For each p∈Mp\in M, denote by Gp⊂GG_{p}\subset G the stabilizer of pp. A GpG_{p}-invariant product neighborhood of pp is a product neighborhood (W,ι)(W,\iota) of pp which is GpG_{p}-invariant, i.e., (g⁡(W),g∗​ι)=(W,ι)(g(W),g_{*}\iota)=(W,\iota) for any g∈Gpg\in G_{p}.
We say that the GG-equivariant map π:M→B\pi:M\to B is a GG-equivariant topological submersion if every point p∈Mp\in M admits a GpG_{p}-invariant product neighborhood. We say that the GG-action is fiberwise locally linear if for each b∈Bb\in B, the action of Gb⊂GG_{b}\subset G on MbM_{b} is locally linear.
Remark 6.14.
Any GG-equivariant vector bundle p:E→Bp:E\to B is a fiberwise locally linear equivariant topological submersion.
Now we recall how to construct smoothings of the total space of a topological submersion with a fiberwise smooth structure.
Definition 6.15.
[AMS21, Definition 4.27]
Let GG be a compact Lie group. Let MM be a topological manifold with a GG-action. Let BB be a smooth GG-manifold and π:M→B\pi:M\to B be a GG-equivariant topological submersion.
(1)
A fiberwise smooth structure on MM (with respect to the map π\pi) is a choice of smooth structures (by definition a maximal C∞C^{\infty}-compatible atlas) on all fibers of π\pi.
(2)
Given a fiberwise smooth structure on MM, two product neighborhoods ιi:Wi→Wi|bi×π⁡(Wi)\iota_{i}:W_{i}\to W_{i}|_{b_{i}}\times\pi(W_{i}), i=1,2i=1,2 are Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1}-compatible if for each p∈W1∩W2p\in W_{1}\cap W_{2} there exists a product neighborhood ι:W→W|b×π⁡(W)\iota:W\to W|_{b}\times\pi(W), b=π⁡(p)b=\pi(p) satisfying the following conditions. For each v∈π⁡(W)v\in\pi(W), the definition of product neighborhoods (see Definition 6.11) implies that the map
ιv:W|v→{v}×W|b\iota_{v}:W|_{v}\to\{v\}\times W|_{b}
is a homeomorphism. Consider the family of maps
ηv:W|b→Wi|bi,w↦Πi​(ιi​(ιv−1​(w)))\eta_{v}:W|_{b}\to W_{i}|_{b_{i}},\ w\mapsto\Pi_{i}\big(\iota_{i}(\iota_{v}^{-1}(w))\big)
where Πi:Wi|bi×π⁡(Wi)→Wi|bi\Pi_{i}:W_{i}|_{b_{i}}\times\pi(W_{i})\to W_{i}|_{b_{i}} is the projection. Then for each vv and i=1,2i=1,2, ηv\eta_{v} is a smooth map from W|bW|_{b} to Wi|biW_{i}|_{b_{i}} and varies with vv continuously with respect to the Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1}-topology.
(3)
A fiberwise smooth Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} GG-bundle is a GG-equivariant topological submersion π:M→B\pi:M\to B together with a collection (ιi)i∈I(\iota_{i})_{i\in I} of Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1}-compatible GpG_{p}-invariant product neighborhoods around a collection of points (pi)i∈I(p_{i})_{i\in I} whose domains cover MM.
Remark 6.16.
Despite our previous discussions work under the setting that MM is an 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold, the above notions is defined for MM being a manifold without boundary. In practice, when we perform smoothing inductively, we apply Theorem 6.10 to the top stratum of a topological 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold MM. Therefore, it suffices to set up certain parts of the theory for manifolds without boundary or corners.
6.1.4. Fiberwise submersions
A fiberwise smooth Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1}-bundle π:M→B\pi:M\to B has a well-defined vertical tangent bundle, denoted by Tvt​MT^{\rm vt}M, by patching up the tangent bundles of the fibers, and an isotopy class of GG-vector bundle lift
Tvt​M→Tμvt​MT^{\rm vt}M\to T_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M
by using a GG-invariant fiberwise Riemannian metric (see [AMS21, Lemma 4.29]).
Now suppose π:M→B\pi:M\to B is a fiberwise smooth Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} GG-bundle. There is a well-defined GG-microbundle
π∗​Tμ​B⊕Tμvt​M,\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M,
a well-defined GG-vector bundle
π∗​T​B⊕Tvt​M,\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}M,
and a well-defined GG-isotopy class of vector bundle lifts
(π∗​T​B⊕Tvt​M)μ→π∗​Tμ​B⊕Tμvt​M(\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}M)_{\mu}\to\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M
(6.3)
constructed using the exponential map after further choosing a Riemannian metric on BB. Note that both the space of fiberwise Riemannian metrics on MM varying continuously over BB, and the space of Riemannian metrics are contractible. Therefore, all these choices induce the same isotopy class of the vector bundle reduction for the direct sum π∗​Tμ​B⊕Tμvt​M\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M.
To qualify the condition for stable GG-smoothing (see Theorem 6.9), one only needs to identify the right hand side with the tangent microbundle of the total space MM. However this identification is not canonical even up to isotopy of microbundle isomorphisms. This is what we need to construct during the smoothing process.
Remark 6.17.
Different data for constructing the splitting (6.3) from [AMS21, Lemma 4.24, Proposition 4.26] can in fact be interpolated with each other, so the splittings (6.3) arising from this way should be isotopic to each other. Keeping track of these choices are important for showing that the “invariants” constructed in this paper are indeed independent of all the auxiliary choices, but we do not need such invariance results for our applications.
Definition 6.18.
[AMS21, Definition 4.22]
Suppose π:M→B\pi:M\to B is a GG-equivariant topological submersion. Let W⊂MW\subset M be a GG-invariant open subset. A GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion along WW is a continuous map ϕ:W~→M\phi:\widetilde{W}\to M where W~⊂W×W\widetilde{W}\subset W\times W is an open neighborhood of the diagonal satisfying the following properties.
(1)
ϕq,b:=ϕ|W~∩({q}×Mb)\phi_{q,b}:=\phi|_{\widetilde{W}\cap(\{q\}\times M_{b})} is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of Mπ⁡(q)M_{\pi(q)} for all b∈π⁡(W)b\in\pi(W) and q∈Wq\in W.
(2)
ϕq,π⁡(q)\phi_{q,\pi(q)} sends each point (q,q′)∈W~∩({q}×Mπ⁡(q))(q,q^{\prime})\in\widetilde{W}\cap(\{q\}\times M_{\pi(q)}) to q′q^{\prime}.
(3)
W~\widetilde{W} is a GG-invariant set with respect to the diagonal GG-action on W×WW\times W and ϕ\phi is a GG-equivariant map.
Remark 6.19.
Suppose MM is smooth and π:M→B\pi:M\to B is a smooth submersion. Then one can construct a fiberwise submersion using a GG-invariant Riemannian metric and the exponential map in the horizontal direction.
Remark 6.20.
A GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion along W=MW=M provides a GG-microbundle morphism
τ:Tμ​M→Tμvt​M\tau:T_{\mu}M\to T_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M
which is defined as
M×M⊇M~∋(p,p′)↦(p,ϕ⁡(p,p′))M\times M\supseteq\widetilde{M}\ni(p,p^{\prime})\mapsto(p,\phi(p,p^{\prime}))
which is the identity morphism on the sub-microbundle Tμvt​M⊂Tμ​MT_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M\subset T_{\mu}M. On the other hand, there is a canonical GG-microbundle morphism
P:Tμ​M→π∗​Tμ​B,(p,p′)↦(p,(π⁡(p),π⁡(p′))).P:T_{\mu}M\to\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B,\ (p,p^{\prime})\mapsto(p,(\pi(p),\pi(p^{\prime}))).
Hence the GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion induces an isomorphism of microbundles
P⊕τ:Tμ​M→π∗​Tμ​B⊕Tμvt​M.P\oplus\tau:T_{\mu}M\to\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M.
If π\pi is a fiberwise smooth Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} GG-bundle, then a GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion along MM induces a well-defined GG-isotopy class of vector bundle lift
(π∗​T​B⊕Tvt​M)μ≅Tμ​M.(\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}M)_{\mu}\cong T_{\mu}M.
Lemma 6.21.
Let C⊂MC\subset M be a GG-invariant closed set and U⊂MU\subset M be a GG-invariant open neighborhood of CC. Let D⊂MD\subset M be another GG-invariant closed set. Suppose ϕ:U~→M\phi:\widetilde{U}\to M is a GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion along UU. Then there exists a GG-invariant open neighborhood WW of C∪DC\cup D and a GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion ψ:W~→M\psi:\widetilde{W}\to M along WW which coincides with ϕ\phi in a small neighborhood of the diagonal of C×CC\times C in U×UU\times U.
Proof.
This is a restatement of the extension lemma [AMS21, Lemma 4.24] and a relative version of [AMS21, Proposition 4.25].
∎
6.2. Pre-smoothing operations and stable complex structures
This subsection can be viewed as a continuation of Section 5, in which we explore the more refined information of the K-charts Kp​q=(Vp​q,Ep​q,Gp​q,Sp​q)K_{pq}=(V_{pq},E_{pq},G_{pq},S_{pq}). These properties will be used in the construction of smoothing. Furthermore, we also present materials relevant for building the normal complex structure.
6.2.1. Topological submersions
We have the following analogue of [AMS21, Corollary 6.28, 6.29].
Proposition 6.22.
The natural forgetful map
πp​q:Vp​q→Bdp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{d_{pq}}
is a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant topological submersion and has the structure of a Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} fiberwise smooth Gp​qG_{pq}-bundle.
Proof.
This statement essentially a reformulation of the standard gluing construction in Floer theory which is presented for instance, in [Par16, Appendix C]. The slight difference is, here we are in a Morse–Bott situation because the asymptotic operator of elements in the moduli space ℳJΨ~,H​(k1,…,kd){\mathcal{M}}_{J_{\tilde{\Psi}},H}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}) has nontrivial kernel along the 𝒞{\mathcal{C}} and ℰi{\mathcal{E}}_{i} direction. However, this does not introduce any problem to the argument.
As a first step, let us show that πp​q\pi_{pq} is a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant topological submersion. Consider a point x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq} represented by (u,Σ,F,0)(u,\Sigma,F,0). Using the sheared almost complex structure and Gromov’s graph trick, we can view (u,Σ,F,η1,…,ηd)(u,\Sigma,F,\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{d}) as a pseudo-holomorphic stable cylinder contained in the 00-section of ℰd→M×𝒞{\mathcal{E}}_{d}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}. Recall that the framing FF defines a holomorphic map ιF:Σ→ℂ​ℙd\iota_{F}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d} of degree dd. Let us choose d′=(d+2)​dd^{\prime}=(d+2)d generic hyperplanes H1,…,Hd′⊂ℂ​ℙdH_{1},\ldots,H_{d^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{d} which intersect the image of μ¯\underline{\smash{\mu}} transversely at points different from nodes and markings, and choose
zi∈v(Σ)∩Hi,i=1,…,d′.z_{i}\in v(\Sigma)\cap H_{i},\ i=1,\ldots,d^{\prime}.
Then the map [v]↦(z1,…,zd′)[v]\mapsto(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d^{\prime}}) is a local diffeomorphism from ℱ(d)ℝ0,2{\mathcal{F}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(d) to an open subset j:U⊂ℳ¯0,2+d′ℝj:U\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{R}}. These intersection points play the role as local stabilizing divisors. Namely, we choose the preimage of the divisors H1,…,Hd′H_{1},\dots,H_{d^{\prime}} under the projection map ℰd→M×𝒞→𝒞→ℂ​Pd{\mathcal{E}}_{d}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}P}^{d} to be the local stabilizing divisors. By keeping track of the positions of the marked points obtained from intersecting the framing map with these divisors, we obtain map
μH:Vp​q→ℳ¯0,2+d′ℝ\mu_{H}:V_{pq}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{R}}
defined over a neighborhood of x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq}.
Let KmapK_{\rm map} be the kernel of the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator at (u,ιF,0)(u,\iota_{F},0) (without deforming the underlying map to ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d}). The gluing map
g:U×K→Vp​qg:U\times K\to V_{pq}
defined by [Par16, Equation (C.10.3)] fit into the following diagram:
U×K g prU Vp​q μH U j ℳ¯0,2+d′ℝ .\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 19.3063pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.67424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 59.04565pt\raise-16.06139pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu_{H}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.04565pt\raise-23.21053pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
