41.67424pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.67424pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{R}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.
According to [Par16, Section C.12], gg is a homeomorphism onto its image. The commutativity of the above diagram follows from the definition [Par16, Equation (C.10.4)]. Furthermore, the restriction of the gluing map gg to a fiber {a}×K\{a\}\times K is smooth, see [Par16, Section C.9] (this has already been explored in Section 5). Note that the topological submersion property holds over a Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant open neighborhood of Sp​q−1​(0)S_{pq}^{-1}(0) in Vp​qV_{pq} by the transversality assumption. Up to restricting to such an open subset, we see that πp​q:Vp​q→Bp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq} is indeed a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant topological submersion.
It remains to see that πp​q\pi_{pq} actually has a Cloc1C^{1}_{\rm loc} Gp​qG_{pq}-bundle structure. Note that the gluing construction of JJ-holomorphic curves is based on applying a Newton–Picard iteration scheme to a pre-glued curve. By [Par16, Proposition B.11.1, C.11.1], the L2L^{2}-norm of the honest solution near the ends of the neck region controls all CkC^{k}-norms through the region. With this in mind, the rest of the argument follows from [AMS21, Corollary 6.29].
∎
As a consequence, we see that the tangent microbundle of the interior of Vp​qV_{pq} admits a vector bundle lift by (6.3) and Lemma 6.21. This suffices for us to construct a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant smoothing on the interior of Vp​qV_{pq}, but it falls short of providing a smoothing of Vp​qV_{pq} as a manifold with corners. This problem will be solved on the outer-collaring Vp​q+V_{pq}^{+} by performing an inductive argument.
6.2.2. Vertical stable complex structures
Following [AB21, Section 11], we describe how to construct a stabilization of the vertical tangent bundle Tvt​Vp​qT^{\rm vt}V_{pq} of the Cloc1C^{1}_{\rm loc} Gp​qG_{pq}-bundle πp​q:Vp​q→Bp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq} such that the resulting vector bundle admits a complex structure. We also explain how to use these stable complex structures to construct normal complex structures on derived orbifold charts.
For any 11-periodic orbit p¯\underline{\smash{p}} of HH, fix a unitary trivialization of the complex vector bundle p¯∗​T​M→S1\underline{\smash{p}}^{*}TM\to S^{1}. Moreover, we choose a complex linear connection ∇\nabla defined on the pullback of T​MTM under the projection map S1×M→MS^{1}\times M\to M, such that its restriction to the graph of any 11-periodic orbit is induced by the chosen trivializations. For a 11-periodic orbit p¯\underline{\smash{p}}, using the projection prS1:ℝ×S1→S1{\rm pr}_{S^{1}}:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to S^{1}, we consider the complex vector bundle
(p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M→ℝ×S1.(\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM\to{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}.
Denote by BH,p¯​(t):S1→End​(p¯∗​T​M)B_{H,\underline{\smash{p}}}(t):S^{1}\to\text{End}(\underline{\smash{p}}^{*}TM) the section obtained by differentiating the flow of XHtX_{H_{t}}, i.e.,
BH,p¯​(t)​(W)=∇WXHt,∀W∈Tp¯​(t)​M.B_{H,\underline{\smash{p}}}(t)(W)=\nabla_{W}X_{H_{t}},\ \forall W\in T_{\underline{\smash{p}}(t)}M.
Using a smooth cut-off function, we can consider the Cauchy–Riemann operator
∇p¯0,1:Ω0​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)→Ω0,1​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}:\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\to\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)
such that
∇p¯0,1={(∇−BH,p¯⊗d​t)J0,1​ near −∞∇0,1 near +∞.\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rr}(\nabla-B_{H,\underline{\smash{p}}}\otimes dt)_{J}^{0,1}\text{ near }-\infty\\
\nabla^{0,1}\text{ near }+\infty.\end{array}\right.
It is a standard fact that ∇p¯0,1\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}} defines an elliptic operator. Therefore, we can find a finite dimensional complex vector space Vp¯−V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-} and a linear map λp¯:Vp¯−→Ω0,1​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}:V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\to\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM) such that the map
∇p¯0,1⊕λp¯:Ω0​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)⊕Vp¯−→Ω0,1​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}:\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\to\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)
is surjective. This map is called the asymptotic operator of p¯\underline{\smash{p}}. We fix a choice of Vp¯−V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-} and λp¯\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}, and denote the kernel of the above map by Vp¯+V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}. The virtual vector space (Vp¯+,Vp¯−)(V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+},V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}) will be used to construct a stable complex structure on Tvt​Vp​qT^{\rm vt}V_{pq}.
Lemma 6.23.
For any p,q∈TFloerp,q\in T^{\rm Floer} such that ℳ¯Floerp​q≠∅\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\neq\emptyset, there exists a complex vector bundle Ip​qvt→Vp​qI^{\rm vt}_{pq}\to V_{pq} such that there exists an homotopy between the vector bundles
Vq¯+⊕Tvt​Vp​q⊕Vp¯−≅Vq¯−⊕Ip​qvt⊕Vp¯+.V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{+}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\cong V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-}\oplus I^{\rm vt}_{pq}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}.
(6.4)
Proof.
We explain the construction in the case of a single-layered thickening. The vertical tangent bundle of Vp​qV_{pq} can be identified with the kernel of the linearized operator associated with (5.24) with the framing FF being kept fixed. To be more precise, using the model as in the proof of Theorem 5.41, given an element in Vp​qV_{pq} with representative (Σ,u,μ,η)(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta), the deformation operator over each component Σα\Sigma_{\alpha} is of the form
W1,p​(Σα,u∗​T​M)⊕W1,p​(Σα,(u,μ)∗​E​(k))→Lp​(Λ0,1​(Σα,u∗​T​M))⊕Lp​(Λ0,1​(Σα,u∗​T​M))\displaystyle W^{1,p}(\Sigma_{\alpha},u^{*}TM)\oplus W^{1,p}(\Sigma_{\alpha},(u,\mu)^{*}E(k))\to L^{p}(\Lambda^{0,1}(\Sigma_{\alpha},u^{*}TM))\oplus L^{p}(\Lambda^{0,1}(\Sigma_{\alpha},u^{*}TM))
(ξ,η′)↦(Du​ξ+P⁡(η′),DE​η′).\displaystyle(\xi,\eta^{\prime})\mapsto(D_{u}\xi+P(\eta^{\prime}),D_{E}\eta^{\prime}).
By taking the direct sum of these linear operators ranging over all the components of Σ\Sigma, and impose the usual matching condition in the case of nodal curves, we indeed identify Tvt​Vp​qT^{\rm vt}V_{pq} over (Σ,u,μ,η)(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta) with the kernel of a Fredholm operator which is schematically written as
Du,μ,ηvt:ℰu,μ,η→ℱu,μ,η.D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}:{\mathcal{E}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\mu,\eta}.
Now we consider the concatenation of the three operators Du,μ,ηvt⊕∇q¯0,1⊕λq¯⊕0D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus 0
ℰu,μ,η⊕(Ω0​((q¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)⊕Vq¯−)⊕Vp¯−→ℱu,μ,η⊕Ω0,1​((q¯∘prS1)∗​T​M).{\mathcal{E}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus(\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{q}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-})\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{q}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM).
This operator is Fredholm and surjective, with kernel identified with
Tvt​Vp​q⊕Vq¯+⊕Vp¯−.T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus V^{+}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}.
We will construct a homotopy of this operator following the proof of [AB21, Proposition 11.30]. To this end, introduce an auxialiary moduli space V~p​q→Vp​q\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}. It is described by adding an additional marked point on the compactified lateral line ℝ∪{±∞}≅[0,1]{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{\pm\infty\}\cong[0,1] of the cylindrical components and the map V~p​q→Vp​q\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq} is the associated forgetful map. The fiber of the universal family over V~p​q\tilde{V}_{pq} is the same as the fiber of Vp​qV_{pq} when the newly-added marked point ≠±∞\neq\pm\infty; when it is given by ±∞\pm\infty, we insert a cylinder between the consecutive two cylindrical components which is mapped to the common asymptotic orbit. In particular, V~p​q→Vp​q\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq} is a fiber bundle with fiber ≅[0,1]\cong[0,1]. For an element (Σ,u,μ,η)∈Vp​q(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)\in V_{pq}, the domain of the universal family over (Σ,u,μ,η)×{1}(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)\times\{1\} coincides with the domain of Du,μ,ηvt⊕∇q¯0,1⊕λq¯⊕0D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus 0.
Write
Du,μ,ηvt=Du,μ,ηvt,ℂ+Yu,μ,ηvtD^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}=D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}+Y^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}
where Du,μ,ηvt,ℂD^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta} is a complex linear Cauchy–Riemann operator and Yu,μ,ηvtY^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta} is a 00-th order differential operator which is complex anti-linear. Then we can consider another operator Du,μ,ηvt,ℂ⊕∇p¯0,1⊕λp¯⊕0D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus 0
ℰu,μ,η⊕(Ω0​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M)⊕Vp¯−)⊕Vq¯−→ℱu,μ,η⊕Ω0,1​((p¯∘prS1)∗​T​M).{\mathcal{E}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus(\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-})\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM).
If this Fredholm operator is surjective, then there exists a finite dimensional complex vector space (Ip​qvt)u,μ,η(I^{\rm vt}_{pq})_{u,\mu,\eta} such that the kernel of this operator is
(Ip​qvt)u,μ,η⊕Vp¯+⊕Vq¯−.(I^{\rm vt}_{pq})_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-}.
Note that using the position of the additional marked point on the lateral line V~p​q→Vp​q\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq} and a cut-off function defined on the domains of the universal family over V~p​q\tilde{V}_{pq} whose differential has support on the horizontal component where the marked point lives on, we can construct a homotopy between two Fredholm operators
Du,μ,ηvt⊕∇q¯0,1⊕λq¯⊕0​ and ​Du,μ,ηvt,ℂ⊕∇p¯0,1⊕λp¯⊕0D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus 0\text{ and }D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus 0
which varies smoothly as we vary (Σ,u,μ,η)(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta) along the fiber of Vp​q→Bp​qV_{pq}\to B_{pq}.
Moreover, Du,μ,ηvt,ℂD^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta} can be made surjective if the kk as from E⁡(k)E(k) is chosen to be large enough. This fact can be derived from exactly the same proof of [AMS21, Proposition 6.26]. Therefore the statement is proved by defining Ip​qvtI^{\rm vt}_{pq} to be the vector bundle over Vp​qV_{pq} by assembling (Ip​qvt)u,μ,η(I^{\rm vt}_{pq})_{u,\mu,\eta}.
∎
Corollary 6.24.
If the almost free action of Gp​qG_{pq} on Vp​qV_{pq} is smooth, then the induced orbifold vector bundle Tvt​Vp​qT^{\rm vt}V_{pq} on the quotient orbifold Vp​q/Gp​qV_{pq}/G_{pq} has a normal complex structure.
Proof.
This follows from Lemma 6.23 and the follwing fact. For p∈𝒫p\in{\mathcal{P}} (also for q∈𝒫q\in{\mathcal{P}}), the trivial vector bundles Vp¯+×Vp​q→Vp​qV_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}\times V_{pq}\to V_{pq} and Vp¯−×Vp​q→Vp​qV_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\times V_{pq}\to V_{pq} can be viewed as Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant vectors for which Gp​qG_{pq} acts trivially on the factor Vp¯±V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{\pm}. In particular, we can use Vp¯±V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{\pm} to stabilize the space Vp​qV_{pq}. Because the induced Gp​qG_{pq}-action on Vp¯±×Vp​q→Vp​qV_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{\pm}\times V_{pq}\to V_{pq} is trivial on the first factor, they do not affect the normal directions to the union of Gp​qG_{pq}-orbits with the same isotropy group of both the tangent bundle of Vp​qV_{pq}. Therefore the statement follows from taking the nontrivial part of the representations of (6.4) after taking the Gp​qG_{pq}-quotient.
∎
6.2.3. Stabilizing the K-chart lift
In the equivariant smoothing theorem of Lashof [Las79] a smooth structure can only be obtained after stabilizing the manifold. Given a GG-manifold MM, a stabilization of MM is M×𝑹M\times{\bm{R}} where 𝑹{\bm{R}} is an orthogonal GG-space.1818
18
We could try to extend the theory to the case of nontrivial bundles but that is not completely necessary. This operation fits into the stabilization of Kuranishi charts. As we are dealing with infinitely many Kuranishi charts associated to a K-chart lift of the Floer category TFloerT^{\rm Floer} (or its outer-collaring), once we stabilize certain charts (and their products) and equipped them with smooth structures, a “larger” chart needs to be stabilized by a larger representation in order to include the stabilization of products corresponding to its strata. Thus we need to introduce the following concept which describes how a system of stabilizations should fit together.
Definition 6.25.
Let T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} be a flow category over 𝒫{\mathcal{P}} and suppose the collection {Kp​q=(Gp​q,Vp​q,Ep​q,Sp​q)}p<q\{K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})\}_{p<q} of Kuranishi charts with the collection {𝜾p​r​q}p<r<q\{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}\}_{p<r<q} of K-chart embeddings define a K-chart lift of T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} (see Definition 5.9). A stabilization of consists of the following objects.
(1)
For each Tp​qT_{pq} a finite-dimensional orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-representation 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq}. Denote by Rp​qR_{pq} the trivial Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant vector bundle Vp​q×𝑹p​qV_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}. Then for each stratum α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q, define the GαG_{\alpha}-equivariant vector bundle RαR_{\alpha} over Vα=Vp​r1×⋯Vrl​qV_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots V_{r_{l}q} to be
Rp​r1⊞⋯⊞Rrl​q.R_{pr_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus R_{r_{l}q}.
(6.5)
(2)
For any pair of strata α≤β\alpha\leq\beta, an equivariant bundle embedding
Rα ϕβ​α Rβ|∂αVβ Vα ∂αVβ .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.87715pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.68445pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.87715pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 39.9368pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.9368pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.
Assume that these data satisfy the following compatibility condition.
(A)
For each triple of strata α≤β≤γ\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma one has ϕγ​β∘ϕβ​α=ϕγ​α\phi_{\gamma\beta}\circ\phi_{\beta\alpha}=\phi_{\gamma\alpha}.
(B)
The bundle embeddings are induced from products. Namely, given a stratum α=pr1⋯rlq∈𝔸𝒫p​q\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}, there exist equivariant isometric linear embeddings
ϕri​ri+1:Rri​ri+1↪Rp​q​ for ​i=0,…,l\phi_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}:R_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}\hookrightarrow R_{pq}\text{ for }i=0,\dots,l
with respect to the group embeddings Gri​ri+1↪Gp​qG_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}\hookrightarrow G_{pq} such that
ϕp​q,α=ϕp​q,p​r1×⋯×ϕp​q,rl​q,\phi_{pq,\alpha}=\phi_{pq,pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\phi_{pq,r_{l}q},
and ϕβ​α\phi_{\beta\alpha} for a general pair β≤α\beta\leq\alpha is constructed similarly using the corresponding factorization.
Given a stabilization containing {𝑹p​q}\{{\bm{R}}_{pq}\} and {ϕβ​α}\{\phi_{\beta\alpha}\} we can define another K-chart lift for T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}}. Its collection of Kuranishi charts are
K^p​q:=(Gp​q,V^p​q,E^p​q,S^p​q):=StabRp​q​(Kp​q);\widehat{K}_{pq}:=(G_{pq},\widehat{V}_{pq},\widehat{E}_{pq},\widehat{S}_{pq}):={\rm Stab}_{R_{pq}}(K_{pq});
its collection of chart embeddings
𝜾^p​r​q:K^p​r×K^r​q→∂p​r​qK^p​q\widehat{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:\widehat{K}_{pr}\times\widehat{K}_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}\widehat{K}_{pq}
are defined as follows. Given (xp​r,ep​r)∈V^p​r(x_{pr},e_{pr})\in\widehat{V}_{pr}, (xr​q,er​q)∈V^r​q(x_{rq},e_{rq})\in\widehat{V}_{rq}, define
ι^p​r​q​((xp​r,ep​r),(xr​q,er​q))=(ιp​r​q​(xp​r,xr​q),ϕp​q,p​r​q​(ep​r,er​q))\widehat{\iota}_{prq}((x_{pr},e_{pr}),(x_{rq},e_{rq}))=(\iota_{prq}(x_{pr},x_{rq}),\phi_{pq,prq}(e_{pr},e_{rq}))
which is clearly equivariant with respect to the group map Gp​r×Gr​q→Gp​qG_{pr}\times G_{rq}\to G_{pq}.
Lemma 6.26.
The data {K^p​q}p<q\{\widehat{K}_{pq}\}_{p<q} with {𝛊^p​r​q}p<r<q\{\widehat{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}\}_{p<r<q} is a K-chart lift of T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}}.
Proof.
This follows directly from the definition.
∎
We need to equip a stabilization certain extra structures.
Definition 6.27.
Suppose the K-chart lift 𝔎𝒫{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathcal{P}} is equipped with a collar structure. Then a stabilization is called collared if there are equivariant bundle isomorphisms
Rp​q|∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α\textstyle{R_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Rp​q\textstyle{R_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}∂αVp​q×[0,ϵ)𝔽α\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Vp​q\textstyle{V_{pq}}
such that the bundle embeddings are collared, i.e.,
Rβ|∂αVβ×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β Rγ|∂αVγ×[0,ϵ)𝔽α∖𝔽β Rβ Rγ|∂βVγ .\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 49.48822pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-49.48822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 73.48822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
