102.17462pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\gamma}|_{\partial^{\beta}V_{\gamma}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.
Note that although our stabilization is constructed using product bundles, the stabilization maps are not necessarily induced from the linear maps between the representations. Therefore, the above definition is not entirely superfluous.
We will also use not-necessarily-trivial inner products on those trivial bundles Rp​qR_{pq} in the construction.
Definition 6.28.
Let 𝔎𝒫{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathcal{P}} be a K-chart lift of T𝒫T^{\mathcal{P}} equipped with a collar structure and a collared stabilization ℜ{\mathfrak{R}}. A collared system of inner products on ℜ{\mathfrak{R}} is a collection of Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant inner products on the bundle Rp​qR_{pq} such that
(1)
All the embeddings ϕβ​α:Rα→Rβ|∂αVβ\phi_{\beta\alpha}:R_{\alpha}\to R_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}} are isometric.
(2)
The collar isomorphisms are isometric.
Suppose ℜ{\mathfrak{R}} is equipped with a collared system of inner products. Then define
Rβ​α⊂Rβ|VαR_{\beta\alpha}\subset R_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}
to be the orthogonal complement of RαR_{\alpha} in Rβ|VαR_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}. Then by basic linear algebra one can see that whenever α≤β≤γ\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma, as subbundles of Rγ|VαR_{\gamma}|_{V_{\alpha}}, one has
Rγ​α=Rγ​β|Vα⊕Rβ​α.R_{\gamma\alpha}=R_{\gamma\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus R_{\beta\alpha}.
Then the original scaffolding can be extended to include the extra pieces Rβ​αR_{\beta\alpha}. Indeed, define
F^β​α:=π∗​Fβ​α⊕π∗​Rβ​α\widehat{F}_{\beta\alpha}:=\pi^{*}F_{\beta\alpha}\oplus\pi^{*}R_{\beta\alpha}
where π\pi denotes (temporarily) the projection Vp​q×𝑹p​q→Vp​qV_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}\to V_{pq} and the induced projection Vα×𝑹α→VαV_{\alpha}\times{\bm{R}}_{\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}. The stabilization described in Proposition 5.64 is also extended to a map
StabF^p​q,α​(V^α)→∂αV^p​q.{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}.
After taking the equivariantization, we obtain a map
Gp​q×Gα(StabF^p​q,α​(V^α))→V^p​qG_{pq}\times_{G_{\alpha}}({\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha}))\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
which defines an equivariant open embedding. We would like to remark that the orthogonal complement of the Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariantization of Rp​q,α→VαR_{pq,\alpha}\to V_{\alpha} in Rp​q|Gp​q​(Vα)R_{pq}|_{G_{pq}(V_{\alpha})} is not necessarily a product bundle. Therefore, some extra care needs to be taken in the smoothing process.
6.2.4. An induction scheme of stabilization
In the smoothing process, the construction of successive stabilizations is elementary but complicated. In general, the stabilizations are constructed inductively, similar to many other inductive constructions. We would like to describe how the induction can be carried out. We first look at the simple case when a moduli space has strata of codimension at most 2. Assume p<qp<q in 𝒫Floer{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer} and let Vp​qV_{pq} be the domain of a global Kuranishi chart of ℳ¯p​qFloer\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}. In the following discussion, we assume that the tangent microbundle of the underlying thickened moduli space of the relevant K-charts admits a vector bundle lift, which is the necessary condition for us to apply the smoothing theory. Moreover, in the discussion of extending the smoothing relatively, we assume that the lifts are compatible in a suitable sense. The construction of vector bundle lifts and the precise meaning of compatibility will be detailed in Section 6.4.
Note that we also need to endow the vector bundle Ep​q→Vp​qE_{pq}\to V_{pq} with a smooth structure after suitable stabilization. However, this is rather straightforward after smoothing Vp​qV_{pq}: we can simply approximate the classifying map of Ep​qE_{pq} by a smooth map which stays in the same homotopy class.
The case with no boundary or corner. When Vp​qV_{pq} has no boundary or corner, then it is the initial step of the induction. One can find a trivial Gp​qG_{pq}-bundle Vp​q×𝑹p​qV_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq} induced by a suitable representation Rp​qR_{pq} needed for stable smoothing.
The case with only codimension one boundary strata. When Vp​qV_{pq} has only codimension one boundary strata, without loss of generality, suppose there is only one boundary stratum Vp​r​qV_{prq}. Using the induction hypothesis, suppose 𝑹p​r{\bm{R}}_{pr} and 𝑹r​q{\bm{R}}_{rq} have been given. Consider the representation 𝑹p​r{\bm{R}}_{pr} resp. 𝑹r​q{\bm{R}}_{rq} of Gp​rG_{pr} resp. Gr​qG_{rq}. By Frobenius reciprocity (see [Sep07, Theorem 7.47]), there exist orthogonal representations 𝑹p​rp​q{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq} resp. 𝑹r​qp​q{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq} of Gp​qG_{pq} which contains Gp​rG_{pr} resp. Gr​qG_{rq} as subrepresentations of Gp​rG_{pr} resp. Gr​qG_{rq}. Then define
𝑹p​q=𝑹p​rp​q⊕𝑹r​qp​q{\bm{R}}_{pq}={\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}
which is an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-representation.
Now we define the bundle embeddings required for stabilization. We introduce more notations. Over the product Vp​r​q=Vp​r×Vr​qV_{prq}=V_{pr}\times V_{rq}, define
Rp​r→p​q:=Rp​r⊞{0},\displaystyle\ R_{pr\to pq}:=R_{pr}\boxplus\{0\},\
Rr​q→p​q:={0}⊞Rr​q.\displaystyle\ R_{rq\to pq}:=\{0\}\boxplus R_{rq}.
Then
Rp​r​q=Rp​r→p​q⊕Rr​q→p​q.R_{prq}=R_{pr\to pq}\oplus R_{rq\to pq}.
We would like the bundle embedding ϕp​q,p​r​q\phi_{pq,prq} to be the sum of
ϕp​r→p​q:Rp​r→p​q→Rp​q|∂p​r​qVp​q,\displaystyle\ \phi_{pr\to pq}:R_{pr\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}},\
ϕr​q→p​q:Rr​q→p​q→Rp​q|∂p​r​qVp​q.\displaystyle\ \phi_{rq\to pq}:R_{rq\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}.
Indeed, ϕp​r→p​q\phi_{pr\to pq} and ϕr​q→p​q\phi_{rq\to pq} are the ones canonically induced from the linear inclusions
𝑹p​r↪𝑹p​rp​q↪𝑹p​q,\displaystyle\ {\bm{R}}_{pr}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq},\
𝑹r​q↪𝑹r​qp​q↪𝑹p​q.\displaystyle\ {\bm{R}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq}.
Then all the requirements of Definition 6.25 are automatically satisfied. In addition, for the purpose of stable smoothing, one can also take an additional direct sum to 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} by another orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-space 𝑹p​q,0{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}.
Note that in this final step, we need to apply the relative smoothing result Theorem 6.10. Indeed, the space Rp​r​qR_{prq} admits a smooth structure by taking the product of smooth structures on Rp​rR_{pr} and Rr​qR_{rq}, whose existence is based on the induction hypothesis. Then the equivariantization Gp​q​(Rp​r​q)G_{pq}(R_{prq}) has a smooth structure. Denote by Gp​q​(Rp​r​q⟂)G_{pq}(R_{prq}^{\perp}) the equivariantization of the orthogonal complement of Rp​r​qR_{prq} in Rp​q|Vp​r​qR_{pq}|_{V_{prq}}. Using the projection map Rp​r​q→Vp​r​qR_{prq}\to V_{prq}, the vector bundle Gp​q​(Rp​r​q⟂)G_{pq}(R_{prq}^{\perp}) can be pulled back to Gp​q​(Rp​r​q)G_{pq}(R_{prq}) and the total space of this vector bundle can be endowed with a smooth structure, by approximating the classifying map. This in turn equips Rp​q|∂p​r​qVp​qR_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}} with a smooth structure. Using the collar structure
∂p​r​qVp​q×[0,ϵ)→Vp​q\partial^{prq}V_{pq}\times[0,\epsilon)\to V_{pq}
which extends to a collar structure of the stabilized charts, we see that an open neighborhood of Rp​q|∂p​r​qVp​qR_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}} has a smooth structure using the product decomposition
Rp​q|∂p​r​qVp​q×[0,ϵ).R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon).
By replacing the open interval [0,ϵ)[0,\epsilon) by a closed interval of the form [0,ϵ2][0,\frac{\epsilon}{2}], a suitable compatibility between the vector bundle lifts guarantees that we can apply Theorem 6.10 to obtain the Gp​qG_{pq} orthogonal representation 𝑹p​q,0{\bm{R}}_{pq,0} which induces a stabilized smoothing for Vp​qV_{pq} extending the previously constructed stabilized smoothings on Vp​rV_{pr} and Vr​qV_{rq}.
Remark 6.29.
We hope that the above arguments showcase the importance of applying Frobenius reciprocity, approximations of classifying maps of equivariant vector bundles, and the relative smoothing result in our inductive construction. Moreover, as we can see from above, outer-collaring conveniently provide us with an automatic smoothing near the boundary stratum. Additionally, it is important to keep in mind that the compatibility between the vector bundle lifts of tangent microbundles of the total space and boundary stratum is crucial for the application of Theorem 6.10.
The case with only codimension one or two strata. Now we consider the case when 𝔸p​q𝒫={p​q,p​r​q,p​s​q,p​r​s​q}{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}=\{pq,prq,psq,prsq\}. Suppose the representations
𝑹p​r,𝑹r​s,𝑹s​q,𝑹p​s,𝑹r​q{\bm{R}}_{pr},\ {\bm{R}}_{rs},\ {\bm{R}}_{sq},{\bm{R}}_{ps},{\bm{R}}_{rq}
are chosen so that they induce compatible stabilized smoothings except the top stratum Vp​qV_{pq}. Define the bundles Rp​r→p​qR_{pr\to pq} etc. in a way similar to the previous case. Suppose we have also defined bundle embeddings
ϕp​r→p​s,ϕr​s→p​s,ϕr​s→r​q,ϕs​q→r​q.\phi_{pr\to ps},\ \phi_{rs\to ps},\ \phi_{rs\to rq},\ \phi_{sq\to rq}.
In addition, we make the following assumptions, which basically says that for p​sps and r​qrq, the stabilization data are constructed as in the previous case.
(1)
𝑹p​s{\bm{R}}_{ps} is the direct sum
𝑹p​s=𝑹p​rp​s⊕𝑹r​sp​s⊕𝑹p​s,0{\bm{R}}_{ps}={\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{ps}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{ps,0}
of orthogonal Gp​sG_{ps}-spaces such that 𝑹p​rp​s{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps} resp. 𝑹r​sp​s{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{ps} contains 𝑹p​r{\bm{R}}_{pr} resp. 𝑹r​s{\bm{R}}_{rs} as subrepresentations of Gp​rG_{pr} resp. Gr​sG_{rs}. Similarly, there is a direct sum
𝑹r​q=𝑹r​sr​q⊕𝑹s​qr​q⊕𝑹r​q,0{\bm{R}}_{rq}={\bm{R}}_{rs}^{rq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{rq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rq,0}
of orthogonal Gr​qG_{rq}-spaces such that 𝑹r​sr​q{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{rq} resp. 𝑹s​qr​q{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{rq} contains 𝑹r​s{\bm{R}}_{rs} resp. 𝑹s​q{\bm{R}}_{sq} as subrepresentations of Gr​sG_{rs} resp. Gs​qG_{sq}.
(2)
The bundle embedding
ϕp​r→p​s:Rp​r→p​s→Rp​s|∂p​r​sVp​s\phi_{pr\to ps}:R_{pr\to ps}\to R_{ps}|_{\partial^{prs}V_{ps}}
is induced from the linear inclusion 𝑹p​r↪𝑹p​rp​s↪𝑹p​s{\bm{R}}_{pr}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ps}. Similar requirement applies to other bundle embeddings.
Now we define an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-space 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq}. There are two codimension one strata, p​r​qprq and p​s​qpsq. For the Gp​rG_{pr}-space 𝑹p​r{\bm{R}}_{pr}, by Frobenius reciprocity, there is a Gp​qG_{pq}-orthogonal space 𝑹p​rp​q{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq} which contains 𝑹p​r{\bm{R}}_{pr} as a subrepresentation of Gp​rG_{pr}. We choose similarly 𝑹r​qp​q{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}, 𝑹p​sp​q{\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}, 𝑹s​qp​q{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{pq}. For the purpose of stable smoothing, we choose another Gp​qG_{pq}-orthogonal space 𝑹p​q,0{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}, which is constructed similarly as the case of one boundary stratum by applying relative smoothing. Then define
𝑹p​q:=(𝑹p​rp​q⊕𝑹r​qp​q)⊕(𝑹p​sp​q⊕𝑹s​qp​q)⊕𝑹p​q,0.{\bm{R}}_{pq}:=\Big({\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}\Big)\oplus\Big({\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{pq}\Big)\oplus{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}.
We rewrite this decomposition as
𝑹p​q=𝑹p​q,r⊕𝑹p​q,s⊕𝑹p​q,0.{\bm{R}}_{pq}={\bm{R}}_{pq,r}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{pq,s}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}.
(6.6)
Then we want to define the bundle embeddings into
Rp​q:=Rp​q,r⊕Rp​q,s⊕Rp​q,0.R_{pq}:=R_{pq,r}\oplus R_{pq,s}\oplus R_{pq,0}.
This time the bundle embeddings are not purely induced from linear maps between the representations.
(1)
We define ϕp​r→p​q\phi_{pr\to pq} and ϕs​q→p​q\phi_{sq\to pq}. We only describe ϕp​r→p​q\phi_{pr\to pq} in detail. With respect to (6.6), we write
ϕp​r→p​q=ϕp​r,r⊕ϕp​r,s⊕0.\phi_{pr\to pq}=\phi_{pr,r}\oplus\phi_{pr,s}\oplus 0.
We define ϕp​r,r:Rp​r→p​q→Rp​q,r\phi_{pr,r}:R_{pr\to pq}\to R_{pq,r} to be the one induced from the linear inclusion
𝑹p​r↪𝑹p​rp​q↪𝑹p​q,r.{\bm{R}}_{pr}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,r}.
For ϕp​r,s:Rp​r→p​q→Rp​q,s\phi_{pr,s}:R_{pr\to pq}\to R_{pq,s}, notice that it is defined over Vp​r​qV_{prq}. We first define it over ∂p​r​s​qVp​r​q\partial^{prsq}V_{prq}, which is induced from the existing bundle map
ϕp​r→p​s:Rp​r→p​s→Rp​s\phi_{pr\to ps}:R_{pr\to ps}\to R_{ps}
and its equivariantization and stabilization (by the bundle associated to the scaffolding), composed with the linear inclusion
Rp​s↪Rp​sp​q↪Rp​q,s.R_{ps}\hookrightarrow R_{ps}^{pq}\hookrightarrow R_{pq,s}.
To extend to the whole Vp​r​qV_{prq}, we use the collar structure and a cut-off function on Vp​r​qV_{prq} which is supported in the collar region near ∂p​r​s​qVp​r​q\partial^{prsq}V_{prq} and which only depends on the collar coordinate.
(2)
We define ϕr​s→p​q\phi_{rs\to pq}. Again, we write ϕr​s→p​q=ϕr​s,r⊕ϕr​s,s⊕0\phi_{rs\to pq}=\phi_{rs,r}\oplus\phi_{rs,s}\oplus 0. The first component ϕr​s,r\phi_{rs,r} is induced from the bundle map
ϕr​s→r​q:Rr​s→r​q→Rr​q\phi_{rs\to rq}:R_{rs\to rq}\to R_{rq}
composed with the linear inclusion 𝑹r​q↪𝑹r​qp​q↪𝑹p​q,r{\bm{R}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,r}; the second component ϕr​s,s\phi_{rs,s} is induced from the bundle map
ϕr​s→p​s:Rr​s→p​s→Rp​s\phi_{rs\to ps}:R_{rs\to ps}\to R_{ps}
composed with the linear inclusion 𝑹p​s↪𝑹p​sp​q↪𝑹p​q,s{\bm{R}}_{ps}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,s}.
(3)
The definitions of ϕp​s→p​q\phi_{ps\to pq} and ϕr​q→p​q\phi_{rq\to pq} is similar to the case of ϕp​r→p​q\phi_{pr\to pq} and ϕs​q→p​q\phi_{sq\to pq}. We only describe in detail the bundle map ϕp​s→p​q\phi_{ps\to pq} (which should be defined over Vp​s​qV_{psq}). We write ϕp​s→p​q\phi_{ps\to pq} as ϕp​s,r⊕ϕp​s,s⊕0\phi_{ps,r}\oplus\phi_{ps,s}\oplus 0. The second component ϕp​s,s\phi_{ps,s} is naturally induced from the linear inclusion 𝑹p​s↪𝑹p​sp​q↪𝑹p​q,s{\bm{R}}_{ps}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,s}, For the first component ϕp​s,r\phi_{ps,r}, we decompose it further as
ϕp​s,r=ϕp​s,p​r⊕ϕp​s,r​s.\phi_{ps,r}=\phi_{ps,pr}\oplus\phi_{ps,rs}.
We first define it over the boundary stratum ∂p​r​s​qVp​s​q\partial^{prsq}V_{psq} and then use a cut-off function to turn it off as we leave this stratum towards the interior of Vp​s​qV_{psq}. Consider the composition of orthogonal projections
𝑹p​s→𝑹p​rp​s⊕𝑹r​sp​s→𝑹p​r⊕𝑹r​s{\bm{R}}_{ps}\to{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{ps}\to{\bm{R}}_{pr}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs}
which induces a bundle map
Rp​s→p​q|Vp​r​s​q→Rp​r→p​q|Vp​r​s​q⊕Rr​s→p​q.R_{ps\to pq}|_{V_{prsq}}\to R_{pr\to pq}|_{V_{prsq}}\oplus R_{rs\to pq}.
Then using the linear inclusions 𝑹p​r→𝑹p​rp​q{\bm{R}}_{pr}\to{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq} and the map Rr​s→r​q→Rr​q→Rr​qp​qR_{rs\to rq}\to R_{rq}\to R_{rq}^{pq}, one defines the map ϕp​s,r\phi_{ps,r} over the closed set Vp​r​s​qV_{prsq}. One can then equivariantize, stabilize to define it over ∂p​r​s​qVp​s​q\partial^{prsq}V_{psq}. Lastly, use a cut-off function to extend this component to a neighborhood.
Remark 6.30.
We put effort on defining the embeddings maps between charts in the above discussions, which contains certain distinct features than the case with fewer strata because the bundle maps are no longer linear. We would like to remark that the compatibility between the stabilized smoothings of Vp​r​qV_{prq} and Vp​s​qV_{psq} near their common stratum Vp​r​s​qV_{prsq} is already guaranteed by the inductive nature of our construction.
The above inductive strategy, especially the case with codimension two strata, indicates that it seems difficult to construct a stabilization of K-chart lifts of a flow category using purely linear maps between representations. This brings in an extra layer of complexity in the following smoothing process because we would like the bundle embeddings also to be smooth.
6.3. The main theorem about smoothing
We state the main theorem on smoothing the outer-collared K-chart lift of (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}. In the following, a smoothing on a 𝔸{\mathbb{A}}-manifold VV equipped with a collar structure (Definition 3.26) is called a collared smoothing if the smoothing is equal to the product of the standard smooth structure on [0,ϵ)𝔽α[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}} and a smoothing on ∂αV\partial^{\alpha}V over the collar region ∂αV×[0,ϵ)𝔽α\partial^{\alpha}V\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}. The same notion is also used for a smoothing of a vector bundle.
Theorem 6.31.
Given the outer-collared K-chart lift 𝔎+{\mathfrak{K}}^{+} of the outer-collared flow category (TFloer)+(T^{{\rm Floer}})^{+} equipped with a collared scaffolding (see Proposition 5.64), there exist the following objects. We revome the “++” to make the notations more succinct.
(1)
A stabilization of 𝔎{\mathfrak{K}} (see Definition 6.25), given by a collection of orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-spaces 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} and a collection of bundle embeddings.
(2)
A collared Gp​qG_{pq}-smoothing on V^p​q:=Vp​q×𝑹p​q\widehat{V}_{pq}:=V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}.
(3)
A collared bundle Gp​qG_{pq}-smoothing on the O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle O^p​q→V^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}, where O^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq} is the pullback of Op​qO_{pq} under the natural projection V^p​q→Vp​q\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}.
(4)
A collared Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant inner product on the trivial bundle Rp​q→Vp​qR_{pq}\to V_{pq} inducing an inner product on the vector bundle R^p​q→V^p​q\widehat{R}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}, where R^p​q\widehat{R}_{pq} is the pullback of Rp​qR_{pq} under the natural projection V^p​q→Vp​q\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}.
(5)
A collared Gp​qG_{pq}-smoothing on the O⁡(n)O(n)-bundles R^p​q\widehat{R}_{pq}.
These objects satisfy the following conditions.
(A)
The smoothing of V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq} induces a smooth bundle structure Q^p​q\widehat{Q}_{pq} as it is induced from the Gp​qG_{pq}-representation 𝑸p​q{\bm{Q}}_{pq}. Then the smooth structures on O^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq} and R^p​q\widehat{R}_{pq} induce a smooth structure on the obstruction bundle
E^p​q=O^p​q⊕Q^p​q⊕R^p​q.\widehat{E}_{pq}=\widehat{O}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq}.
(B)
Boundary smoothings are given by products and stabilizations. More precisely, this means the following. For any stratum α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q of 𝔸p​qFloer{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}, the smoothings on V^p​r1\widehat{V}_{pr_{1}}, …\ldots, V^rl​q\widehat{V}_{r_{l}q} induce a smooth structure on the prodcut V^α=V^p​r1×⋯×V^rl​q\widehat{V}_{\alpha}=\widehat{V}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\widehat{V}_{r_{l}q} and its equivariantization V^α∼\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}; the smooth bundle structures on O^p​r1\widehat{O}_{pr_{1}}, …\ldots, O^rl​q\widehat{O}_{r_{l}q} induce a smooth structure on the bundle O^α∼→V^α∼\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{\sim}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}; there is also a canonical smooth bundle structure on Rα∼R_{\alpha}^{\sim} as it is induced from trivial bundles. Then
(a)
The embedding V^α↪∂αV^p​q\widehat{V}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq} is smooth.
(b)
The bundle embedding O^α↪O^p​q|∂αV^p​q\widehat{O}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}} and the bundle embedding R^α↪R^p​q|∂αV^p​q\widehat{R}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\widehat{R}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}} are smooth bundle embeddings. It follows that the orthogonal complement O^p​q,α\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha} and the orthogonal complement R^p​q,α\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha} are smooth bundles over V^α\widehat{V}_{\alpha}. It follows that the bundle
F^p​q,α=O^p​q,α⊕Q^p​q,α⊕R^p​q,α→V^α\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}=\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
is smooth, where the smooth structure on Q^p​q,α\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha} is induced from the orthogonal complement of the Gp​qG_{pq}-subrepresentation 𝑸α↪𝑸p​q{\bm{Q}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow{\bm{Q}}_{pq}.
(c)
The stabilization map
θ^p​q,α:StabF^p​q,α​(V^α)→∂αVp​q\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}\Big(\widehat{V}_{\alpha}\Big)\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
(which is the θp​q,α\theta_{pq,\alpha} in (5.50) lifted) is a smooth embedding.
(d)
Let π^p​q,α:∂αV^p​q→V^α∼\widehat{\pi}_{pq,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim} be the projection map induced from the stabilization map above, which is smooth. Then bundle isomorphism
ϑ^p​q,α:π^p​q,α∗​(O^p​q|V^α)→O^p​q|∂αV^p​q\widehat{\vartheta}_{pq,\alpha}:\widehat{\pi}_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\Big(\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\Big)\to\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
(included in the scaffolding) is smooth.
Assuming this theorem, it is straightforward to construct a derived orbifold lift of the flow category (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+} by taking group quotients. Remove the ⋅^\widehat{\cdot} from the notation. Define
Cp​q=(𝒰p​q,ℰp​q,𝒮p​q,ψp​q)C_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq})
where 𝒰p​q=Vp​q/Gp​q{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}=V_{pq}/G_{pq}, ℰp​q=Ep​q/Gp​q{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}=E_{pq}/G_{pq}, 𝒮p​q:𝒰p​q→ℰp​q{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq} is the induced orbibundle section, and ψp​q:𝒮p​q−1(0)→ℳ¯+p​q\psi_{pq}:{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{-1}(0)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{+} is the induced footprint map. Moreover, for α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q, one has the natural identification
Kα/Gα≅Cα:=Cp​r1×⋯×Crl​q.K_{\alpha}/G_{\alpha}\cong C_{\alpha}:=C_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{r_{l}q}.
The scaffolding in the K-chart sense also descends to a scaffolding in the D-chart sense. Indeed, for α,β∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer} with α≤β\alpha\leq\beta, the bundle
ℱβ​α:=Fβ​α/Gα→𝒰α{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}:=F_{\beta\alpha}/G_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
is a smooth orbifold vector bundle over 𝒰α{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}. Then the stabilization map StabFβ​α​(Vα)→∂αVβ{\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta}\alpha}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta} induces
𝜽β​α:Stabℱβ​α​(Cα)→∂αCβ.{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}.
One checkes directly that the data (ℱβ​α,𝜽β​α)α≤β({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha})_{\alpha\leq\beta} form a scaffolding in the D-presentation of ℳ¯p​q+\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{+}. It should be pointed out that these derived orbifold charts are effective due to the geometric construction.
Theorem 6.32.
The D-chart lift of (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+} induced from {Cp​q}\{C_{pq}\} equipped with the compatible collar structure and scaffolding can be upgraded to an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift by doing a further stabilization of the corresponding K-chart lift.
Proof.
In the case of a single chart (𝒰p​q,ℰp​q,𝒮p​q,ψp​q)({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq}), recall that 𝒰p​q{\mathcal{U}}_{pq} is obtained from a quotient V^p​q/Gp​q\widehat{V}_{pq}/G_{pq}, such that
(1)
The space V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq} is equal to the product Vp​q×𝑹p​qV_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}.
(2)
There is a Cloc1C^{1}_{\rm loc} Gp​qG_{pq}-bundle structure πp​q:Vp​q→Bp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq} which ensures that the tangent space of the smoothing of V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq} is isotopic to the direct sum
Rp​q⊕Tvt​Vp​q⊕π∗​T​Bp​q,R_{pq}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus\pi^{*}TB_{pq},
(6.7)
where we abuse the notation to denote the corresponding bundles obtained by the pullback under the natural projection map V^p​q→Vp​q\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}.
The first simple obervation is that the stabilizing factors 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} can be taken to be unitary representations. We can simply use 𝑹p​q⊕𝒊​𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq}\oplus{\bm{i}}{\bm{R}}_{pq} instead of a possibly real representation 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq}. Moreover, the bundle inclusions Rα⊕𝒊​Rα↪Rp​q⊕𝒊​Rp​qR_{\alpha}\oplus{\bm{i}}R_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow R_{pq}\oplus{\bm{i}}R_{pq} are then complex linear and the inner products on the trivial bundles Rp​q⊕𝒊​Rp​qR_{pq}\oplus{\bm{i}}R_{pq} become Hermitian.
We would like to do a further stabilization to the (already smoothed) K-chart lift. Indeed, we stabilize by the Gp​qG_{pq}-representation 𝑸dp​q{\bm{Q}}_{d_{pq}}. Let Q^p​q′→V^p​q\widehat{Q}_{pq}^{\prime}\to\widehat{V}_{pq} be the trivial bundle (to be distinguished from the original QQ-bundle). Notice that the bundle inclusions Qδ↪QdQ_{\delta}\hookrightarrow Q_{d} over Bδ⊂BdB_{\delta}\subset B_{d} for all δ∈𝔸d\delta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{d} is defined via a linear inclusion 𝑸δ→𝑸d{\bm{Q}}_{\delta}\to{\bm{Q}}_{d} the corresponding bundle. Then there are natural bundle inclusions
Q^α′→Q^p​q′|V^α.\widehat{Q}_{\alpha}^{\prime}\to\widehat{Q}_{pq}^{\prime}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.
As this bundle map is induced from linear map between representation spaces, it is automatically smooth. Hence we obtained another smooth K-chart lift whose K-charts are
K^^p​q=StabQ^p​q′​K^p​q.\widehat{\widehat{K}}_{pq}={\rm Stab}_{\widehat{Q}_{pq}^{\prime}}\widehat{K}_{pq}.
It induces a new D-chart lift.
Now we consider the normal complex structures on this new D-chart lift. Indeed, the obstruction bundles are now all complex. Indeed,
E^^p​q=O^^p​q⊕R^^p​q⊕Q^^p​q⊕Q^^p​q′\widehat{\widehat{E}}_{pq}=\widehat{\widehat{O}}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{R}}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{Q}}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{Q}}{}_{pq}^{\prime}
and we give the last two summands the natural complex structure.
Now the quotient orbifold
V^^p​q/Gp​q\widehat{\widehat{V}}_{pq}/G_{pq}
is normally complex. Indeed, (6.7) becomes
TV^^p​q≅(Rp​q⊗ℂ)⊕TvtVp​q⊕(πp​q∗TBdp​q⊕Q^^)′dp​q.T\widehat{\widehat{V}}_{pq}\cong\big(R_{pq}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\big)\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus(\pi_{pq}^{*}TB_{d_{pq}}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{Q}}{}_{d_{pq}}^{\prime}).
As shown in Proposition 5.27, the quotient of the last summand above is normally complex. Moreover, by Lemma 6.23 and Corollary 6.24, the quotient of Tvt​Vp​qT^{\rm vt}V_{pq} is also normally complex.
To obtain a normal complex structure on the D-chart lift, we further check:
(1)
A complex structure on the scaffolding. Indeed, the original difference bundles
F^p​q,α=O^p​q,α⊕Q^p​q,α⊕R^p​q,α\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}=\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}
is already complex as explained below. First, the bundle O^p​q,α\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha} is complex because we can choose the metric on the OO-bundles to be Hermitian. The factor Qp​q,αQ_{pq,\alpha} is complex because it consists of “off-diagonal” Hermitian matrices. As mentioned above, the stabilization in the smoothing can be chosen to be complex representations, therefore R^p​q,α\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha} also has a complex structure. Then the further stabilization by 𝑸dp​q{\bm{Q}}_{d_{pq}} gives an extra copy of Q^p​q,α\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha} which is complex as well.
(2)
Compatibility between the information on the product boundary charts and the information on the restriction of the chart to the boundary. This follows from the constructions [AB21, Section 11] by incorporating the geometry of the broken trajectories from the thick-and-thin decomposition of the domains.
(3)
The compatibility with collars follows from the construction.
By examing conditions of Definition 3.36 we obtained a normal complex structure on this (further stabilized) D-chart lift of (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}.
For any capped orbit p∈TFloerp\in T^{\rm Floer}, we define its associated virtual vector space to be
(Vp+,Vp−):=(Vp¯+,Vp¯−),(V_{p}^{+},V_{p}^{-}):=(V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+},V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}),
where the right hand side is constructed before Lemma 6.23. Then the existence of orientation (Definition 3.43) is a classical result in Floer theory [FH93]. For a modern exposition, the reader could refer to [Par16, Section C.13].
∎
6.3.1. Constructing straightening
We sketch how to construct a straightening on the D-chart lift which is compatible with the existing collar and scaffolding structures. This construction relies on the concrete geometric feature of the obstruction bundles. We do not know if instead the straightening can be constructed in a more abstract level.
We start with moduli spaces without boundary or corners. Let Cp​qC_{pq} be the D-chart. Then by the method of [BX22, Lemma 3.15, Lemma 3.20], one can construct a straightening on the pair (𝒰p​q,ℰp​q)({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}). Indeed, notice that there are a sequence of obstruction bundles ℰp​q(d){\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)} for d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}. We can actually construct, not just a connection on ℰp​q(d0){\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d_{0})} for the lowest d0=dp​qd_{0}=d_{pq} (which is required for straightening), but actually a sequence of metrics on all ℰp​q(d){\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)} and metric connections such that the natural inclusion ℰp​q(d)↪ℰp​q(d′){\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})} (for d<d′d<d^{\prime}) preserves the metric and connection.
Inductively, suppose we have constructed such compatible structures for all Cr​sC_{rs} for dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq}. These structures induce metrics and metric connections on the difference bundles ℱβ​α{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}. Now consider the chart Cp​qC_{pq}. Over a lowest stratum α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q, the existing structures induce metrics and metric connections on ℰβ(d)|𝒰α{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}^{(d)}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}} for all d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} and proper strata between α\alpha and p​qpq. We would like to extend the metric and connection to ℰp​q(d){\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)} over 𝒰α{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha} for all d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}. Starting from the lowest d0=dp​qd_{0}=d_{pq}. Properties proved in Lemma 5.54 shows that we can first extend the metric and metric connection to the sum ℰp​rl→p​q(d0)+ℰr1​q→p​q(d0){\mathcal{E}}_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d_{0})}+{\mathcal{E}}_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d_{0})}. This will be compatible with all existing metrics and connections. Then we extend (arbitrarily but satisfying the requirement for straightening) to ℰp​q(d0)|𝒰α{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}. Then it induces a metric and connection on the difference bundle ℱp​q,α{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha}. Via the stabilization map from 𝒰α{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha} to ∂α𝒰p​q\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}, one obtains a metric on ∂α𝒰p​q\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq} which is straightened. Next, we can inductively construct metrics and connections on ℰp​q(d){\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)} for higher dd. Once we finished for the lowest stratum α\alpha, we can use the collar structure to extend to all nearby higher strata. For a next stratum β\beta, we carry out the induction from d0d_{0} above again. We omit the details.
6.4. Proof of Theorem 6.31
To save notations, we remove all the superscript “+” which indicates the outer-collaring. Instead, we keep in mind that all objects and structures have corresponding collar structures or respect the collar structure.
6.4.1. Smoothing charts without boundary
Consider a moduli space ℳ¯p​q\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq} of Floer trajectories which has no codimension one strata. Let Kp​q=(Gp​q,Vp​q,Ep​q,Sp​q)K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq}) be the global Kuranishi chart constructed. Then Vp​qV_{pq} has no boundary or corner and one can apply Lashof’s smoothing theory directly. In this case there is no outer-collaring to the chart. The projection πp​q:Vp​q→Bp​q\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq} is a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant Cloc1C^{1}_{\rm loc} fiberwise smooth topological submersion. Then by the existence of GG-equivariant fiberwise submersions (Lemma 6.21), one can choose a GG-equivariant fiberwise submersion of πp​q\pi_{pq} which induces an isotopy class of Gp​qG_{pq}-vector bundle reductions of Tμ​Vp​qT_{\mu}V_{pq}. By Lashof’s theorem (Theorem 6.9) on stable GG-smoothings, there exists a stable Gp​qG_{pq}-smoothing on Vp​qV_{pq}, i.e., a finite-dimensional orthogonal representation 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} of Gp​qG_{pq} and a Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant smooth structure on the product
V^p​q:=Vp​q×𝑹p​q.\widehat{V}_{pq}:=V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}.
Let π^p​q:V^p​q→Bp​q\widehat{\pi}_{pq}:\widehat{V}_{pq}\to B_{pq} be composition V^p​q→Vp​q→Bp​q\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}\to B_{pq}. Then there is an isomorphism of Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant vector bundles
T​V^p​q≅Tvt​V^p​q⊕π^p​q∗​T​Bp​q.T\widehat{V}_{pq}\cong T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{pq}^{*}TB_{pq}.
(However, the projection π^p​q\widehat{\pi}_{pq} may not be smooth and the fibers may not be smooth submanifolds.)
Moreover one can give smooth structures on the obstruction bundles. Indeed, one can inductively construct a structure of smooth equivariant O⁡(n)O(n)-bundles on O^p​q(d)→V^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{pq} for all d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} such that O^p​q(d)\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)} is a smooth subbundle of O^p​q(d+1)\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d+1)} by smoothing the relevant equivariant classifying map. The bundle R^p​q→V^p​q\widehat{R}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}, which is the trivial bundle with fiber 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} automatically has a smooth structure. We also use the inner product on 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} to equip the bundle R^p​q\widehat{R}_{pq} with an inner product structure, which is a smooth inner product.
6.4.2. Smoothing charts with boundary and corners
Now consider a chart Kp​q=(Gp​q,Vp​q,Ep​q,Sp​q)K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq}). We have assumed that Kp​qK_{pq} has a collar structure which was actually constructed via outer-collaring. We need to construct a stable smoothing which is of “product type” near the boundary and which extends the existing stable smoothings. We first state the induction hypothesis. As an initial remark, we induct on the energy of the moduli spaces dp​qd_{pq}.
Induction Hypothesis I. Stabilization. Suppose we have the following data.
(1)
An orthogonal Gr​sG_{rs}-representation 𝑹r​s{\bm{R}}_{rs} for each r​srs with dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq}. We define a bundle
Ru​v→r​s∼→∂r​u​v​sVr​s​whenever​r​u​v​s∈𝔸r​sFloerR_{uv\to rs}^{\sim}\to\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}\ {\rm whenever}\ ruvs\in{\mathbb{A}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}
as follows. Consider the product Vr​u​v​s=Vr​u×Vu​v×Vv​sV_{ruvs}=V_{ru}\times V_{uv}\times V_{vs}. Then the trivial bundle Ru​v=Vu​v×𝑹u​v→Vu​vR_{uv}=V_{uv}\times{\bm{R}}_{uv}\to V_{uv} is pulled back to Vr​u​v​sV_{ruvs}. Via the stabilization map StabFr​s,r​u​v​s​(Vr​u​v​s)↪∂r​u​v​sVr​s{\rm Stab}_{F_{rs,ruvs}}(V_{ruvs})\hookrightarrow\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs} this trivial bundle is pulled back to a trivial bundle. Then using the Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariantization we obtain a not-necessarily-trivial vector bundle
Ru​v→r​s∼→∂r​u​v​sVr​s≅Gr​s×Gr​u​v​s(StabFr​s,r​u​v​s​(Vr​u​v​s)).R_{uv\to rs}^{\sim}\to\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}\cong G_{rs}\times_{G_{ruvs}}({\rm Stab}_{F_{rs,ruvs}}(V_{ruvs})).
(2)
A Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariant bundle embedding
ϕu​v→r​s:Ru​v→r​s→Rr​s|∂r​u​v​sVr​s.\phi_{uv\to rs}:R_{uv\to rs}\to R_{rs}|_{\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}}.
(3)
For each α=ru1⋯uls∈𝔸r​sFloer\alpha=ru_{1}\cdots u_{l}s\in{\mathbb{A}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}, define a bundle map
ϕr​s,α:Rα:=Rr​u1⊞⋯⊞Rul​s→Rr​s\phi_{rs,\alpha}:R_{\alpha}:=R_{ru_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus R_{u_{l}s}\to R_{rs}
to be the sum of the maps ϕr​u1→r​s,…,ϕul​s→r​s\phi_{ru_{1}\to rs},\ldots,\phi_{u_{l}s\to rs}. We require that the bundle map is actually a bundle embedding. This induces bundle embeddings
ϕβ​α:Rα→Rβ​α≤β,α,β∈𝔸r​sFloer.\phi_{\beta\alpha}:R_{\alpha}\to R_{\beta}\ \alpha\leq\beta,\ \alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}.
We require that the collection {Rr​s}\{R_{rs}\} and the collection {ϕβ​α}\{\phi_{\beta\alpha}\} satisfy the requirements for stabilization of a K-chart lift (see Definition 6.25) up to level dp​qd_{pq}, i.e., the K-charts for moduli spaces ℳ¯r​sFloer\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer} with dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq} are equipped with stabilizations induced from Rr​sR_{rs} with compatibility conditions satisfied.
(4)
We also assume we have a Gr​sG_{rs}-invariant inner product on the trivial bundle Rr​s→Vr​sR_{rs}\to V_{rs} for all dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq} such that the bundle maps ϕr​s,α\phi_{rs,\alpha} are all isometric.
Moreover, we assume that the representations 𝑹r​s{\bm{R}}_{rs} and bundle embeddings ϕu​v→r​s\phi_{uv\to rs} are of the following particular forms. (One can see the discussion in Section 6.2.4 for why we impose the following requirement.)
(1)
We have a Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariant orthogonal decomposition
𝑹r​s=𝑹r​s,−⊕𝑹r​s,0{\bm{R}}_{rs}={\bm{R}}_{rs,-}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs,0}
and a Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariant orthogonal decomposition
𝑹r​s,−=⨁r<w<s𝑹r​s,w=⨁r<w<s𝑹r​wr​s⊕𝑹w​sr​s.{\bm{R}}_{rs,-}=\bigoplus_{r<w<s}{\bm{R}}_{rs,w}=\bigoplus_{r<w<s}{\bm{R}}_{rw}^{rs}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{ws}^{rs}.
Intuitively, 𝑹r​s,−{\bm{R}}_{rs,-} comes from lower stratum via Frobenius reciprocity, and 𝑹r​s,0{\bm{R}}_{rs,0} is chosen so that a relative smoothing exists. Then one has the (trivial) bundles Rr​s,−R_{rs,-}, Rr​s,0R_{rs,0}, Rr​wr​sR_{rw}^{rs}, Rw​sr​sR_{ws}^{rs} over Vr​sV_{rs}.
(2)
Whenever r<w<sr<w<s, the representations 𝑹r​wr​s{\bm{R}}_{rw}^{rs} resp. 𝑹w​sr​s{\bm{R}}_{ws}^{rs} contains 𝑹r​w{\bm{R}}_{rw} resp. 𝑹w​s{\bm{R}}_{ws} as orthogonal subrepresentations of Gr​wG_{rw} resp. Gw​sG_{ws}.
(3)
For all u​v≺r​suv\prec rs (which implies r≤u<v≤sr\leq u<v\leq s), abbreviate ϕu​v=ϕu​v→r​s\phi_{uv}=\phi_{uv\to rs}. Then the range of ϕu​v\phi_{uv} is contained in Rr​s,−R_{rs,-} and can be written as
ϕu​v=⨁r<w<sϕu​v,w,ϕu​v,w:Ru​v→r​s→Rr​s,w.\phi_{uv}=\bigoplus_{r<w<s}\phi_{uv,w},\ \phi_{uv,w}:R_{uv\to rs}\to R_{rs,w}.
Moreover, for each ww, the map ϕu​v,w\phi_{uv,w} satisfies the following conditions. We write ϕu​v,w:Ru​v→r​s→Rr​s,w=Rr​wr​s⊕Rw​sr​s\phi_{uv,w}:R_{uv\to rs}\to R_{rs,w}=R_{rw}^{rs}\oplus R_{ws}^{rs} as the sum ϕu​v,r​w⊕ϕu​v,w​s\phi_{uv,rw}\oplus\phi_{uv,ws}.
(a)
If w≤uw\leq u, then ϕu​v,r​w=0\phi_{uv,rw}=0. Moreover, over the stratum Vr​w​u​v​s⊂Vr​u​v​sV_{rwuvs}\subset V_{ruvs}, ϕu​v,w​s\phi_{uv,ws} is induced from the bundle map
Ru​v→w​s\textstyle{R_{uv\to ws}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}ϕu​v→w​s\scriptstyle{\phi_{uv\to ws}}Rw​s\textstyle{R_{ws}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Vw​u​v​s\textstyle{V_{wuvs}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Vw​s\textstyle{V_{ws}}
and the linear inclusion 𝑹w​s↪𝑹w​sr​s{\bm{R}}_{ws}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ws}^{rs}.
(b)
If w≥vw\geq v, then ϕu​v,w​s=0\phi_{uv,ws}=0. Moreover, over the stratum Vr​u​v​w​s⊂Vr​u​v​sV_{ruvws}\subset V_{ruvs}, ϕu​v,r​w\phi_{uv,rw} is induced from the bundle map
Ru​v→r​w\textstyle{R_{uv\to rw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}ϕu​v→r​w\scriptstyle{\phi_{uv\to rw}}Rr​w\textstyle{R_{rw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Vr​u​v​w\textstyle{V_{ruvw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Vr​w\textstyle{V_{rw}}
and the linear inclusion 𝑹r​w↪𝑹r​wr​s{\bm{R}}_{rw}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{rw}^{rs}.
(c)
If u<w<vu<w<v, then the following is true. Consider the inclusion
𝑹u​w⊕𝑹w​v↪𝑹u​wu​v⊕𝑹w​vu​v↪𝑹u​v.{\bm{R}}_{uw}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wv}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{uw}^{uv}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wv}^{uv}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{uv}.
The orthogonal projection
𝑹u​v→𝑹u​w⊕𝑹w​v{\bm{R}}_{uv}\to{\bm{R}}_{uw}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wv}
is Gu​w​vG_{uwv}-equivariant, hence induces a bundle map
Ru​v|Vu​w​v→Ru​w⊞Rw​v.R_{uv}|_{V_{uwv}}\to R_{uw}\boxplus R_{wv}.
On the other hand, there are bundle embeddings
Ru​w→r​w↪Rr​w↪Rr​wr​s,\displaystyle\ R_{uw\to rw}\hookrightarrow R_{rw}\hookrightarrow R_{rw}^{rs},\
Rw​v→w​s↪Rw​s↪Rw​sr​s.\displaystyle\ R_{wv\to ws}\hookrightarrow R_{ws}\hookrightarrow R_{ws}^{rs}.
Then over the stratum Vr​u​w​v​s⊂Vr​u​v​sV_{ruwvs}\subset V_{ruvs}, the map ϕu​v,w\phi_{uv,w} is the composition
Ru​v→r​s¯|Vr​u​w​v​s¯→Ru​w→r​s¯⊕Rw​v→r​s¯→Rr​wr​s⊕Rw​sr​s.R_{\underline{\smash{uv\to rs}}}|_{V_{\underline{\smash{ruwvs}}}}\to R_{\underline{\smash{uw\to rs}}}\oplus R_{\underline{\smash{wv\to rs}}}\to R_{rw}^{rs}\oplus R_{ws}^{rs}.
(d)
If none of the above happends, then ϕu​v,w=0\phi_{uv,w}=0.
Moreover, the bundles and bundle maps will be added with ⋅^\widehat{\cdot} when lifted to the stabilizations V^r​s\widehat{V}_{rs}. Such lifts are canonical.
Induction Hypothesis II. Fiberwise submersion. Whenever dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq}, we have chosen a Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariant fiberwise submersion (Definition 6.18) of π^r​s:V^r​s→Br​s\widehat{\pi}_{rs}:\widehat{V}_{rs}\to B_{rs}, which is a microbundle isomorphism
Tμ​V^r​s≅Tμvt​V^r​s⊕π^r​s∗​Tμ​Bdr​sT_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{rs}\cong T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{rs}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{rs}^{*}T_{\mu}B_{d_{rs}}
which is the canonical projection map on the second component. Together with the structure of Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} fiberwise smooth Gr​sG_{rs}-bundle on π^r​s\widehat{\pi}_{rs}, this provides an isotopy class of vector bundle lifts
Tvt​V^r​s⊕π^r​s∗​T​Bdr​s→Tμ​V^r​s,T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{rs}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{rs}^{*}TB_{d_{rs}}\to T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{rs},
(6.8)
where the isotopy class is fixed by Equation (6.3).
Induction Hypothesis III. Stable smoothings and bundle smoothings. Whenever dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq}, we have chosen a Gr​sG_{rs}-invariant smoothing on V^r​s:=Vr​s×𝑹r​s\widehat{V}_{rs}:=V_{rs}\times{\bm{R}}_{rs} in the Gr​sG_{rs}-isotopy class corresponding to stable isotopy class of the vector bundle lift (6.8). Moreover, we have chosen a structure of smooth Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariant O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle on O^r​s(d)→V^r​s\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{rs} for all d≥dr​sd\geq d_{rs} and a structure of smooth Gr​sG_{rs}-equivariant O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle on R^r​s\widehat{R}_{rs}.
Before we state the conditions about these structures, we summarize a few consequences of these induction hypotheses. First, the stable smoothings induce, for each stratum α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q, a GαG_{\alpha}-equivariant smooth structure on the product
V^α=V^p​r1×⋯×V^rk​q\widehat{V}_{\alpha}=\widehat{V}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\widehat{V}_{r_{k}q}
and hence a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant smooth structure on V^α∼\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim} by taking the equivariantization. Second, the smooth structures on the vector bundles induce smooth structures on the product bundles O^α\widehat{O}_{\alpha} and R^α\widehat{R}_{\alpha}. Third, as the bundle Qr​s→Vr​sQ_{rs}\to V_{rs} is trivial, the pullback Q^r​s→V^r​s\widehat{Q}_{rs}\to\widehat{V}_{rs} is automatically smooth. Similarly, the bundle
Q^r​s,α=(Q^α)⊥→V^α\widehat{Q}_{rs,\alpha}=(\widehat{Q}_{\alpha})^{\bot}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
is smooth because it comes from a product bundle.
Now we state the conditions.
(1)
The bundle embedding
ϕ^β​α:R^α→R^β|∂αV^β\widehat{\phi}_{\beta\alpha}:\widehat{R}_{\alpha}\to\widehat{R}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{\beta}}
is smooth.1919
19
This is not automatic as this bundle embedding is not purely induced from linear maps between representation spaces. This implies that the orthogonal complement
R^r​s,α→V^α\widehat{R}_{rs,\alpha}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
is a smooth bundle.
(2)
Whenever d′>d≥dr​sd^{\prime}>d\geq d_{rs}, the natural bundle embedding O^r​s(d)→O^r​s(d′)\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}\to\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d^{\prime})} induced from (5.34) is smooth.
(3)
For all d≥dr​sd\geq d_{rs}, the bundle embedding
ϕ^u​v→r​s:O^u​v→r​s(d)→O^r​s(d)|∂r​u​v​sVr​s\widehat{\phi}_{uv\to rs}:\widehat{O}_{uv\to rs}^{(d)}\to\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}}
is smooth. This implies that (when d=dr​sd=d_{rs}) the orthogonal complement
O^r​s,α→Vα\widehat{O}_{rs,\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}
is a smooth bundle.
(4)
It follows that
F^r​s,α:=O^r​s,α⊕Q^r​s,α⊕R^r​s,α\widehat{F}_{rs,\alpha}:=\widehat{O}_{rs,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{rs,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{rs,\alpha}
over the smooth manifold with corners V^α\widehat{V}_{\alpha} is smooth. Then we require that the stabilization map
θ^r​s,α:StabF^r​s,α​(V^α)→∂αV^r​s\widehat{\theta}_{rs,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{rs,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{rs}
is a germ of diffeomorphisms onto an open set. These structures ensure that the projection map
π^r​s,α:∂αVr​s→V^α∼\widehat{\pi}_{rs,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}V_{rs}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}
is smooth.
(5)
For all d≥dr​sd\geq d_{rs}, the bundle isomorphism
ψ^r​s,α:π^r​s,α∗​(O^r​s(d)|V^α∼)→O^r​s(d)|∂αVr​s\widehat{\psi}_{rs,\alpha}:\widehat{\pi}_{rs,\alpha}^{*}\Big(\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\Big)\to\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{rs}}
is smooth.
(6)
The existing smoothings (on both domains and bundles) respect the corner structures. Namely, for all r​srs with dr​s<dp​qd_{rs}<d_{pq}, item (B) of Theorem 6.31 is satisfied.
Now we start to construct the stabilized smoothings corresponding to the Kuranishi chart Kp​qK_{pq}.
Inductive Construction I. We construct an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-space 𝐑p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} (which defines the trivial bundle Rp​q→Vp​qR_{pq}\to V_{pq}) and bundle embeddings
ϕr​s→p​q:Rr​s→p​q→Rp​q|∂p​r​s​qVp​q\phi_{rs\to pq}:R_{rs\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prsq}V_{pq}}
which, together with the existing stabilizations and bundle embeddings, satisfy the conditions required for stabilizations.
(1)
Firstly, we need to find an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-representation 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} such that the stabilization Rp​q=Vp​q×𝑹p​qR_{pq}=V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq} receives embeddings from RαR_{\alpha} for all α∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}.
To start, for each p<w<qp<w<q, consider the Gp​wG_{pw}-representation 𝑹p​w{\bm{R}}_{pw} and the Gw​qG_{wq}-representation 𝑹w​q{\bm{R}}_{wq} granted by the induction hypothesis. By Frobenius reciprocity, there exist an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-representation 𝑹p​wp​q{\bm{R}}_{pw}^{pq} which contains 𝑹p​w{\bm{R}}_{pw} as a subrepresentation of Gp​wG_{pw}, and an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-representation 𝑹w​qp​q{\bm{R}}_{wq}^{pq} which contains 𝑹w​q{\bm{R}}_{wq} as a subrepresentation of Gw​qG_{wq}. Then define
𝑹p​q:=⨁p<w<q𝑹p​wp​q⊕𝑹w​qp​q{\bm{R}}_{pq}:=\bigoplus_{p<w<q}{\bm{R}}_{pw}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wq}^{pq}
(6.9)
which is an orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-space. Now we define the bundle embeddings
ϕr​s→p​q:Rr​s→p​q→Rp​q|∂p​r​s​qVp​q.\phi_{rs\to pq}:R_{rs\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prsq}V_{pq}}.
We abbreviate the decomposition 6.9 as
𝑹p​q=⨁w𝑹p​q,w=⨁p<w<q𝑹p​wp​q⊕𝑹w​qp​q.{\bm{R}}_{pq}=\bigoplus_{w}{\bm{R}}_{pq,w}=\bigoplus_{p<w<q}{\bm{R}}_{pw}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wq}^{pq}.
Abbreviate the bundle embedding to be defined by ϕr​s\phi_{rs}. Then we define it to be the direct sum
ϕr​s=⨁wϕr​s,w=⨁wϕr​s,p​w⊕ϕr​s,w​q​where​ϕr​s,w:Rr​s→p​q→Rp​q,w|∂p​r​s​qVp​q.\phi_{rs}=\bigoplus_{w}\phi_{rs,w}=\bigoplus_{w}\phi_{rs,pw}\oplus\phi_{rs,wq}\ {\rm where}\ \phi_{rs,w}:R_{rs\to pq}\to R_{pq,w}|_{\partial^{prsq}V_{pq}}.
In fact, for each ww, one can define the restriction of ϕr​s,w\phi_{rs,w} to the corresponding stratum involving ww, such as p​w​r​s​qpwrsq, p​r​s​w​qprswq, or p​r​w​s​qprwsq in the form described in Induction Hypothesis I. These are all codimension-1 boundary strata of p​r​s​qprsq. Then using the collar structure of existing objects and a cut-off function which only depends on the collar coordinate, one can turn off the corresponding maps ϕr​s,w\phi_{rs,w} once we go away from this corner. On the other hand, if w=rw=r or w=sw=s, we just have a bundle embedding coming from the induction hypothesis. One can check that the newly constructed objects still satisfy the requirement for a stabilization up to level dp​qd_{pq} (see Definition 6.25).
(2)
Secondly, we would like to construct an inner product on Rp​qR_{pq}.
We start from a minimal α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q. Notice that inside Rp​q|∂αVp​qR_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}, there are a collection of embedded subbundles Rβ→p​q|∂αVp​qR_{\beta\to pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}} for all β\beta with α≤β<p​q\alpha\leq\beta<pq with an inner product equipped. We first check that, for any pair β,β′\beta,\beta^{\prime} of such strata, their inner products agree on the overlap
Rβ→p​q∩Rβ′→p​q⊂Rp​q.R_{\beta\to pq}\cap R_{\beta^{\prime}\to pq}\subset R_{pq}.
In fact the intersection ∂βVp​q∩∂β′Vp​q\partial^{\beta}V_{pq}\cap\partial^{\beta^{\prime}}V_{pq} is a deeper stratum ∂β​#​β′Vp​q\partial^{\beta\#\beta^{\prime}}V_{pq}. Here β​#​β′\beta\#\beta^{\prime} stands for the stratum described by the word which includes all the intermediate capped orbits in β\beta and β′\beta^{\prime} between pp and qq. We can in fact check from the explicit construction that
Rβ→p​q∩Rβ′→p​q=Rβ​#​β′→p​qR_{\beta\to pq}\cap R_{\beta^{\prime}\to pq}=R_{\beta\#\beta^{\prime}\to pq}
Hence by induction hypothesis, the inner products agrees on overlaps.
Now there are two special codimension one strata, β1=p​r1​q\beta_{1}=pr_{1}q and βl=p​rl​q\beta_{l}=pr_{l}q. We can check that
Rβ→p​q⊂Rβ1→p​q+Rβl→p​q.R_{\beta\to pq}\subset R_{\beta_{1}\to pq}+R_{\beta_{l}\to pq}.
Hence we can define an inner product on the sum Rβ1→p​q+Rβl→p​qR_{\beta_{1}\to pq}+R_{\beta_{l}\to pq} which extends the existing ones. Then extend arbitrarily to Rp​qR_{pq} over this stratum.
Inductively, we can use the collar structure near α\alpha to extend the inner product on ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq} to a neighborhood. Then the same argument above can be applied to construct an inner product inductively on Rp​qR_{pq}.
Inductive Construction II. We construct smooth bundle structures on the obstruction bundles O^p​q(d)→V^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{pq} for all d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} over ∂V^p​q\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}. The construction is inductive on strata α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q of p​qpq. We start with a minimal α\alpha. Then there are the vector bundles
Oα(d)→Vα=Vp​r1×⋯×Vrl​q,∀d≥dp​q.O_{\alpha}^{(d)}\to V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q},\ \forall d\geq d_{pq}.
Denote by its pullback to V^α\widehat{V}_{\alpha} by
O^α(d)→V^α.\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}.
The induction hypothesis granted a smooth O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle structure on it which is the product of each individual factor. Notice that this is a subbundle of O^p​q(d)|V^α\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}. We would like to extend this smooth structure to a smooth structure on O^p​q|V^α\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.
Start with d=d0=dp​qd=d_{0}=d_{pq} and remember O^p​q=O^p​q(d0)\widehat{O}_{pq}=\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}. Consider any intermediate stratum β=ps1⋯smq\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q. Then one has the inclusion induced by (5.53)
O^α(d0)⊂O^β(d0)|V^α⊂O^p​q|V^α.\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d_{0})}\subset\widehat{O}_{\beta}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\subset\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.
Notice that by the induction hypothesis, the smooth structure on O^β(d0)|V^α\widehat{O}_{\beta}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}} extends the smooth structure on O^α(d0)\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d_{0})}. This requires that the smooth structure on O^p​q|V^α\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}} needs to be an extension of all such smooth structures. Indeed, consider two codimension one strata, p​r1​qpr_{1}q and p​rl​qpr_{l}q. Consider the two bundles
O^p​rl→p​q(d0)|V^α,\displaystyle\ \widehat{O}_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}},\
O^r1​q→p​q(d0)|V^α.\displaystyle\ \widehat{O}_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.
Abbreviate them temporarily by O^rl(d0)\widehat{O}_{r_{l}}^{(d_{0})} and O^r1(d0)\widehat{O}_{r_{1}}^{(d_{0})} respectively. Then it is easy to see that for any intermediate stratum β\beta, one has
O^β(d0)|V^α⊂O^rl(d0)+O^r1(d0).\widehat{O}_{\beta}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\subset\widehat{O}_{r_{l}}^{(d_{0})}+\widehat{O}_{r_{1}}^{(d_{0})}.
(6.10)
Then Lemma 6.33 below provides a smooth structure on the sum O^rl(d0)+O^r1(d0)\widehat{O}_{r_{l}}^{(d_{0})}+\widehat{O}_{r_{1}}^{(d_{0})}. Then we extend this extension to O^p​q|V^α\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}} by choosing an arbitrary smooth structure on its orthogonal complement.
Lemma 6.33.
Let MM be a smooth manifold and let E→ME\to M be a topological vector bundle equipped with an inner product. Let E1,E2⊂EE_{1},E_{2}\subset E be subbundles such that E=E1+E2E=E_{1}+E_{2}. Moreover, suppose E1E_{1} and E2E_{2} are equipped with structures of smooth vector bundles with respect to which the restriction of the inner product is smooth, such that the intersection E1∩E2E_{1}\cap E_{2} is both a smooth subbundle of E1E_{1} and a smooth subbundle of E2E_{2}. Then there exists a unique smooth bundle structure of EE which extends the smooth bundle structures on E1E_{1} and E2E_{2} such that the inner product is smooth.
Proof.
Define a smooth vector bundle E′→ME^{\prime}\to M from E1E_{1} and E2E_{2} as
E′:=E1′⊕E2′⊕(E1∩E2)E^{\prime}:=E_{1}^{\prime}\oplus E_{2}^{\prime}\oplus(E_{1}\cap E_{2})
where E1′E_{1}^{\prime} resp. E2′E_{2}^{\prime} is the orthogonal complement of E1∩E2E_{1}\cap E_{2} in E1E_{1} resp. E2E_{2}. Then there is an canonical isometric bundle isomorphism E′→EE^{\prime}\to E.
∎
As O^p​q,α\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha} is the orthogonal complement of O^α(d0)\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d_{0})}, the above smooth structure induces a smooth structure on O^p​q|V^α→V^α\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}, hence one on O^p​q,α→V^α\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}. On the other hand, run the same argument as above, one can cook up a smooth O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle structure on R^p​q|V^α\widehat{R}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}} which extends existing ones. Hence the orthogonal complement R^p​q,α\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha} is a smooth O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle. The bundle Q^p​q,α\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}, which is essentially trivial, automatically carries a smooth structure. Hence the bundle
F^p​q,α=O^p​q,α⊕Q^p​q,α⊕R^p​q,α\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}=\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}
(6.11)
is smooth. Then the stabilization map (see (5.50))
θ^p​q,α:StabF^p​q,α​(V^α)→∂αV^p​q\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
and its equivariantization induce a smooth structure on the stratum ∂αV^p​q\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}.
Next we can inductively construct smooth structures on the bundle O^p​q(d)|Vp​q,α¯\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{pq,\underline{\smash{\alpha}}}} for all d≥d0=dp​qd\geq d_{0}=d_{pq}. This is similar to the construction of inner products. We omit the details. Using equivariantizations and the bundle isomorphisms coming from the stabilization map associated to F^p​q,α\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}, one obtains smooth structures on the bundle O^p​q(d)|∂αV^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}} for all d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}. Using the collar structure, we extend slightly to a neighborhood of ∂αV^p​q\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq} inside V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq}.
We can carry on the induction for all strata α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q. Suppose we have constructed a smooth structure near the boundary of ∂αV^p​q\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq} and smooth bundle structures on O^p​q(d)\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)} over this neighborhood. Now start with d=d0=dp​qd=d_{0}=d_{pq} and we would like to construct the smooth structure of ∂αV^p​q\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq} and the smooth bundle structure on O^p​q(d0)|∂αV^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}. First, the induction hypothesis granted a smooth structure on the product V^α\widehat{V}_{\alpha}. This is compatible with all the smooth structures near the boundary of ∂αV^p​q\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}. Then similar to above, one can have a smooth structure on the sum (the right hand side of (6.10)). Then extend to a smooth structure on O^p​q|∂αV^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}. The stabilization map then grants a smooth structure on ∂αV^p​q\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq} which extends the existing one near the boundary. The bundle isomorphism provides a smooth bundle structure of O^p​q|∂αV^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}. Inductively, one extends the smooth bundle structure to O^p​q(d)|∂αV^p​q\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}} for all d≥d0d\geq d_{0}. Then use the collar structure we extend the structures slightly into the interior of V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq}.
Inductive Construction III. We construct a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant vector bundle reduction of Tμ​V^p​qT_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq} which is smooth near the boundary and is in the the isotopy class of the vector bundle reduction induced from the existing smoothings. The previous construction induces a Gp​qG_{pq}-smoothing on a neighborhood Nϵ​(∂V^p​q)N_{\epsilon}(\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}) of the boundary, using the smooth structures on bundles of the form (6.11) and equivariantization. Hence there is a corresponding (isotopy class) of vector bundle lift of the tangent microbundle Tμ​V^p​qT_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq} restricted to this region. From the construction we can see that
T⁡(Nϵ​(∂V^p​q))≅(Tvt​V^p​q⊕π^p​q∗​T​Bdp​q)|Nϵ​(∂V^p​q).T\left(N_{\epsilon}(\partial\widehat{V}_{pq})\right)\cong\left(T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{pq}^{*}TB_{d_{pq}}\right)|_{N_{\epsilon}(\partial\widehat{V}_{pq})}.
On the other hand, we know that via a fiberwise submersion on V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq} one can obtain another vector bundle lift of Tμ​V^p​qT_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq} by the same vector bundle.
We claim that these two vector bundle lifts, when restricted to their common domains, are in the same stable Gp​qG_{pq}-isotopy class of vector bundle lifts. We show why this is the case if α\alpha is a codimension 1 stratum. In this case, we know that
∂αVp​q=StabFp​q,α​(Vα)\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}={\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})
i.e., the boundary ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq} is a disk bundle over VαV_{\alpha}. We can choose an (invariant) connection on Fp​q,αF_{pq,\alpha} to induce a smooth splitting
T​Fp​q,α≅Tvt​Fp​q,α⊕πFp​q,α∗​T​Vα.TF_{pq,\alpha}\cong T^{\rm vt}F_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\pi_{F_{pq,\alpha}}^{*}TV_{\alpha}.
It also induces a corresponding splitting on the microbundle level. Although the fibers of the projection onto Bδ⁡(α)B_{\delta(\alpha)} are no longer smooth, the microbundle version of the above splitting can still be restricted to each fiber. Then combining with the microbundle splitting of Tμ​VαT_{\mu}V_{\alpha} one obtains
Tμ​(∂αVp​q)≅πFp​q,α∗​(Fp​q,α)μ⊕πFp​q,α∗​Tμ​Vα≅Tμvt​(∂αVp​q)⊕π∗​Tμ​Bδ⁡(α).T_{\mu}(\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq})\cong\pi_{F_{pq,\alpha}}^{*}(F_{pq,\alpha})_{\mu}\oplus\pi_{F_{pq,\alpha}}^{*}T_{\mu}V_{\alpha}\cong T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}(\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq})\oplus\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B_{\delta(\alpha)}.
Notice that this is an equivariant fiberwise submersion of ∂αVp​q→∂δ⁡(α)Bd\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\to\partial^{\delta(\alpha)}B_{d} (see Definition 6.18). The collar structure can be used to extend this fiberwise submersion trivially into the collar region. Using Lemma 6.21 one can extend it globally to Vp​qV_{pq}. Then using the fiberwise smooth structure we obtained a vector bundle lift of Tμ​Vp​qT_{\mu}V_{pq}.
On the other hand, the vector bundle lift on VαV_{\alpha} induced from smoothing is in the same stable isotopy class of vector bundle lifts as the one from fiberwise smooth structure and microbundle splitting. Therefore, one can find another orthogonal Gp​qG_{pq}-space 𝑹p​q′{\bm{R}}_{pq}^{\prime} such that the 𝑹p​q′{\bm{R}}_{pq}^{\prime}-stabilization of the interior vector bundle lift of Tμ​V^p​qT_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq} is isotopic to the one from the boundary smooth structure. Also notice that one can choose a Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant continuous cut-off function on the overlap of the two domains of the vector bundle lifts to interpolate this two vector bundle lifts using the isotopy. Note that this is one salient feature of the existence of collar structures: we are free to interpolate between different data over the collar regions. Therefore, we have obtained a vector bundle lift of Tμ​V^p​qT_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq} which is smooth near the boundary.
In general, when α\alpha is not a codimension 1 stratum, there are many intermediate stratum. However, one can choose connections on all the intermediate difference bundles Fβ​αF_{\beta\alpha} compatibly, utilizing Lemma 5.54. This still allows us to build the interior microbundle splitting which extends the boundary ones. We omit the details.
Inductive Construction IV. Smoothing for p​qpq. In the previous steps, we have obtained a smooth structure on a neighborhood of ∂V^p​q\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}, a smooth O⁡(n)O(n)-bundle structure of O^p​q(d)\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)} over this neighborhood, and a Gp​qG_{pq}-equivariant vector bundle reduction
ρp​q:(Tvt​V^p​q⊕π^p​q∗​Bp​q)μ→Tμ​V^p​q\rho_{pq}:(T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{pq}^{*}B_{pq})_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}
which is smooth near ∂V^p​q\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}. Then one can apply the relative version of Lashof’s GG-smoothing theorem (see Appendix B and Theorem B.3) to obtain another Gp​qG_{pq}-orthogonal space 𝑹p​q,0{\bm{R}}_{pq,0} and a Gp​qG_{pq}-smoothing on Int​Vp​q^×𝑹p​q,0{\rm Int}\widehat{V_{pq}}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq,0} which coincides with the stabilization of the existing smoothing. Redefine 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} by taking direct sum with 𝑹p​q,0{\bm{R}}_{pq,0} and redefine V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq}, O^p​q(d)\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)} etc. Moreover, one can extend the smooth structures on O^p​q(d)\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)} inductively to the interior of V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq}. Furthermore, we extend the existing inner product on the trivial bundle Rp​qR_{pq} to the whole V^p​q\widehat{V}_{pq} smoothly.
Finally, we see that we have constructed a smoothing for any K-chart Kp​qK_{pq} with energy dp​qd_{pq}. Furthermore, they satisfy the induction hypotheses listed at the beginning. Therefore, Theorem 6.31 holds. ∎
7. Constructions for PSS, SSP, and the homotopy
In this subsection, we discuss K-charts for PSS, SSP, and continuation type moduli spaces. The constructions here are carried out almost verbatim as the case of moduli spaces of Floer trajectories. Therefore, our main purpose here is to fix the notations, and only the key modifications are presented in detail.
7.1. PSS bimodule and SSP bimodule
7.1.1. Thimbles and auxiliary moduli spaces
Definition 7.1.
(1)
A prestable PSS thimble is a triple (Σ,ΣPSS,𝐋)(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{PSS}},{\bf L}) where Σ\Sigma is a genus 00 prestable curve with two marked points z−,z+z_{-},z_{+}, with ΣPSS\Sigma_{\text{PSS}} being a distinguished horizontal irreducible component of Σ\Sigma, and 𝐋=(Li){\bf L}=({\rm L}_{i}) where Li{\rm L}_{i} is a lateral line on each horizontal component lying between ΣPSS\Sigma_{\text{PSS}} (included!) and the marked point z+z_{+} (see Figure 2).
Figure 2. An example of prestable PSS thimble. The red curves are the lateral lines and the gray sphere is ΣPSS\Sigma_{\rm PSS} on which we interpolate between the Hamiltonian HH and a constant function.
(2)
A prestable SSP thimble is a triple (Σ,ΣSSP,𝐋)(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{SSP}},{\bf L}) where Σ\Sigma is a genus 00 prestable curve with two marked points z−,z+z_{-},z_{+}, with ΣSSP\Sigma_{\text{SSP}} being a distinguished horizontal irreducible component of Σ\Sigma, and 𝐋=(Li){\bf L}=({\rm L}_{i}) where Li{\rm L}_{i} is a lateral line on each horizontal component lying between the marked point z−z_{-} and ΣSSP\Sigma_{\text{SSP}}.
By imposing extra marked points and stability conditions, one can define the notion of stable marked PSS/SSP thimble as in Definition 5.12. Given an integer d>0d>0, the moduli space of stable PSS resp. SSP thimbles in ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d} is the set of equivalence classes of objects
(Σ,ΣPSS,𝐋,u),resp.(Σ,ΣSSP,𝐋,u)(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm PSS},{\bf L},u),\ \ \ \ \ {\rm resp.}\ \ \ \ \ (\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm SSP},{\bf L},u)
where (Σ,ΣPSS/ΣSSP,𝐋)(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{PSS}}/\Sigma_{\text{SSP}},{\bf L}) is a prestable PSS/SSP thimble and u:Σ→ℂ​ℙdu:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d} is a stable map. We define ℱ0,2PSS​(d){\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d) to be the subset of moduli spaces of stable PSS thimbles such that the underlying map u:Σ→ℂ​ℙdu:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d} is not contained in any hyperplane, and ℱ0,2SSP​(d){\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d) can be defined similarly. These two moduli spaces are smooth manifolds with corners. Just as Lemma 5.15, the following statements hold.
(1)
The natural forgetful map ℱ0,2PSS​(d),ℱ0,2SSP​(d)→ℱ0,2​(d){\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d),{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d)\to{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}(d) is smooth.
(2)
The evaluation maps ev±\text{ev}_{\pm} at the marked points z±z_{\pm} are smooth and stratified submersive onto ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d}.
(3)
By intersecting with d⁡(d+2)d(d+2) generic hyperplanes in ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d}, the moduli spaces ℱ0,2PSS​(d),ℱ0,2SSP​(d){\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d),{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d) are locally diffeomorphic to a real blowup of the Deligne–Mumford space ℳ¯0,d⁡(d+2)\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,d(d+2)}.
Note that we do not necessarily equip all the horizontal components of (Σ,𝐋)(\Sigma,{\bf L}) with a lateral line, so the real blowup in (3) is different from the one in Lemma 5.15.
The spaces ℱ0,2PSS​(d){\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm PSS}(d) and ℱ0,2SSP​(d){\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm SSP}(d) are stratified by the same poset 𝔸d{\mathbb{A}}_{d} of ordered partitions of dd (with the same depth function, see Notation 5.7). However, the geometric meaning of an element δ=(d0,…,dl)∈𝔸d\delta=(d_{0},\ldots,d_{l})\in{\mathbb{A}}_{d} is different. For example, in the PSS case, the integer d0d_{0} represents the total degree of the components between z−z_{-} and ΣPSS\Sigma_{\rm PSS} (included).
Notation 7.2.
(1)
Let p,qp,q be capped 1-periodic capped Hamiltonian orbits. Introduce the integers
d∙q:=𝒜H​(q)−CPSS,\displaystyle\ d_{\bullet q}:={\mathcal{A}}_{H}(q)-C^{\rm PSS},\
dp∙:=CSSP−𝒜H(p)\displaystyle\ d_{p\bullet}:=C^{\rm SSP}-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)
where CPSSC^{\rm PSS} resp. CSSPC^{\rm SSP} is the constant chosen to define the PSS{\rm PSS} resp. SSP{\rm SSP} moduli spaces (see Subsection 4.3).
(2)
Extra symmetry groups
G∙qPSS:={g∈P​U​(d∙q+1)|g⁡([1,0,…,0])=[1,0,…,0]∈ℂ​ℙd∙p}≅U⁡(d∙p)G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}:=\{g\in PU(d_{\bullet q}+1)\ |\ g([1,0,\ldots,0])=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{\bullet p}}\}\cong U(d_{\bullet p})
Gp∙SSP:={g∈PU(dp∙+1)|g([1,0,…,0])=[1,0,…,0]∈ℂℙdp∙}≅U(dp∙)G_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:=\{g\in PU(d_{p\bullet}+1)\ |\ g([1,0,\ldots,0])=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{p\bullet}}\}\cong U(d_{p\bullet})
where the isomorphisms are induced from (5.3).
(3)

𝔸∙qPSS:={∙r1⋯rlq|r1<⋯<rl<p as objects in TFloer}{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=\{\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q|r_{1}<\cdots<r_{l}<p\text{ as objects in }T^{\rm Floer}\}
𝔸p∙SSP:={pr1⋯rl∙|p<r1<⋯<rl as objects in TFloer},{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:=\{pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet|p<r_{1}<\cdots<r_{l}\text{ as objects in }T^{\rm Floer}\},
with depth functions
depth(∙r1⋯rlq)=l,depth(pr1⋯rl∙)=l.{\rm depth}(\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q)=l,\ {\rm depth}(pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet)=l.
The concatenation of words induces maps between homogeneous posets
𝔸∙pPSS×𝔸p​qFloer→𝔸∙qPSS{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}

There are natural maps of posets
δPSS:𝔸∙qPSS→𝔸d∙q,δSSP:𝔸p∙SSP→𝔸dp∙\delta^{\rm PSS}:{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{\bullet q}},\ \delta^{\rm SSP}:{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{p\bullet}}
defined by
δPSS(∙r1⋯rlq):=(d∙r1,…,drl​q),δSSP(pr1⋯rl∙):=(dp​r1,…,drl∙),\delta^{\rm PSS}(\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q):=(d_{\bullet r_{1}},\dots,d_{r_{l}q}),\ \delta^{\rm SSP}(pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet):=(d_{pr_{1}},\dots,d_{r_{l}\bullet}),
such that the following diagrams commute.
