(4)
For an index α=∙r1⋯rlp∈𝔸PSS∙q\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}p\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}, there is an embedding of Lie groups
GPSSα:=GPSS∙r1×⋯×Grl​q↪GPSS∙q=GPSS∙qG^{\text{PSS}}_{\alpha}:=G^{\text{PSS}}_{\bullet r_{1}}\times\cdots\times G_{r_{l}q}\hookrightarrow G^{\text{PSS}}_{\bullet q}=G^{\text{PSS}}_{\bullet q}
as from Notation 5.7, which further induces an embedding GαPSS↪GβPSSG^{\text{PSS}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow G^{\text{PSS}}_{\beta} for any pair α≤β\alpha\leq\beta in 𝔸∙pPSS{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}. Analogously, there are embeddings of Lie groups GαSSP↪GβSSPG^{\text{SSP}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow G^{\text{SSP}}_{\beta} where α≤β\alpha\leq\beta are in 𝔸SSPp∙{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}.
(5)
The system of auxiliary moduli spaces
BdPSS:={x=[Σ,ΣPSS,𝐋,u]∈ℱ0,2PSS​(d)|ev−​(x)=[1,0,…,0]∈ℂ​ℙd},B^{\text{PSS}}_{d}:=\big\{x=[\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{PSS}},{\bf L},u]\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d)\ |\ {\rm ev}_{-}(x)=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}\big\},
BdSSP:={x=[Σ,ΣSSP,𝐋,u]∈ℱ0,2SSP​(d)|ev−​(x)=[1,0,…,0]∈ℂ​ℙd},B^{\text{SSP}}_{d}:=\big\{x=[\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{SSP}},{\bf L},u]\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d)\ |\ {\rm ev}_{-}(x)=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}\big\},
where ev−​(x){\rm ev}_{-}(x) is the image of the evaluation map of uu at the marked point z−z_{-}.
Each BdPSSB^{\text{PSS}}_{d} resp. BdSSPB^{\text{SSP}}_{d} is a smooth 𝔸d{\mathbb{A}}_{d}-manifold and has a GdG_{d}-action.
(6)
For α=∙r1⋯rlq∈𝔸PSS∙q\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q} (resp. pr1⋯rl∙∈𝔸SSPp∙pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}), define
BPSSα:=BPSSd∙r1×⋯×Bdrl​qB^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}:=B^{\rm PSS}_{d_{\bullet r_{1}}}\times\cdots\times B_{d_{r_{l}q}}
resp. BSSPα:=Bdp​r1×⋯×BSSPdrl∙.\text{resp. }B^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}:=B_{d_{pr_{1}}}\times\cdots\times B^{\rm SSP}_{d_{r_{l}\bullet}}.
Then BαPSSB^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha} (resp. BαSSPB^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}) has a smooth GαPSSG_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS} (resp. GαSSPG_{\alpha}^{\rm SSP}) action.
(7)
Just as Equation (5.11), under the group embedding GαPSS↪G∙qPSSG^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow G_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}, there is an equivariant embedding
BαPSS↪B∙qPSS,B^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow B^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},
which is part of the data of the embeddings of the form
BαPSS↪BβPSSB^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow B^{\rm PSS}_{\beta}
for any α≤β\alpha\leq\beta under the group embedding GαPSS↪GβPSSG_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}\hookrightarrow G_{\beta}^{\rm PSS}. This fact is also true after replacing PSS by SSP.
7.1.2. Description of global charts
The following statement allows to construct framings from solutions to the PSS/SSP equations. The proof is the same as the proof of Corollary 5.30 using the integrality assumptions.
Proposition 7.3.
Let (Σ,ΣPSS,𝐋)(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm PSS},{\bm{L}}) be a prestable PSS thimble, and suppose that
u:Σ→Mu:\Sigma\to M
is a stable PSS thimble, i.e. the restriction of uu to the irreducible component ΣPSS\Sigma_{\rm PSS} solves the PSS equation (4.6), its restriction to the cylindrical components on the right of ΣPSS\Sigma_{\rm PSS} solves the Floer equation (4.2), while its restriction to other components solves the genuine JJ-holomorphic curve equation, and uu has finite automorphism group. Consider the 22-form Ωu,PSS\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}} defined by
Ωu,PSS:={u∗​ω−d⁡(Hs,tPSS​(u)​d​t) on the distinguished component ΣPSSu∗​ω−d⁡(Ht​(u)​d​t) on cylindrical components on the right of ΣPSSu∗​ω on other irreducible components.\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{s,t}^{\rm PSS}(u)dt)&\text{ on the distinguished component $\Sigma_{\rm PSS}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{t}(u)dt)&\text{ on cylindrical components on the right of $\Sigma_{\rm PSS}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega&\text{ on other irreducible components.}\end{array}\right.
(7.1)
Then there exists a holomorphic Hermitian line bundle on Σ\Sigma whose curvature form is −2​π​𝐢​Ωu,PSS-2\pi{\bm{i}}\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}. Moreover, this line bundle is unique up to isomorphism.
Similarly, if (Σ,ΣSSP,𝐋)(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm SSP},{\bm{L}}) is a prestable SSP thimble and u:Σ→Mu:\Sigma\to M is a stable SSP thimble which solves the SSP equation (4.10) on ΣSSP\Sigma_{{\rm SSP}}, there exists a Hermitian line bundle over Σ\Sigma, unique up to isomorphism, such that the curvature of its Chern connection is equal to −2​π​𝐢​Ωu,PSS-2\pi{\bm{i}}\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}, where
Ωu,SSP:={u∗​ω−d⁡(Hs,tSSP​(u)​d​t) on the distinguished component ΣSSPu∗​ω−d⁡(Ht​(u)​d​t) on cylindrical components on the left of ΣSSPu∗​ω on other irreducible components.∎\Omega_{u,{\rm SSP}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{s,t}^{\rm SSP}(u)dt)&\text{ on the distinguished component $\Sigma_{\rm SSP}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{t}(u)dt)&\text{ on cylindrical components on the left of $\Sigma_{\rm SSP}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega&\text{ on other irreducible components.}\qed\end{array}\right.
Given a stable PSS (resp. SSP) thimble, let us denote the line bundle constructed as above by Lu,PSSL_{u,{\rm PSS}} (resp. Lu,SSPL_{u,{\rm SSP}}). Now we can introduce the concept of framed curves in the context of PSS/SSP map.
Definition 7.4.
Given a moduli space ℳ¯PSS∙q\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q} of stable PSS thimbles and a stratum α=∙r1⋯rlq\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q which corresponds to thimbles breaking at the orbits r1,…,rlr_{1},\dots,r_{l}, denoting d=d∙qd=d_{\bullet q}, a framed PSS thimble (of type α\alpha) is a tuple (u,Σ,F)(u,\Sigma,F) where
(1)
Σ\Sigma is a prestable PSS thimble with ll horizontal levels on the right of ΣPSS\Sigma_{\rm PSS}. (see Definition 7.1).
(2)
u:Σ→Mu:\Sigma\to M is a smooth map whose restriction to each cylindrical component on the right of ΣPSS\Sigma_{\rm PSS} (included) converges to periodic orbits prescribed by the capped orbits p,r1,…,rl,qp,r_{1},\dots,r_{l},q at ±∞\pm\infty in an exponential rate, and the topological energy of these levels are prescribed accordingly. Moreover, the 22-form Ωu,PSS\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}} defined in Equation (7.1) is non-negative and is strictly positive on each unstable component of uu.
(3)
F=(f0,f1,…,fd)F=(f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}) is basis of global sections of the line bundle Lu,PSSL_{u,{\rm PSS}} constructed using the 22-form (7.1). Moreover, the induced holomorphic map
ιF,PSS:Σ→ℂ​ℙd,w↦[f0​(w),…,fd​(w)]\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d},\ w\mapsto[f_{0}(w),\ldots,f_{d}(w)]
(7.2)
is a stable map, which represents a point [ιF,PSS]∈ℱ0,2PSS​(d)[\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}]\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d). Denote by
ι~F,PSS:Σ→𝒞PSS=𝒞0,2PSS​(d)\tilde{\iota}_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}={\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d)
to be the identification between Σ\Sigma and the fiber of the universal curve 𝒞0,2PSS​(d)→ℱ0,2PSS​(d){\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d)\to{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d) over [ιF,PSS][\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}]. This will also be called the domain map.
By replacing PSS with SSP, we obtain the definition of framed PSS thimble. Isomorphisms of framed PSS/SSP thimbles are defined in the same way as Definition 5.32. The compact Lie group P​U​(d+1)PU(d+1) acts on the space of framed PSS/SSP thimbles by changing the framing. The stabilizer of this P​U​(d+1)PU(d+1) action at (u,Σ,F)(u,\Sigma,F) agrees with the automorphism group of the map u:Σ→Mu:\Sigma\to M if uu is a stable PSS/SSP thimble.
Now we can repeat the global thickening construction in the setting of PSS/SSP maps as we did in the case for Floer trajectories. Let pp be a capped 11-periodic orbit. Fix d=d∙qd=d_{\bullet q}. Let
k1<⋯<kdk_{1}<\cdots<k_{d}
be a sequence of positive integers. Define V∙qPSS:=V∙qPSS​(k1,…,kd)V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}) be the moduli space of tuples
(Σ,u,F,η1,…,ηd)(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d})
where Σ\Sigma is a prestable PSS thimble, u:Σ→Mu:\Sigma\to M is a smooth map with topological energy dd converging to qq at the marked point z+z_{+}, FF is a frame of the line bundle Lu,PSS→ΣL_{u,{\rm PSS}}\to\Sigma inducing a holomorphic map ιF,PSS:Σ→ℂ​ℙd\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d} such that the framing F=(f0,…,fd)F=(f_{0},\dots,f_{d}) satisfies [f0(z−):⋯:fd(z−)]=[1:0:⋯:0][f_{0}(z_{-}):\cdots:f_{d}(z_{-})]=[1:0:\cdots:0], and the corresponding domain map ι~F,PSS:Σ→𝒞PSS\tilde{\iota}_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}, and (denoting by u~=(u,ι~F,PSS):Σ→M×𝒞PSS\tilde{u}=(u,\tilde{\iota}_{F,{\rm PSS}}):\Sigma\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS})
ηi∈u~∗​Ei=Γ⁡(Hom¯​(ιF,PSS∗​T​ℂ​ℙd,u∗​TM⊕⨁j=1i−1u~∗​Ej)⊗ιF,PSS∗​𝒪​(ki))⊗H0​(ιF,PSS∗​𝒪​(ki))0¯\eta_{i}\in\tilde{u}^{*}E_{i}=\Gamma\left(\overline{\rm Hom}\big(\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{i-1}\tilde{u}^{*}E_{j}\big)\otimes\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})\right)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i}))_{0}}
satisfying the following equations
{∂¯J,HPSS​u+πu∗​T​M​(∑i=1d⟨ηi⟩∘d​ιF,PSS)=0,∂¯​ηi+πEi​(∑j=i+1d⟨ηj⟩∘d​ιF,PSS)=0,i=1,…,d,\left\{\begin{aligned} \overline{\partial}_{J,H^{\rm PSS}}u+\pi_{u^{*}TM}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle\eta_{i}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}\right)&\ =0,\\
\overline{\partial}\eta_{i}+\pi_{E_{i}}\left(\sum_{j=i+1}^{d}\langle\eta_{j}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}\right)&\ =0,\ i=1,\ldots,d,\end{aligned}\right.
(7.3)
where EiE_{i} is defined in the same way as (5.31), and the ∂¯\overline{\partial}-operator ∂¯J,HPSS\overline{\partial}_{J,H^{\rm PSS}} is read off from the PSS equation 4.6.
By replacing PSS with SSP, we can similarly define the thickened moduli space Vp∙SSP=Vp∙SSP(k1,…,kd)V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d}) for a capped orbit pp.
The space V∙qPSSV_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} (resp. Vp∙SSPV_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}) admits a G∙qPSS=Gd∙qG_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=G_{d_{\bullet q}} (resp. Gp∙SSP=Gdp∙G_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=G_{d_{p\bullet}}) action extending the action on the framing. The obstruction bundle over E∙qPSS→V∙q∙E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to V_{\bullet q}^{\bullet} also has a direct sum decomposition
E∙qPSS=O∙qPSS⊕Q∙qPSSE_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=O_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\oplus Q_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
where O∙qPSSO_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} is specified by (5.33), and Q∙qPSSQ_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} is the trivial vector bundle from 𝑸d∙q{\bm{Q}}_{d_{\bullet q}} as in (5.12). This is a G∙qPSSG^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}-equivariant vector bundle. As for the Kuranishi section, it is defined by
S∙qPSS:V∙qPSS→E∙qPSS[Σ,u,F,η]↦(η,HF)\begin{split}S_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}&\ \to E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\\
[\Sigma,u,F,\eta]&\ \mapsto(\eta,H_{F})\end{split}
where HF∈Q∙qPSSH_{F}\in Q_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} is represented by the Hermitian matrix whose entries are
∫Σ⟨fi,fj⟩Lu,PSS​Ωu,PSS,\int_{\Sigma}\langle f_{i},f_{j}\rangle_{L_{u,{\rm PSS}}}\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}},
where f0,…,fdf_{0},\dots,f_{d} is a basis representing the frame FF. It is obvious that there exists a homeomorphism
ψ∙qPSS:(S∙qPSS)−1(0)/G∙qPSS→∼ℳ¯.PSS∙q\psi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:(S_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS})^{-1}(0)/G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}.
This finishes the description of the K-chart
K∙qPSS:=K∙qPSS​(k1,…,kd):=(G∙qPSS,V∙qPSS,E∙qPSS,S∙qPSS,ψ∙qPSS).K_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=K_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}):=(G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS},E^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},S_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS},\psi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}).
The same description applies to the SSP moduli space and we obtain the K-chart
Kp∙SSP:=Kp∙SSP=(Gp∙SSP,Vp∙SSP,Ep∙SSP,Sp∙SSP,ψp∙SSP)K_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:=K_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=(G^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet},V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP},E_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP},S_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP},\psi_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP})
for the moduli space ℳ¯p∙SSP\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}.
The following statement follows from the proof of Theorem 5.47.
Proposition 7.5.
For fixed k1,…,kd−1k_{1},\dots,k_{d-1}, as long as kdk_{d} is sufficiently large, the thickened moduli spaces V∙qPSS=V∙qPSS​(k1,…,kd)V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}) and Vp∙SSP=Vp∙SSP(k1,…,kd)V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}) is regular near the zero locus of the Kuranishi section and E∙qPSS→V∙qPSSE_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}, Ep∙SSP→Vp∙SSPE_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\to V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP} are indeed vector bundles. ∎
7.1.3. The K-chart lift of the flow bimodule
We would like the system of K-charts obtained above provide a K-chart lift of the flow bimodule MPSSM^{\rm PSS} resp. MSSPM^{\rm SSP}. Still we describe the PSS case with more details. Recall that the moduli space (4.7) is a 𝔸∙qPSS{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}-space. Given a stratum α=∙r1⋯rlq\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q, the moduli space
∂αℳ¯∙qPSS=ℳ¯∙r1PSS×ℳ¯r1​r2Floer×⋯×ℳ¯Floerrl​q\partial^{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet r_{1}}^{\rm PSS}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{1}r_{2}}^{\rm Floer}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{{\rm Floer}}_{r_{l}q}
has a product K-chart
KαPSS:=K∙r1PSS×Kr1​r2×⋯×Krl​qK_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}:=K_{\bullet r_{1}}^{\rm PSS}\times K_{r_{1}r_{2}}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{l}q}
where Kri​ri+1K_{r_{i}r_{i+1}} is the K-chart constructed in (5.37) for ℳ¯ri​ri+1Floer\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}^{\rm Floer}. Similarly, for α=pr1⋯rl∙\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet, the moduli space
∂αℳ¯p∙SSP=ℳ¯Floerp​r1×⋯×ℳ¯rl−1​rlFloer×ℳ¯SSPrl∙\partial^{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm Floer}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{l-1}r_{l}}^{\rm Floer}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm SSP}_{r_{l}\bullet}
has a product K-chart
KαSSP:=Kp​r1×⋯×Krl−1​rl×Krl∙SSP.K_{\alpha}^{\rm SSP}:=K_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{l-1}r_{l}}\times K_{r_{l}\bullet}^{\rm SSP}.
Recall that the flow category TFloerT^{\rm Floer} is endowed with a weak K-chart lift as from Theorem 5.11 of the form
{Kp​q=(Gp​q,Vp​q,Ep​q,Sp​q,ψp​q)}p<q,\big\{K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq},\psi_{pq})\big\}_{p<q},
together with the collection of weak K-chart embeddings
{𝜾p​r​q:Kp​r×Kr​q⇀∂p​r​qKp​q}p<r<q.\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:K_{pr}\times K_{rq}\rightharpoonup\partial^{prq}K_{pq}\big\}_{p<r<q}.
Theorem 7.6.
For a capped 11-periodic orbits pp resp. qq, there exist a collection of weak K-chart embeddings
{𝜾∙r​q:K∙rPSS×Kr​q⇀∂∙r​qK∙qPSS}r<q\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\bullet rq}:K_{\bullet r}^{\rm PSS}\times K_{rq}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\bullet rq}K_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\big\}_{r<q}
resp. {𝜾pr∙:Kp​r×Kr∙SSP⇀∂pr∙Kp∙SSP}p<r,\text{resp. }\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{pr\bullet}:K_{pr}\times K^{\rm SSP}_{r\bullet}\rightharpoonup\partial^{pr\bullet}K_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\big\}_{p<r},
such that the following statements are true.
(1)
The following diagrams are commutative under the chart embeddings:
K∙rPSS×Kr​s×Ks​q\textstyle{K^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet r}\times K_{rs}\times K_{sq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}∂∙r​sK∙sPSS×Ks​q\textstyle{\partial^{\bullet rs}K_{\bullet s}^{\rm PSS}\times K_{sq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}K∙rPSS×∂r​s​qKr​q\textstyle{K_{\bullet r}^{\rm PSS}\times\partial^{rsq}K_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}∂∙r​s​qK∙qPSS,\textstyle{\partial^{\bullet rsq}K^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},}
Kr​s×Ks​p×KSSPp∙\textstyle{K_{rs}\times K_{sp}\times K^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}∂r​s​pKr​p×KSSPp∙\textstyle{\partial^{rsp}K_{rp}\times K^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}Kr​s×∂sp∙KSSPs∙\textstyle{K_{rs}\times\partial^{sp\bullet}K^{\rm SSP}_{s\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}∂∙r​s​pK∙pSSP.\textstyle{\partial^{\bullet rsp}K^{\rm SSP}_{\bullet p}.}
(2)
For each stratum α=∙r1⋯rlq∈𝔸∙qPSS\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} resp. α=pr1⋯rl∙∈𝔸p∙SSP\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet\in{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}, define the product chart
KαPSS:=K∙r1PSS×⋯×Krl​qK_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}:=K_{\bullet r_{1}}^{\rm PSS}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{l}q}
resp.KSSPα:=Kp​r1×⋯×KSSPrl∙.{\rm resp.}K^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}:=K_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times K^{\rm SSP}_{r_{l}\bullet}.
Then for any pair α≤β\alpha\leq\beta in 𝔸∙qPSS{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} resp. 𝔸p∙SSP{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}, there are induced product weak embeddings
𝜾β​αPSS:KαPSS⇀∂αKβPSS​ resp. ​𝜾β​αSSP:KαSSP⇀∂αKβSSP.{\bm{\iota}}^{\rm PSS}_{\beta\alpha}:K^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\alpha}K^{\rm PSS}_{\beta}\text{\ resp. }{\bm{\iota}}^{\rm SSP}_{\beta\alpha}:K^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\alpha}K^{\rm SSP}_{\beta}.
Then the system
{KαPSS}α∈𝔸∙qPSS resp. {KαSSP}α∈𝔸p∙SSP\{K^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}}\text{ resp. }\{K^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}}
defines a weak K-chart presentation of the 𝔸∙qPSS{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}-space ℳ¯∙qPSS\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} resp. the 𝔸p∙SSP{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}-space ℳ¯p∙SSP\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}.
(3)
After outer-collaring, one can match up the Kuranishi sections so that the weak K-chart presentation is a genuine K-chart presentation.
(4)
The natural projection π∙qPSS:V∙qPSS→Bd∙qPSS\pi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to B_{d_{\bullet q}}^{\rm PSS} resp. πp∙SSP:Vp∙SSP→Bdp∙SSP\pi_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\to B_{d_{p\bullet}}^{\rm SSP} has a Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} equivariant fiberwise smooth bundle structure.
Sketch of proof.
The embeddings 𝜾∙r​qPSS{\bm{\iota}}_{\bullet rq}^{\rm PSS} and 𝜾pr∙SSP{\bm{\iota}}_{pr\bullet}^{\rm SSP} are defined in a similar way as in Section 5.4 when we embed a product of K-charts of a list of Floer moduli spaces. It is routine to check that these embeddings satisfy the listed commutative diagrams and give weak K-chart presentations of the corresponding PSS and SSP moduli spaces. Notice that for the same reason, the product Kuranishi section only matches with the boundary restriction of the larger Kuranishi chart in the OO-summand. We can use outer-collaring and an interpolation to match their QQ-summand. Lastly, the projection π∙qPSS\pi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} and πp∙SSP\pi_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP} have the structure of Cloc1C_{\rm loc}^{1} equivariant fiberwise smooth bundles for the same reason as in the case of Floer trajectories.
∎
7.1.4. Scaffolding
We could state and prove a proposition similar to Proposition 5.64. However, instead of doing the tedious thing, we only describe how the existing scaffoldings for the K-chart lift of (TFloer)+(T^{\rm Floer})^{+} can be extended. Notice that we will not use any object or structure we chose in Section 6. Essentially (for the PSS case), we would like to choose subbundles
F∙q,αPSS⊂O∙q|VαPSSF_{\bullet q,\alpha}^{\rm PSS}\subset O_{\bullet q}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}}
and define stabilization maps
StabF∙q,α​(VαPSS)→∂αV∙qPSS{\rm Stab}_{F_{\bullet q,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
whose G∙qG_{\bullet q}-equivariantization is an open embedding. (We skip the discussion of the companion bundle isomorphisms.) This bundle F∙q,αF_{\bullet q,\alpha} is indeed the orthogonal complement of EαPSSE_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS} inside E∙qPSSE_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}; a good choice of an inner products can be made inductively as in the proof of Proposition 5.64 which extend the inner products we have already chosen on the obstruction bundles over the thickend Floer moduli spaces. Then the stabilization map is still constructed using the implicit function theorem which depends on choosing a family of local Banach manifold charts, a family of approximate solutions, and a family of right inverses. These objects can all be extended from the existing ones we have chosen for the Floer moduli spaces. Therefore a compatible system of stabilization maps can be constructed. Of course, the collar structure is necessary for the construction.
7.1.5. Stable smoothing
We would like to construct a system of stable equivariant smoothings of V∙qPSSV_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} resp. Vp∙SSPV_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}. Notice that we have chosen stabilizations by Gp​qG_{pq}-representations 𝑹p​q{\bm{R}}_{pq} for all p<qp<q in 𝒫Floer{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}. The method we used in Subsection 6.4 can be used again here to construct stabilizations 𝑹∙qPSS{\bm{R}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} and to construct smoothings on V^∙qPSS:=V∙qPSS×𝑹∙qPSS\widehat{V}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\times{\bm{R}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} whose boundary is diffeomorphic to products of stabilized thickenings.
We can also require that the stabilizations satisfy the following property. Namely, there exist smooth submersive evaluation maps
ev∙:V^∙qPSS→M{\rm ev}_{\bullet}:\widehat{V}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to M
which coincides with the original evaluation map on V∙qPSSV_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}. Indeed, by observation of Abouzaid–McLean–Smith [AMS21, Lemma 4.5], for a single smooth Kuranishi chart, on a further stabilization there exists a smooth submersive extension of the evaluation map. Then when we inductively construct stable smoothings, we can always achieve this extra condition.
It follows that by intersecting with the unstable manifolds of the given Morse function f:M→ℝf:M\to{\mathbb{R}}, one obtains smooth Kuranishi charts
K^x​qPSS=(G∙qPSS,V^x​qPSS,E^x​qPSS,S^x​qPSS)\widehat{K}_{xq}^{\rm PSS}=(G_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS},\widehat{V}_{xq}^{\rm PSS},\widehat{E}_{xq}^{\rm PSS},\widehat{S}_{xq}^{\rm PSS})
where V^x​qPSS⊂V^∙qPSS\widehat{V}_{xq}^{\rm PSS}\subset\widehat{V}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS} is the intersection with the (outer-collared) compactified unstable manifold Wu​(x¯)¯⊂M\overline{W^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}})}\subset M. It is straightforward to check that we obtained a smooth K-chart lift of the flow bimodule (MPSS)+(M^{\rm PSS})^{+}.
Then after taking quotient, we obtained a D-chart lift of (MPSS)+(M^{\rm PSS})^{+} equipped with a collar structure and a scaffolding. The straightening that is compatible with the collar structure and the scaffolding can be constructed in a way similar to the case of Floer trajectories.
7.1.6. Normal complex structures and orientaions
The construction of normal complex structure and orientation on K^x​qPSS\widehat{K}_{xq}^{\rm PSS} essentially follows from the proof of Theorem 6.32. and the relevant discussions in [AB21, Section 11]. Two remarks should be pointed out. First, the stabilization from [AMS21, Lemma 4.5] and the fiber product with unstable submanifolds does not affect the normal complex structure, because the changes on the tangent bundles are all included in the trivial summand of the corresponding representation of the isotropy group. Second, when constructing orientation structures, the virtual vector bundle associated with an object x∈TMorsex\in T^{\rm Morse} is defined by (T​Wu​(x¯),0)(TW^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}}),0).
7.2. Pearly bimodule
Consider the pearly bimodule defined in Section 4. Here we describe a K-chart lift of it and show how to use the same argument to construct a stable smoothing. First, for each dd, consider an auxiliary moduli space which is similar to BdB_{d}, BdPSSB_{d}^{\rm PSS}, and BdSSPB_{d}^{\rm SSP} defined before. Indeed, let ℱ0,2pearl​(d){\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d) be the moduli space of parametrized 2-marked holomorphic spheres into ℂ​ℙd\mathbb{CP}^{d} which have degree dd and whose images are not entirely contained in a hyperplane. In particular, each point of ℱ0,2pearl​(d){\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d) is represented by a stable map with 2 marked points that have a distinguished component whose parametrization is fixed. ℱ0,2pearl​(d){\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d) is a smooth manifold (with no boundary or corners). Moreover there are holomorphic submersions
ev±:ℱ0,2pearl​(d)→ℂ​ℙd.{\rm ev}_{\pm}:{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}.
Let
Bdpearl⊂ℱ0,2pearl​(d)B_{d}^{\rm pearl}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)
be the subset of elements whose evaluation at z−z_{-} is [1,0,…,0]∈ℂ​ℙd[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}. Then BdpearlB_{d}^{\rm pearl} is a smooth manifold. There is a Gd≅U⁡(d)G_{d}\cong U(d)-action on BdpearlB_{d}^{\rm pearl}.
Now consider the corresponding space ℳ¯(d)pearl∙⁣∙\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d) of stable parametrized holomorphic spheres in MM whose degree is dd. Using multi-layered thickening associated to a sequence of integers k1<k2<⋯<kd<⋯k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{d}<\cdots one can produce a similar thickened moduli space
K∙⁣∙pearl​(d)=(Gd,V∙⁣∙pearl​(d),E∙⁣∙pearl​(d),S∙⁣∙pearl).K_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)=(G_{d},V_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d),E_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d),S_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}).
The domain V∙⁣∙pearl​(d)V_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d) admits a GdG_{d}-action and an equivariant projection map
π∙⁣∙pearl:V∙⁣∙pearl​(d)→Bdpearl.\pi_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}:V_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)\to B_{d}^{\rm pearl}.
Moreover, by taking intersections with stable and unstable submanifolds of the Morse function f:M→ℝf:M\to{\mathbb{R}}, one obtains stratified charts
Kx​ypearl=(Gx​y,Vx​ypearl,Ex​ypearl,Sx​ypearl)K_{xy}^{\rm pearl}=(G_{xy},V_{xy}^{\rm pearl},E_{xy}^{\rm pearl},S_{xy}^{\rm pearl})
This collection of charts obviously defines a K-chart lift of the pearly bimodule MpearlM^{\rm pearl}.
Theorem 7.7.
There exists a “single-layered” normally complex derived orbifold lift of the outer-collared pearly bimodule (Mpearl)+(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}.
Sketch of Proof.
We can construct stable smoothings of K∙⁣∙pearl​(d)K_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d) individualy for each d≥1d\geq 1 to obtain smooth Kuranishi charts. To save notations, still denote them by K∙⁣∙pearl​(d)K_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d). We can do a further stabilization such that the evaluations at z±z_{\pm} are smooth submersions onto MM. Hence each Kx​ypearlK_{xy}^{\rm pearl} becomes smooth stratified Kuranishi charts. Its outer-collaring is again smooth Kuranishi charts. The scaffoldings are trivial as the Morse theory part is already regular. Then taking quotient by Gx​yG_{xy} one obtains a D-chart lift of (Mpearl)+(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}, denoted by (𝔇pearl)+({\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl})^{+}. As the scaffolding is trivial, this is a single-layered lift. Lastly, there is a normal complex structure and an orientation on (𝔇pearl)+({\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl})^{+} for the same reason as the case of [AMS21].
∎
7.3. The homotopy
Lastly, we explain the collection of D-chart lifts of the system of moduli spaces referred to as the “homotopy moduli spaces.” Although this ad hoc collection lacks a formal package, the constructions are almost identical to previous cases. The global chart construction, including the multi-layered strategy, is the same as the case of Floer trajectories or PSS/SSP cases. Then one obtains a weak K-chart lift of the collection of moduli spaces. By taking outer-collaring and interpolating between the product Kuranishi section and the boundary restriction of Kuranishi sections, one obtains a K-chart lift of the outer-collared system. The construction of scaffolding (see Proposition 5.64) has completely the same induction procedure. We can also run the smoothing procedure without essential differences.
Appendix A Product of canonical Whitney stratifications
A.1. Basics of Whitney stratifications
We review the basic notions of (Whitney) prestratifications and stratification. Following Mather [Mat73], our convention is the same as our previous work [BX22].
Definition A.1.
Let SS be a topological space. A prestratification on SS is a decomposition
S=⨆α∈𝔄SαS=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{A}}}S_{\alpha}
of SS into the disjoint union of locally closed subsets satisfying the following condition
(1)
The decomposition is locally finite.
(2)
(Axiom of frontier) If α,β∈𝔄\alpha,\beta\in{\mathfrak{A}}, Sα∩Sβ¯≠∅S_{\alpha}\cap\overline{S_{\beta}}\neq\emptyset, then Sα⊂Sβ¯S_{\alpha}\subset\overline{S_{\beta}}.
Each SαS_{\alpha} is called a stratum of the prestratification. The axiom of frontier induces a partial order among strata: Sα≤SβS_{\alpha}\leq S_{\beta} if Sα⊂Sβ¯S_{\alpha}\subset\overline{S_{\beta}}. We use the symbol 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}} to denote the prestratification as well as the partially ordered set of strata. A space equipped with a prestratification 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}} is called an 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}}-stratified space.
Now we consider the notion of stratifications. Two subsets A,B⊆MA,B\subseteq M are called equivalent at x∈Mx\in M if there exists an open neighborhood UxU_{x} of xx such that A∩Ux=B∩UxA\cap U_{x}=B\cap U_{x}. An equivalence class is called a setgerm at xx. Given a prestratification 𝒜{\mathcal{A}} on S⊆MS\subseteq M, it assigns to each x∈Sx\in S a setgerm represented by the unique stratum that contains xx. A stratification of a subset S⊂MS\subset M is a rule which assigns to each point x∈Sx\in S a set-germ SxS_{x}, such that for each x∈Sx\in S, there is an open neighborhood UxU_{x} of xx and a prestratification of Ux∩SU_{x}\cap S such that the setgerm-valued function restricted to UxU_{x} is induced from this prestratification.
Definition A.2 (Whitney stratification).
Let MM be a smooth manifold.
(1)
Given two disjoint smooth submanifolds SS, S′S^{\prime}, we say that the pair (S,S′)(S,S^{\prime}) satisfies Whitney’s condition (b) at x∈S′∩S¯x\in S^{\prime}\cap\overline{S} if the following is true: suppose xi∈Sx_{i}\in S, xi′∈S′x_{i}^{\prime}\in S^{\prime} are two sequences converging to x∈S¯∩S′x\in\overline{S}\cap S^{\prime}. Suppose the sequence of tangent spaces Txi​ST_{x_{i}}S converges to a subspace H⊂Tx​MH\subset T_{x}M and the sequence of secant lines xi​xi′¯\overline{x_{i}x_{i}^{\prime}} converges to a line L⊂Tx​ML\subset T_{x}M, then L⊂HL\subset H.
(2)
Let S⊂MS\subset M be a subset. A prestratification on SS is called a Whitney prestratification if each stratum SαS_{\alpha} is a smooth submanifold and each pair (Sα,Sβ)(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta}) of strata with Sβ⊊Sα¯S_{\beta}\subsetneq\overline{S_{\alpha}} satisfies Whitney’s condition (b) at every point of SβS_{\beta}.
(3)
A stratification on S⊂MS\subset M is called a Whitney stratification if for each point x∈Sx\in S there exists an open neighborhood Ux⊂SU_{x}\subset S of xx and a Whitney prestratification on UxU_{x} which induces this stratification inside UxU_{x}. We use the symbol 𝒮{\mathcal{S}} to denote a Whitney stratification on SS, which is a setgerm-valued function on SS.
On the same set there could be many different Whitney stratifications. One can use a partial order among them to compare. Suppose 𝒮1{\mathcal{S}}_{1} and 𝒮2{\mathcal{S}}_{2} are two Whitney stratifications, which assign to each xx setgerms S1,xS_{1,x} and S2,xS_{2,x} respectively. Then define
Si(k)={x∈S|dimℝSi,x≤k},i=1,2.S_{i}^{(k)}=\{x\in S\ |\ {\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}S_{i,x}\leq k\},\ i=1,2.
Then one has descending sequence of closed sets
Si=Si(m)⊇Si(m−1)⊇⋯⊇Si(0)S_{i}=S_{i}^{(m)}\supseteq S_{i}^{(m-1)}\supseteq\cdots\supseteq S_{i}^{(0)}
where m=dimℝ​Mm={\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}M.
Definition A.3.
𝒮1<𝒮2{\mathcal{S}}_{1}<{\mathcal{S}}_{2} if there exists an integer kk such that
S1(l)=S2(l)​∀l>k​and​S1(k)⊊S2(k).S_{1}^{(l)}=S_{2}^{(l)}\ \forall l>k\ {\rm and}\ S_{1}^{(k)}\subsetneq S_{2}^{(k)}.
If a Whitney stratification is minimal, then it is unique. Moreover, a minimal Whitney stratification on S⊆MS\subseteq M is invariant under diffeomorphisms of MM which preserve SS set-wise (see [BX22, Lemma A.11]).
Theorem A.4.
[Whi65]
Given a smooth complex algebraic variety and S⊆MS\subseteq M a constructible set. There exists a minimal Whitney stratification which is induced from a Whitney prestratification whose strata are all smooth complex algebraic submanifolds.
We sketch the constructive proof following Mather [Mat73] as this construction will be needed for further discussions. Suppose the (real) dimension of MM is mm. Then we construct inductively a sequence of closed algebraic subsets
S=S(m)⊇S(m−1)⊃⋯⊇S(0)S=S^{(m)}\supseteq S^{(m-1)}\supset\cdots\supseteq S^{(0)}
satisfying the following conditions.
(1)
Each S(l)S^{(l)} is a closed algebraic subset of MM of real dimension at most ll.
(2)
Each S̊(l):=S(l)∖S(l−1)\mathring{S}^{(l)}:=S^{(l)}\setminus S^{(l-1)} is a smooth complex algebraic submanifold of (real) dimension ll. It can empty, for example, when ll is odd.
(3)
For each pair l>kl>k, the pair (S̊(l),S̊(k))(\mathring{S}^{(l)},\mathring{S}^{(k)}) satisfies Whitney’s condition (b) at every point of S̊(k)\mathring{S}^{(k)}.
Indeed, suppose S(m),⋯,S(k)S^{(m)},\cdots,S^{(k)} have been constructed. Then we define S(k−1)⊂S(k)S^{(k-1)}\subset S^{(k)} to be the subset of points x∈S(k)x\in S^{(k)} satisfying one of the following conditions.
(1)
xx is a singular point of S(k)S^{(k)} or a regular point of S(k)S^{(k)} with local (real) dimension strictly less than kk.
(2)
xx is a regular point of S(k)S^{(k)} with local dimension kk and there exists l>kl>k such that the pair (S̊(l),Sr​e​g(k))(\mathring{S}^{(l)},S^{(k)}_{reg}) does not satisfy Whitney’s condition (b) at xx. Here Sr​e​g(k)⊆S(k)S_{reg}^{(k)}\subseteq S^{(k)} is the Zariski open subset of regular points of local dimension kk.
Basic algebraic geometry implies that the set of points satisfying the first condition above is a closed algebraic set of real dimension strictly less than kk. On the other hand, it is a fundamental theorem of Whitney that the set of points satisfying the second condition above is also a closed algebraic set of real dimension strictly less than kk. Then this produces a Whitney prestratification whose strata are all connected components of all S̊(k)\mathring{S}^{(k)}. It is not difficult to prove that this Whitney stratification is the minimal one.
A.2. Product of canonical Whitney stratifications
Now we consider if the product of canonical Whitney stratifications on a product algebraic set is the canonical one. Let M1,M2M_{1},M_{2} be smooth complex algebraic varieties and S1⊆M1S_{1}\subseteq M_{1}, S2⊆M2S_{2}\subseteq M_{2} be complex algebraic subsets, equipped with the canonical Whitney stratification of with levels Si(k)S_{i}^{(k)}. Denote
M=M1×M2,\displaystyle\ M=M_{1}\times M_{2},\
S=S1×S2.\displaystyle\ S=S_{1}\times S_{2}.
For each k≥0k\geq 0, denote
S̊×(k):=⋃k1+k2=kS̊1(k1)×S̊2(k2).\mathring{S}_{\times}^{(k)}:=\bigcup_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})}.
It is easy to see that the prestratification on SS whose strata are all connected components of all S̊×(k)\mathring{S}_{\times}^{(k)} is a Whitney prestratification. Denote the associated Whitney stratification by 𝒮×{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}. It is easy to see the induced dimension filtration on SS is given by
S×(k)=⋃k1+k2=kS1(k1)×S2(k2).S_{\times}^{(k)}=\bigcup_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}S_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(k_{2})}.
Proposition A.5.
The Whitney stratification 𝒮×{\mathcal{S}}_{\times} on SS is the minimal (canonical) one.
Proof.
We know that the canonical Whitney stratification, denoted by 𝒮{\mathcal{S}}, is the minimal one. Then one has 𝒮≤𝒮×{\mathcal{S}}\leq{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}. Suppose this is not an equality. Then by definition (see Definition A.3), there exists ll such that
S(k)=S×(k)​∀k>l,S(l)⊊S×(l).S^{(k)}=S_{\times}^{(k)}\ \forall k>l,\ S^{(l)}\subsetneq S_{\times}^{(l)}.
Therefore, there exists a point
x=(x1,x2)∈S(l+1)=S×(l+1)=⋃l1+l2=l+1S1(l1)×S2(l2),x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in S^{(l+1)}=S_{\times}^{(l+1)}=\bigcup_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l+1}S_{1}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(l_{2})},
x∈S×(l)x\in S_{\times}^{(l)}, x∉S(l)x\notin S^{(l)}. Then by the construction of the canonical Whitney stratification, xx is a regular point of S(l+1)S^{(l+1)} of local dimension l+1l+1. Then xx is a regular point of the union of S1(l1)×S2(l2)S_{1}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(l_{2})} for all possible l1+l2=l+1l_{1}+l_{2}=l+1. Then for some l1,l2l_{1},l_{2} with l1+l2=l+1l_{1}+l_{2}=l+1, x=(x1,x2)x=(x_{1},x_{2}) is a regular point of S1(l1)×S2(l2)S_{1}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(l_{2})} with local dimension l+1l+1. This implies that xix_{i} is a regular point of local dimension lil_{i} in each factor.
However, x∈S×(l)x\in S_{\times}^{(l)} also implies that either x1∈S1(l1−1)x_{1}\in S_{1}^{(l_{1}-1)} or x2∈S2(l2−1)x_{2}\in S_{2}^{(l_{2}-1)}. Without loss of generality, we assume x1∈S1(l1−1)x_{1}\in S_{1}^{(l_{1}-1)}. Then as x1x_{1} is a regular point of S1(l1)S_{1}^{(l_{1})}, it implies that for some k1>l1k_{1}>l_{1}, Whitney’s condition (b) for (S̊1(k1),S1,r​e​g(l1))(\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})},S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}) fails at x1x_{1}. Then there exists a sequence y1,ν∈S̊1(k1)y_{1,\nu}\in\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})} and a sequence x1,ν∈S1,r​e​g(l1)x_{1,\nu}\in S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}, both of which converging to x1x_{1} such that Ty1,ν​S̊1(k1)T_{y_{1,\nu}}\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})} converges to a subspace H1⊂Tx1​M1H_{1}\subset T_{x_{1}}M_{1}, the secant line x1,ν​y1,ν¯\overline{x_{1,\nu}y_{1,\nu}} converges to a line L1⊂Tx1​ML_{1}\subset T_{x_{1}}M, but L1⊈H1L_{1}\nsubseteq H_{1}. Now we separate the discussion in two scenarioes
(1)
If x2∈S̊2(l2)x_{2}\in\mathring{S}_{2}^{(l_{2})}, then consider the two sequences (x1,ν,x2)∈S1,r​e​g(l1)×S2,r​e​g(l2)⊂Sr​e​g(l+1)(x_{1,\nu},x_{2})\in S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2,reg}^{(l_{2})}\subset S^{(l+1)}_{reg}, (y1,ν,x2)∈S̊1(k1)×S̊2(l2)⊂S̊(k1+l2)(y_{1,\nu},x_{2})\in\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times\mathring{S}_{2}^{(l_{2})}\subset\mathring{S}^{(k_{1}+l_{2})}. Then one can see that Whitney’s condition (b) fails for (S̊(k1+l2),Sr​e​g(l+1))(\mathring{S}^{(k_{1}+l_{2})},S_{reg}^{(l+1)}) at the limit x=(x1,x2)x=(x_{1},x_{2}), which is a contradiction that x∈S̊(l+1)x\in\mathring{S}^{(l+1)}.
(2)
Suppose x2∉S̊2(l2)x_{2}\notin\mathring{S}_{2}^{(l_{2})}. Then by construction, there exist some k2>l2k_{2}>l_{2}, a sequence y2,ν∈S̊2(k2)y_{2,\nu}\in\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})} converging to x2x_{2}, a sequence x2,ν∈S2,r​e​g(l2)x_{2,\nu}\in S_{2,reg}^{(l_{2})} converging to x2x_{2}, such that the sequence of tangent spaces Ty2,ν​S̊2(k2)T_{y_{2,\nu}}\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})} converges to H2H_{2} and the sequence of secant lines x2,ν​y2,ν¯\overline{x_{2,\nu}y_{2,\nu}} converges to a line L2L_{2} but L2L_{2} is not contained in H2H_{2}. Then consider the sequence of points yν=(y1,ν,y2,ν)∈S̊1(k1)×S̊2(k2)⊂S̊(k1+k2)y_{\nu}=(y_{1,\nu},y_{2,\nu})\in\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})}\subset\mathring{S}^{(k_{1}+k_{2})}, the sequence of points xν=(x1,ν,x2,ν)∈S1,r​e​g(l1)×S2,r​e​g(l2)⊂Sr​e​g(l+1+l2)=Sr​e​g(l+1)x_{\nu}=(x_{1,\nu},x_{2,\nu})\in S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2,reg}^{(l_{2})}\subset S_{reg}^{(l+1+l_{2})}=S_{reg}^{(l+1)}. This breaks the hypothesis that x∈S̊(l+1)x\in\mathring{S}^{(l+1)}.
∎
A.3. Relative case
Now we consider the relative case. Let SS be equipped with a prestratification 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}}
S=⨆α∈𝔄SαS=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{A}}}S_{\alpha}
such that each stratum SαS_{\alpha} is algebraic. A Whitney stratification 𝒮{\mathcal{S}} on SS is said to respect the given prestratification if for each x∈Sx\in S, the germ SxS_{x} is contained in the stratum SαS_{\alpha} which contains xx. We call such Whitney stratifications 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}}-Whitney stratifications. We proved in [BX22] that there exists a unique minimal 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}}-Whitney stratification, which we call the canonical 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}}-Whitney stratification.
For the purpose of this paper, we need to verify that such canonical Whitney stratification is natural with respect to products. Let M,NM,N be two smooth complex algebraic varieties and S⊂MS\subset M, T⊂NT\subset N be constructible subsets. Given algebraic prestratifications
S=⨆α∈𝔄Sα,\displaystyle\ S=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{A}}}S_{\alpha},\
T=⨆β∈𝔅Tβ,\displaystyle\ T=\bigsqcup_{\beta\in{\mathfrak{B}}}T_{\beta},
one has an associated decomposition
R:=S×T=⨆(α,β)∈𝔄×𝔅T(α,β)=⨆(α,β)∈𝔄×𝔅Sα×Tβ.R:=S\times T=\bigsqcup_{(\alpha,\beta)\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}T_{(\alpha,\beta)}=\bigsqcup_{(\alpha,\beta)\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta}.
We can check that this is still a prestratification with algebraic strata. Moreover, the induced partial order on the set of strata 𝔄×𝔅{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}} is the product one:
(α,β)≤(α′,β′)⟺α≤α′​and​β≤β′.(\alpha,\beta)\leq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\Longleftrightarrow\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\ {\rm and}\ \beta\leq\beta^{\prime}.
Let the prestratification be denoted by 𝔄×𝔅{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}. On SS resp. TT there are minimal 𝔄{\mathfrak{A}}- resp. 𝔅{\mathfrak{B}}-Whitney stratification, whose product is also a Whitney stratification on R=S×TR=S\times T.
Proposition A.6.
The product Whitney stratification on R=S×TR=S\times T is the minimal 𝔄×𝔅{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}-Whitney stratification.
Proof.
We prove by induction. Proposition A.5 implies that for each top stratum (α0,β0)∈𝔄×𝔅(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0})\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}, the product Whitney stratification coincides with the minimal Whitney stratification on Sα0×Tβ0S_{\alpha_{0}}\times T_{\beta_{0}}. Now given a stratum (α,β)∈𝔄×𝔅(\alpha,\beta)\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}. Suppose for any (α′,β′)>(α,β)(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})>(\alpha,\beta), we have proved that the product Whitney stratification on Sα′×Tβ′S_{\alpha^{\prime}}\times T_{\beta^{\prime}} coincides with the restriction of the minimal (𝔄×𝔅)({\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}})-Whitney stratification. We would like to show that it is still the case for the pair (α,β)(\alpha,\beta).
Suppose our claim is false. Then there exists k≥0k\geq 0 such that
(Sα×Tβ)(l)=⋃p+q=lSα(p)×Tβ(q),∀l≥k+1,(Sα×Tβ)(k)⊊⋃p+q=kSα(p)×Tβ(q).(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(l)}=\bigcup_{p+q=l}S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)},\ \forall l\geq k+1,\ (S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k)}\subsetneq\bigcup_{p+q=k}S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)}.
Then one can choose a point
x∈(Sα×Tβ)(k+1)∖(Sα×Tβ)(k)x\in(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k+1)}\setminus(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k)}
(A.1)
such that
x∈⋃p+q=kSα(p)×Tβ(q).x\in\bigcup_{p+q=k}S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)}.
(A.2)
By the construction of the minimal Whitney stratifications on constructible sets (sketched after Theorem A.4), one knows that x=(y,z)x=(y,z) is a regular point of (Sα×Tβ)(k+1)(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k+1)} of local dimension k+1k+1. This implies that for some p+q=k+1p+q=k+1, xx is a regular point of Sα(p)×Tβ(q)S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)} of local dimension k+1k+1. Then yy resp. zz is a regular point of Sα(p)S_{\alpha}^{(p)} resp. Tβ(q)T_{\beta}^{(q)} of dimension pp resp. qq. Moreover, (A.2) implies that either y∈Sα(p−1)y\in S_{\alpha}^{(p-1)} or z∈Tβ(q−1)z\in T_{\beta}^{(q-1)}.
We claim that yy is not in the boundary of any S̊α′(p′)\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})} with α′>α\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha and p′≤pp^{\prime}\leq p. If it is the case, assume that z∈T̊β(q′)z\in\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q^{\prime})} for some q′≤qq^{\prime}\leq q. Then we see that
x=(y,z)∈∂S̊α′(p′)×T̊β(q′)⊂∂(S̊α′(p′)×T̊β(q′))⊂∂R̊(α′,β)(p′+q′).x=(y,z)\in\partial\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}\times\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q^{\prime})}\subset\partial\left(\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}\times\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q^{\prime})}\right)\subset\partial\mathring{R}_{(\alpha^{\prime},\beta)}^{(p^{\prime}+q^{\prime})}.
Notice that p′+q′≤k+1p^{\prime}+q^{\prime}\leq k+1 and (α′,β)>(α,β)(\alpha^{\prime},\beta)>(\alpha,\beta). This contradicts the fact that x∉R(α,β)(k)x\notin R_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{(k)}. Similarly, zz is not in the boundary of any T̊β′(q′)\mathring{T}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{(q^{\prime})} with β′>β\beta^{\prime}>\beta and q′≤qq^{\prime}\leq q.
Without loss of generality, assume we are in one of the following two scenarios.
(1)
y∈Sα(p−1)y\in S_{\alpha}^{(p-1)} and z∈T̊β(q)z\in\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q)}. Then we know that for some α′≥α\alpha^{\prime}\geq\alpha and p′≥p+1p^{\prime}\geq p+1, Whitey’s condition (b) fails for the pair (S̊α′(p′),Sα,r​e​g(p))(\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})},S_{\alpha,reg}^{(p)}) at yy. Then this implies that Whitney’s condition (b) fails for the pair
(R̊(α′,β)(p′+q),R(α,β),r​e​g(p+q))\left(\mathring{R}_{(\alpha^{\prime},\beta)}^{(p^{\prime}+q)},R_{(\alpha,\beta),reg}^{(p+q)}\right)
at x=(y,z)x=(y,z). This contradicts the assumption that x∉R(α,β)(k)x\notin R_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{(k)}.
(2)
y∈Sα(p−1)y\in S_{\alpha}^{(p-1)} and z∈Tβ(q−1)z\in T_{\beta}^{(q-1)}. Then we know for some α′≥α\alpha^{\prime}\geq\alpha, β′≥β\beta^{\prime}\geq\beta, p′≥p+1p^{\prime}\geq p+1, q′≥q+1q^{\prime}\geq q+1, Whitney’s condition (b) fails for the pair (S̊α′(p′)×T̊β′(q′),Sα,r​e​g(p)×Tβ,r​e​g(q))(\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}\times\mathring{T}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{(q^{\prime})},S_{\alpha,reg}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta,reg}^{(q)}) at (y,z)(y,z). It follows that Whitney’s condition (b) fails for the pair
(R̊(α′,β′)(p′+q′),R(α,β),r​e​g(p+q)).\left(\mathring{R}_{(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})}^{(p^{\prime}+q^{\prime})},R_{(\alpha,\beta),reg}^{(p+q)}\right).
at x=(y,z)x=(y,z). This contradicts the assumption that x∉R(α,β)(k)x\notin R_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{(k)}.
∎
Appendix B Relative stable equivariant smoothing
Our construction in this paper relies on equivariant smoothings of infintely many moduli spaces. It is necessary to have an extension of the stable GG-smoothing results of Lashof [Las79] to a relative setting. Namely, if a GG-manifold MM has a GG-smoothing over an open set, then one can extend the existing smoothing to MM once the tangent microbundle admits a GG-vector bundle reduction which is compatible with the existing smoothing.
Lashof’s original construction relies on two technical results. The first one is Mostow’s embedding theorem [Mos57] and the second is Jaworowski’s extension theorem [Jaw76]. We need to recall these two theorems in order to obtain a generalization of Lashof’s results on stable GG-smoothing to the relative setting.
Lemma B.1.
[Mos57, Lemma 5.2]
Let GG be a compact Lie group of transformations on a metric space EE, and let T1,T2T_{1},T_{2} be invariant subsets with E=T1∪T2E=T_{1}\cup T_{2} and T2T_{2} be closed. Assume that there exist GG-equivariant topological embeddings φi:Ti→𝐑i\varphi_{i}:T_{i}\to{\bm{R}}_{i}, i=1,2i=1,2 where 𝐑1,𝐑2{\bm{R}}_{1},{\bm{R}}_{2} are orthogonal GG-spaces. Then there exists a GG-equivariant topological embedding φ:E→𝐑1⊕𝐑2\varphi:E\to{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2} such that
φ|T2=(0,φ2).\varphi|_{T_{2}}=(0,\varphi_{2}).
Theorem B.2.
[Jaw76, Theorem 2.2]
Let XX be a locally compact GG-space which can be equivariantly embedded into some finite-dimensional orthogonal GG-vector space. Suppose XX has only finitely many orbit types. Let A⊂XA\subset X be a closed GG-subspace and f:A→Yf:A\to Y be a continuous GG-map to a locally compact separable metrizable GG-space YY. If for each x∈X∖Ax\in X\setminus A the fixed point set YGxY^{G_{x}} is an ANR (absolute neighborhood retract), then ff can be extended to a continuous GG-map from a neighborhood of AA in XX.
Now we want to extend Lashof’s construction to a relative setting.
Theorem B.3.
Let MM be a topological GG-manifold with only finitely many orbit types. Let O⊂MO\subset M be an open subset such that its closure O¯\overline{O} is compact. Suppose that
(1)
the tangent microbundle Tμ​MT_{\mu}M of MM admits a GG-vector bundle reduction φ:Eμ→Tμ​M\varphi:E_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}M for a vector bundle E→ME\to M.
(2)
A GG-invariant open neighborhood UU of O¯\overline{O} is equipped with a GG-smoothing and E|UE|_{U} is equipped with a smooth GG-bundle structure such that the reduction φ\varphi is smooth over UU. Moreover, φ\varphi is in the same isotopy class of the canonical T​U→Tμ​UTU\to T_{\mu}U.
Then there exists a finite-dimensional orthogonal representation 𝐑{\bm{R}} of GG and a GG-smoothing α\alpha on M×𝐑M\times{\bm{R}} satisfying
(1)
The induced stable isotopy class of GG-vector bundle reductions of Tμ​(M×R)T_{\mu}(M\times R) coincides with the 𝑹{\bm{R}}-stabilization of φ\varphi.
(2)
There exists a GG-invariant open neighborhood U′U^{\prime} of O¯\overline{O} with U′¯⊂U\overline{U^{\prime}}\subset U, such that the restriction of the GG-smoothing α\alpha on U′×𝑹U^{\prime}\times{\bm{R}} is diffeomorphic to the given GG-smoothing on U′×𝑹U^{\prime}\times{\bm{R}}.
Proof.
We generalize the construction of Lashof [Las79] to the current setting. We first consider the case that E→ME\to M is trivial. Assume that
E=M×𝑾E=M\times{\bm{W}}
where 𝑾{\bm{W}} is an orthogonal GG-space. The vector bundle lift is given by a map
exp:M×𝑾→M×M\exp:M\times{\bm{W}}\to M\times M
which is smooth over U×𝑾U\times{\bm{W}}. For each x∈Mx\in M, denote the restriction of exp\exp to {x}×𝑾\{x\}\times{\bm{W}} by
expx:𝑾→{x}×M\exp_{x}:{\bm{W}}\to\{x\}\times M
which is a homeomorphism onto an open neighborhood of xx.
Now we prove the following claims.
Claim A. There exist an orthogonal GG-representation 𝐑{\bm{R}} and a GG-equivariant topological embedding ι:M→𝐑\iota:M\to{\bm{R}} which is smooth near O¯\overline{O}.
Proof of Claim A. As O¯\overline{O} is compact, by a theorem of Palais (see [Pal57, Theorem III]), there is a smooth GG-equivariant embedding from an open neighborhood of O¯\overline{O} into an orthogonal GG-space 𝑹1{\bm{R}}_{1}. We may assume that this embedding is defined and smooth over UU, denoted by ιU:U→𝑹1\iota_{U}:U\to{\bm{R}}_{1}. Let U′⊂UU^{\prime}\subset U be a GG-invariant open neighborhood of O¯\overline{O} such that U′¯⊂U\overline{U^{\prime}}\subset U. Then M′:=M∖U′¯M^{\prime}:=M\setminus\overline{U^{\prime}} is a topological GG-manifold with only finitely many orbit types. Hence by Mostow’s embedding theorem (Theorem B.1), there exists a GG-orthogonal representation 𝑹2{\bm{R}}_{2} and a GG-equivariant topological embedding ιM′:M′→𝑹2\iota_{M^{\prime}}:M^{\prime}\to{\bm{R}}_{2}. Then by Lemma B.1, writing M=U′¯∪M′M=\overline{U^{\prime}}\cup M^{\prime}, there exists a GG-equivariant topological embedding
ι:M→𝑹1⊕𝑹2\iota:M\to{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}
such that its restriction to U′¯\overline{U^{\prime}} coincides with (ιU,0)(\iota_{U},0). In particular, the restriction of ι\iota to U′U^{\prime} is smooth. Now define 𝑹:=𝑹1⊕𝑹2{\bm{R}}:={\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}. End of the proof of Claim A.
Claim B. There is a GG-invariant open neighborhood Nι⊂𝐑N_{\iota}\subset{\bm{R}} and a continuous GG-equivariant retraction r:Nι→ι⁡(M)r:N_{\iota}\to\iota(M) which is smooth near ι⁡(O¯)\iota(\overline{O}).
Proof of Claim B. Note that there exist a GG-invariant open neighborhood NU′⊂𝑹1N_{U^{\prime}}\subset{\bm{R}}_{1} of ιU​(U′)\iota_{U}(U^{\prime}) and a smooth GG-equivariant retraction
rU′:NU′¯→ιU​(U′).r_{U^{\prime}}:\overline{N_{U^{\prime}}}\to\iota_{U}(U^{\prime}).
Then rU′r_{U^{\prime}} induces a retraction from NU′¯×𝑹2\overline{N_{U^{\prime}}}\times{\bm{R}}_{2} to ι⁡(U′)\iota(U^{\prime}). Choose a smaller open neighborhood U′′U^{\prime\prime} of O¯\overline{O} with U′′¯⊂U′\overline{U^{\prime\prime}}\subset U^{\prime}. By applying Jaworowski’s extension theorem (Theorem B.2) to X=𝑹1⊕𝑹2X={\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}, A=(rU′−1​(U′′¯)×𝑹2)∪ι⁡(M)A=(r_{U^{\prime}}^{-1}(\overline{U^{\prime\prime}})\times{\bm{R}}_{2})\cup\iota(M), and Y=ι⁡(M)Y=\iota(M), one obtains an extension, i.e., a GG-invariant open neighborhood NιN_{\iota} of ι⁡(M)\iota(M) and a GG-invariant retraction r:Nι→ι⁡(M)r:N_{\iota}\to\iota(M) which coincides with rU′r_{U^{\prime}} near ι⁡(U′′¯)\iota(\overline{U^{\prime\prime}}). Indeed, such an extension is a retraction because it extends the identity map Y→YY\to Y. In particular, it is smooth near ι⁡(O¯)\iota(\overline{O}). End of the proof of Claim B.
Claim C. There is a GG-equivariant continuous map
ψ:M×𝑹→𝑹×𝑾\psi:M\times{\bm{R}}\to{\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}}
satisfying
(1)
ψ\psi is a homeomorphism onto an open subset.
(2)
ψ\psi is a diffeomorphism near O¯×{0}\overline{O}\times\{0\} with respect to the given GG-smoothing near O¯\overline{O}.
Proof of Claim C. Consider the GG-equivariant map
θ:M×𝑹→𝑹,(x,y)↦x+y.\theta:M\times{\bm{R}}\to{\bm{R}},\ (x,y)\mapsto x+y.
There exists a continuous function δ:M→(0,+∞)\delta:M\to(0,+\infty) such that
|y|<δ⁡(x)⟹θ⁡(x,y)∈Nι.|y|<\delta(x)\Longrightarrow\theta(x,y)\in N_{\iota}.
We can choose δ\delta such that it is smooth over UU. Let y↦y′y\mapsto y^{\prime} be a GG-invariant smooth map which maps 𝑹{\bm{R}} diffeomorphically onto the unit ball 𝑹⁡(1){\bm{R}}(1). Then define
ψ:M×𝑹→𝑹×𝑾,(x,y)↦(θ⁡(x,δ⁡(x)​y′),expx−1⁡(r⁡(θ⁡(x,δ⁡(x)​y′)))).\begin{split}\psi:M\times{\bm{R}}\to&\ {\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}},\\
(x,y)\mapsto&\ \Big(\theta(x,\delta(x)y^{\prime}),\exp_{x}^{-1}(r(\theta(x,\delta(x)y^{\prime})))\Big).\end{split}
This map is of the same form of the one used in [Las79, Theorem 1.3]. Hence this map is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of 𝑹×𝑾{\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}}. Moreover, by the explicit construction one can verify that ψ\psi is a smooth embedding near O¯×{0}⊂O¯×𝑹\overline{O}\times\{0\}\subset\overline{O}\times{\bm{R}}. End of the proof of Claim C.
Therefore, ψ\psi pulls back the standard GG-invariant smooth structure on 𝑹×𝑾{\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}} to a smooth structure on M×𝑹M\times{\bm{R}}, giving a stable GG-smoothing on MM. Moroever, as ψ\psi is smooth near O¯×{0}\overline{O}\times\{0\}, this stable GG-smoothing is diffeomorphic to the 𝑹{\bm{R}}-stabilization of the given one. Lastly, one can check easily that the induced stable GG-vector bundle reduction of Tμ​(M×𝑹)T_{\mu}(M\times{\bm{R}}) is in the same isotopy class of the given one.
Now we consider the general case where E→ME\to M is not necessarily trivial. We know that EE has a stable inverse, which is a GG-equivariant vector bundle πF:F→M\pi_{F}:F\to M such that E⊕F≅M×𝑾E\oplus F\cong M\times{\bm{W}} for some orthogonal GG-space 𝑾{\bm{W}}. We may assume that both EE and FF are smooth GG-equivariant vector bundles near O¯\overline{O} and the isomorphism E⊕F≅M×𝑾E\oplus F\cong M\times{\bm{W}} is smooth there. We may assume assume that the GG-vector bundle reduction, which is equivalent to a map
exp:E→M×M\exp:E\to M\times M
is smooth near O¯\overline{O}.
Consider the total space FF. It is a topological GG-manifold hence has a GG-tangent microbundle Tμ​FT_{\mu}F. By [Las79, Lemma 1.6], the GG-vector bundle reduction
exp:E→M×M\exp:E\to M\times M
of Tμ​MT_{\mu}M induces a GG-vector bundle reduction
φ^:F×𝑾≅πF∗​(E⊕F)→F×F\widehat{\varphi}:F\times{\bm{W}}\cong\pi_{F}^{*}(E\oplus F)\to F\times F
of Tμ​FT_{\mu}F. Notice that there is an induced GG-smoothing in a neighborhood of πF−1​(O¯)\pi_{F}^{-1}(\overline{O}) in the total space of FF. The construction of φ^\widehat{\varphi} in [Las79, Lemma 1.6] can be carried out to guarantee that with respect to the smooth structures on EE and FF near O¯\overline{O}, φ^\widehat{\varphi} is smooth near πF−1​(O¯)\pi_{F}^{-1}(\overline{O}). Then by the special case when EE is trivial, one can construct a GG-smoothing on F×𝑾F\times{\bm{W}} for some orthogonal GG-space 𝑹{\bm{R}} which coincides with the induced smooth structure near πF−1​(O¯)×𝑹\pi_{F}^{-1}(\overline{O})\times{\bm{R}}. Then consider
M×𝑾×𝑹=(E⊕F)×𝑹=πF∗​E×𝑹M\times{\bm{W}}\times{\bm{R}}=(E\oplus F)\times{\bm{R}}=\pi_{F}^{*}E\times{\bm{R}}
which is the total space of a GG-equivariant vector bundle over FF. Hence we can choose a smooth bundle structure on this bundle making the total space M×𝑾×𝑹M\times{\bm{W}}\times{\bm{R}} smooth. As EE and FF are already smooth near O¯\overline{O}, we can guarantee that the smooth bundle structure is induced from the original smooth bundle structures on EE and FF near O¯\overline{O}.
∎
Appendix C A proof sketch of Proposition 5.64
C.1. Analytical setup
We provide a systematic setup which generalizes the case of a single stabilization map in Subsection 5.5. For each p​qpq and each integer d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} we describe a topological space 𝒞p​q(d){\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)} which is morally a stratified Banach fiber bundle over Bdp​qB_{d_{pq}}. Indeed, for each x¯∈Bdp​q\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d_{pq}}, its fiber over the universal curve is a prestable cylinder Σx¯\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}} over which there is a stable map μ¯x¯:Σx¯→ℂ​ℙdp​q\underline{\smash{\mu}}_{\underline{\smash{x}}}:\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}} representing x¯\underline{\smash{x}}. Then there are bundles
Ex¯(d)→Σx¯×ME_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}\to\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\times M
whose fibers are similar to the one (5.46) in the single-layered case. Fixing a>2a>2, there is then a space 𝒞1,a​(Σx¯,Ex¯(d)){\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}) of W1,aW^{1,a}-sections (u,η):Σx¯→Ex¯(d)(u,\eta):\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}, where u:Σx¯→Mu:\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to M is a map asymptotic to possible periodic orbits at cylindrical nodes or the two marked points and η\eta is a section of Σx¯(d)|graph​u\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}|_{{\rm graph}u} such the total map (u,η)(u,\eta) is of class W1,aW^{1,a}. Define
𝒞p​q(d)=⨆x¯∈Bdp​q𝒞1,a​(Σx¯,Ex¯(d)).{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}=\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d_{pq}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}).
It is stratified by α∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}. There are natural inclusions
𝒞p​q(d)↪𝒞p​q(d′)​∀d′>d.{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}\ \forall d^{\prime}>d.
There are also inclusions
∂αVp​q⊂∂α𝒞p​q(dp​q)⊂∂α𝒞p​q(d)​∀d≥dp​q.\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\subset\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d_{pq})}\subset\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}\ \forall d\geq d_{pq}.
Notice that there is a Gp​qℂG_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}-action on 𝒞p​q(d){\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}.
Over each 𝒞1,a​(Σx¯,Ex¯(d)){\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}) there is also a Banach space bundle denoted by Yx¯Y_{\underline{\smash{x}}} whose fiber at (u,η)(u,\eta) is the space of LaL^{a}-sections of certain bundles along (u,η)(u,\eta). We omit its detailed definition. Indeed there is a section ℱx¯(d){\mathcal{F}}_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)} of this Banach space bundle whose zero locus in the d=dp​qd=d_{pq} case is the fiber of Vp​qV_{pq} over x¯\underline{\smash{x}}.
One can consider the linearization of the (multi-layered) thickened Floer equation at any x∈∂αVp​qx\in\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}. Indeed, there are sequences of linear operators
Dx(d):Tx​𝒞1,a​(Σx¯,Ex¯(d))→Yx¯|x.D_{x}^{(d)}:T_{x}{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)})\to Y_{\underline{\smash{x}}}|_{x}.
In fact, by the nature of the thickening equation (perturbations with lower dd does not deform the equation for perturbations with higher d′d^{\prime}), the linear operators are always block upper-triangular, i.e., when d<d′d<d^{\prime}, one has
Dx(d′)=[Dx(d)∗0Dx[d,d′]]D_{x}^{(d^{\prime})}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}D_{x}^{(d)}&*\\
0&D_{x}^{[d,d^{\prime}]}\end{array}\right]
where the last block is a linear Cauchy–Riemann operator on some vector bundle.
Our construction guarantees that all these linearizations are surjective. Hence we can consider their bounded right inverses. By the above block upper-triangular form, we can have right inverses which are also block upper-triangular. We always assume this without further clarification in the rest of this appendix.
The construction of a compatible system of stabilization maps (as well as other structures included in Proposition 5.64), as we showed in the case of a single stabilization map, depends on the choices of three kinds of structures. They are 1) continuous families of right inverses, 2) families of approximate solution maps, and 3) families of local Banach manifold charts and local Banach vector bundle trivializations. A crucial point of our proof presented in this appendix is that these three kinds of objects can always be obtained from local to global and can always be interpolated between each other.
We first define these notions under the current setting.
Definition C.1.
An approximate solution map (for level dd) is a germ of continuous maps
Op​q(d)→𝒞p​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}
which satisfies the following conditions.
(1)
It is level preserving, i.e., if d′<dd^{\prime}<d, then it sends Op​q(d′)⊂Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}\subset O_{pq}^{(d)} into 𝒞p​q(d′){\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}.
(2)
It is fiberwise smooth. It means the following. For each x¯∈Bdp​q\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d_{pq}}, the fiber πp​q−1​(x¯)⊂Vp​q\pi_{pq}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}})\subset V_{pq} of the thickened moduli is smooth, the fiberwise restriction of the level-dd obstruction bundle Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)} is smooth, and the Banach manifold 𝒞1,a​(Σx¯,Ox¯(d)){\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},O_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}) is also smooth. Then the fiberwise restriction of the approximate solution map is smooth.
(3)
Over Vα⊂Vp​qV_{\alpha}\subset V_{pq} for any α∈𝔸p​qFloer\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}, the approximate solution map is equal to the product.
Remark C.2.
(1)
An obvious choice of such approximate solution map is just to add sections of the obstruction bundle linearly in the infinite-dimensional space. This obvious option does not deform the underlying map into MM. However in certain stages of our construction we must use more general kinds of approximate solution maps which deforms the underlying map.
(2)
To construct the stabilization map from a product VαV_{\alpha} to ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}, we do not need to use the whole approximate solution map for all dd, but only its restriction to some subbundle of Op​q(dp​q)O_{pq}^{(d_{pq})} restricted to VαV_{\alpha}. However, using the approximate solution map to Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)} for all dd helps one to extend the map we actually need from a lower to a higher stratum.
(3)
Remember that the obstruction bundle also contains the quadratic form part Qp​qQ_{pq}. When constructing the actual approximate solutions to the thickened equation, we also need to change the approximate solution by variables from Qp​qQ_{pq} via the Gp​qℂG_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}-actions.
We can also define the notion of continuous family of bounded right inverses, for all levels, generalizing the case of Subsection 5.5.
We can also define the notion of families of local charts/trivializations. The obvious choices are the one coming from parallel transport along shortest geodesics starting at any x∈Vp​qx\in V_{pq}. We should regard this kind of choices as “continuous” when the domain changes. However, we may also need to use the chart induced from a map x′x^{\prime} near xx. We can also define a notion of continuity.
C.2. The inductive proof of Proposition 5.64
We have the following induction procedure to construct the structures we need.
Step 1. We first consider any minimal thickened moduli space Vp​qV_{pq} which does not have boundary or corners.
(1)
We can choose Gp​qG_{pq}-invariant inner products on Op​q(d)→Vp​qO_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq} inductively on d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} such that the embeddings Op​q(d)→Op​q(d+1)O_{pq}^{(d)}\to O_{pq}^{(d+1)} for all d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} are isometric.
(2)
We can also inductively construct continuous families of vertical right inverses of all levels d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq} inductively.
(3)
We can also construct a collection of approximate solution map. For each d≥dp​qd\geq d_{pq}, we can choose a germ of maps
Op​q(d)→𝒞p​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}
defined by
[Σ,u,μ,η,ζ]↦[Σ,u,μ,η+ζ].[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta,\zeta]\mapsto[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta+\zeta].
The summation is defined because η\eta and ζ\zeta are contained in the same vector bundle.
Step 2. Suppose we have constructed the three kinds of structures up to an energy level dp​qd_{pq}. There are certain compatibility conditions we need to require. For example, they must respect the collars. We will describe the required conditions we we need them. Now we start to consider the structures over the thickened moduli space Vp​qV_{pq} and the stabilizations maps from each VαV_{\alpha} to ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}. In this step we describe how to define these structures over a minimal stratum α=pr1⋯rlq\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q.
(1)
We first consider the metric on Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)} restricted to VαV_{\alpha}. Start with d0=dp​qd_{0}=d_{pq}. We view this stratum as contained in Vp​r1​rl​qV_{pr_{1}r_{l}q}. Denote r1=rr_{1}=r, rl=sr_{l}=s. Then there are the two subbundles
Op​s→p​q(d0),\displaystyle\ O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})},\
Or​q→p​q(d0)\displaystyle\ O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}
on which the expected properties and inner products we have already constructed induce unique inner products. We verify that these two inner products on these two subbundles agree on their intersection. By the canonical decomposition (5.36), one has
Op​s→p​q(d0)∩Or​q→p​q(d0)⊂Op​q;r​s(d0).O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}\cap O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}\subset O_{pq;rs}^{(d_{0})}.
In fact we can show that
Op​s→p​q(d0)∩Or​q→p​q(d0)=Or​s→p​q(d0).O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}\cap O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}=O_{rs\to pq}^{(d_{0})}.
Then over this intersection the existing inner products agree. Moreover Op​s→p​q(d0)+Or​q→p​q(d0)O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})} contains all existing subbundles of Op​q(d0)|VαO_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{V_{\alpha}}. Hence they induce an inner product on the sum Op​s→p​q(d0)+Or​q→p​q(d0)O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})} which agrees with all existing induced ones. Then extend it arbitrarily to Op​q(d0)|VαO_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{V_{\alpha}}.
(2)
Now we obtained an orthogonal complement
Op​q,α⊂Op​q|Vα.O_{pq,\alpha}\subset O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}.
Define
Fp​q,α:=Op​q,α⊕Qp​q,α→Vα.F_{pq,\alpha}:=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}.
At this moment we can define the stabilization map
θp​q,α:StabFp​q,α​(Vα)→∂αVp​q\theta_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
In fact, the existing approximate solution maps can be extended to one on Op​q(d0)O_{pq}^{(d_{0})} in the same fashion as we extend the inner product in the above. Then we can restrict the approximate solution map to Op​q,αO_{pq,\alpha}. Then as we did in (5.47), we can define a map from Fp​q,α=Op​q,α⊕Qp​q,αF_{pq,\alpha}=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha} to ∂α𝒞p​q(d0)\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}. One can also extend the family of right inverse and the existing charts, in the same fashion as we extended the inner products above. Then it produces the way to correct the approximate solutions to exact solutions canonically. Then the corresponding projection map
πp​q,α:∂αVp​q→Vα∼\pi_{pq,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\to V_{\alpha}^{\sim}
is obtained. From the construction, we can see that the projections are compatible with all existing ones when restrict to ∂αVβ\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta} for an intermediate stratum β\beta. The bundle isomorphism
θ^p​q,α:πp​q,α∗​(Op​q(d)|Vα∼)→Op​q(d)\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}:\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\left(O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\right)\to O_{pq}^{(d)}
for all dd can be obtained.
(3)
Now we inductively construct inner products on Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)} over VαV_{\alpha} for all d≥d0d\geq d_{0}. Suppose we have done this for some dd. Then similar to the above case, one can obtain a unique inner product on the sum
Op​s→p​q(d)+Or​q→p​q(d).O_{ps\to pq}^{(d)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d)}.
We can verify that this metric agrees with the one on Op​q(d−1)O_{pq}^{(d-1)} on the intersection Op​q(d−1)∩(Op​s→p​q(d)+Or​q→p​q(d))O_{pq}^{(d-1)}\cap(O_{ps\to pq}^{(d)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d)}). Indeed,
Op​q(d−1)∩(Op​s→p​q(d)+Or​q→p​q(d))=Op​s→p​q(d−1)+Or​q→p​q(d−1).O_{pq}^{(d-1)}\cap(O_{ps\to pq}^{(d)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d)})=O_{ps\to pq}^{(d-1)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d-1)}.
Hence there exists a further extension to Op​q(d)O_{pq}^{(d)} over the set VαV_{\alpha}.
Then using the bundle isomorphisms we can extend the inner products to Op​q(d)|∂αVp​qO_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}. At last, the approximate solution map, the right inverse map, and the local charts can be extended to ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}.
Step 3. Using the collar structure one can extend all we have constructed over ∂αVp​q\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq} to a small neighborhood. Now we would like to carry out the induction for a higher stratum β\beta. Indeed, for the higher stratum one has its own construction which can be irrelevant to what we just did. What we need to do here is to connect the structures over the collar region near ∂αVβ\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}.
In fact, the interpolation between bundle metrics can be easily done. The interpolation between right inverses can also be done using convex combinations (notice that convex combinations of block upper-triangular operators are still block upper-triangular). The more nonlinear interpolation is the one for approximate solution and the one for local charts/trivializations. For approximate solutions, we can first interpolate (arbitrarily) between the underlying maps into MM, then interpolate over the fiber (linear) direction of Ex¯(d)E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}. For local charts/trivializations, as they are induced from parallel transport “centered” at families of maps into MM, we only needs to interpolate the certer maps. Indeed, the maps we want to interpolate between are close enough to each other. We can do the same construction for all stratum of Vp​qV_{pq} inductively.
Lastly, the construction of the stabilizations maps implies that
πp​q,α=πβ​α∘πp​q,β\pi_{pq,\alpha}=\pi_{\beta\alpha}\circ\pi_{pq,\beta}
The bundle isomorphisms θ^p​q,β\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\beta} for all stratum can be obtained as well.
